# korbat sub-project 3e: account sanctions

Date: 2026-10-09. Status: design, written without the user under the instruction of 2026-10-09 ("resume with all of them ... implement a few steps, then run checks ... Stop only when you are unclear on something"), with a lighter process than 3c's: one architect's model with its labs, three drafters, one review round by three reviewers (the ledger and SQL; the person, the law and the words; fit to the code and the tasks: 56 findings, all applied), and one consistency pass. What that round did not find is found when the code is built; the dry run's amendments will be added at the end as 3c's were. It expands the architect's model `korbat-scratch/research-3e/model-3e.md`, which is decided; what the owner or counsel may overturn is flagged in section 2. It builds on the 3c design (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-02-moderation-cases-3c-design.md`, cited as "3c" with its section) and on 3c's code as the scratch clone holds it (`korbat-scratch/repo`, at the time of writing HEAD `4be080f`: Tasks 1 to 6 committed, migrations 0000 to 0016 built, 0017 and 0018 designed in 3c 6.3 and 6.4 and not yet built).

Research: `korbat-scratch/research-3e/` (`inputs.md`, the index of scope items S1 to S37, disagreements P3-1 to P3-25 and gaps E1 to E28; `gap-E-accounts-law.md`, cited as LAW; `gap-E-practice.md`, cited as PRAC; the lab `lab-3e/` on PostgreSQL 18.6, cited as LAB with its letter) and `korbat-scratch/research-3c/` (`gap-G5.md`, `gap-G7.md`, `gap-G10.md`, cited as G5, G7, G10; `threats-3c.md`, threats T and controls C).

## 1. Context

3c gave korbat cases, decisions that are the statement of reasons, and a ledger of measures from which the database derives every restriction. Every one of its measures acts on one post, one image or one profile field. Nothing acts on an account:

- An author who breaks a rule again and again meets one removal after another and nothing else. The main design promised more: "Strikes expire after 90 days; suspension requires 3 active strikes or an R2/R5 case" (line 185), and 3c moved that promise here whole (3c 15, 3c 16).
- A handle that impersonates someone cannot be taken from its holder. 3c's runbook keeps such a case open "for 3e's handle retirement" (3c 13, Task 12).
- An account that posts threats or floods the feed keeps every write path: the one gate on a write, `lockLive`, asks only whether the profile is deleted (`apps/api/src/social/repo.ts:1303`).
- A person who sends report after report that is plainly unfounded costs the moderators' time without limit; 3c forgets who reported 30 days after the release (3c 6.4), so it holds nothing a measure could rest on.
- Explore ranks by a formula that knows no suspended account and gives a restored post back neither its window nor its age (2c design; G10).

korbat's promise is that limits are narrow and public, that every restriction cites a public rule, shows the person exactly what happened and can be appealed to a different human, and that there is no silent reach reduction (main design, Context). An account measure is the heaviest thing korbat does to a person, so this is where that promise is hardest to keep. The law asks the same: a suspension or termination is a restriction that needs a statement of reasons (Digital Services Act Art. 17(1)(d)), the terms must state the policy in advance (Art. 14), and German case law on platform terms requires that the person be heard before an account is suspended, except in narrow cases (LAW E3).

This design delivers, on 3c's ledger and nothing beside it:

1. **Strikes**: a ledger measure that a final decision on a korbat rule carries when the database says one is due; 90 days of counting; its end by reversal.
2. **Handle retirement**: a measure that takes a handle from everyone, gives its holder a placeholder, and can be reversed.
3. **Suspensions**: a measure with a start and an end written by the database, announced in advance with a reply window, or taken at once on a closed list; its state derived into two guarded columns of `profiles`; one predicate that every write of the acting account obeys.
4. **Termination**: a suspension without end that the purge job turns into 1b's take-down once the decision is final for its author.
5. **The restriction of reporting**: after a warning, resting on a moderator's mark "manifestly unfounded" on rule reports; never on a notice of illegality.
6. **Explore formula version 2**: suspended authors and engagers leave the round; a restored post resumes its window and age.
7. Their statements, mails, console and app screens, terms, export and tests.

No measure of 3e is automatic. Each is a row of a decision that a staff key confirmed, the database decides whether the row can stand and what it means, and no job, no trigger and no clock adds a row to the ledger.

## 2. Decisions taken without the user

Each row gives the decision, what was rejected with its cost, what it rests on and its flag. The mechanics are stated once, in sections 6 and 7, and the rows point to them. Flags: **owner** means the owner may overturn it; **counsel** means it rests on a reading of law that counsel must confirm. Every number is a constant of one SQL function, `standing_rules()` (6.2), mirrored in the contracts, so each is one line to change. "Cautious" means: among the choices a source supports, the one that restricts the person least or hears them most.

**For the owner** (each is a choice with a cost, made so that the work could go on):

- Every number without a source is a cautious default: a strike's 90 days; 3 strikes; the rungs of 3, 7 and 30 days and their memory of 365 days; the 30, 30, 7 and 14 days of the measures taken at once; 20 posts in 60 minutes as a flood; 30 days as the age of a basis; and every number of the restriction of reporting (5 marked reports, half of the reports, 3 further marked reports, 30 days, a frame of 90 days). Rows 5, 6, 14, 15, 20.
- The reply window is 168 hours, where 3c 16 said 72 (row 10). 3c's sentence is amended (section 15).
- The database, not the moderator, decides whether a decision gives a strike (row 3); which decisions give one, with no strikes for profile fields and a strike also when the content is already gone (row 4).
- The ladder ends after three suspensions decided in 365 days, whatever their length, and after a suspension one further strike is enough for the next step (row 6).
- What a suspension stops and leaves, bookmarks among the refused writes, that others may still engage with a suspended account's posts, and that a suspended person cannot edit or blank their own display name and bio: their ways to erase are deleting posts and deleting the account (row 9).
- The statement is the announcement, and the six months to appeal run from it, and anew from a `kept` answer to a reply that held the start (row 13).
- Measures taken at once are an admin's only; a flood is an admin's finding with the count of 20 posts in 60 minutes as its floor; korbat publishes an aim of 24 hours, not a promise, for answering a reply to a suspension taken at once (row 14).
- The protective suspension: a seal for good on images that korbat's staff have not assessed supports one suspension of at most 14 days and nothing more, with a statement that is no finding and an appeal that does not wait for the authorities (rows 15, 15a). Row 15 says what the choice costs either way.
- A terminated account is purged only when the decision is final for its author, up to six months later, and a termination at once is open only for a threat posted after a suspension for an earlier one (row 16).
- A correction that withdraws an announced suspension before its start, to make room for a measure taken at once, in place of a measure at once that supersedes it (row 17).
- The restriction of reporting as a whole, and R6's new sentence (row 20). A known limit of it: lifting a warning withdraws no mark, so a warning lifted because its marks were wrong can be given again on the same marks until the decisions that set them are corrected (6.3).
- No narrower restriction than a suspension (row 24).
- No hold on an account's deletion while its images are sealed (row 27).
- 3e is account sanctions only; authorities' orders are sub-project 3f, after 3e and before 3d (row 0).

**For counsel:**

- The reply window: its length, clock hours as its unit, whether the in-app notice alone is delivery, and what an undelivered mail must change (row 10; LAW E3 (d) to (f)). And that `sent` means accepted by korbat's own relay: korbat reads no bounce, so a mail that bounces later at the person's mail provider gets the 168 hours, not the 336.
- Whether an unverified report with an admin's seal for good is a founded suspicion that carries a suspension at once, for how long, GDPR Art. 10 for a measure recorded on a suspected offence, and whether national law forbids telling the suspect, which a suspension's statement does again (rows 15, 15a; LAW E2, open points 1, 3 and 5). LAW E2 answer 4 asks for a check that somebody carries out; 3e has none before 3f's record of the authority's answer.
- Whether refusing a suspended account's rule reports touches anything a signed-in person sends as an Art. 16 notice (row 9; LAW E6, What stays open). And whether a suspended person's erasure of their own bio (GDPR Art. 17) may be left to deleting the account (row 9).
- From when the six months to appeal run for an announced measure: DSA Art. 20(1) counts from the day the person is informed of the decision (row 13).
- Termination: the closed list of grounds, the waiting state, and that nothing is kept to recognise a new account of the person (row 16; LAW E5).
- A handle retired under R4 is kept, as a handle and nothing else, because refusing it needs it; 3c's promise that no copy of R4 content outlives the restriction's finality does not reach it (6.2, 6.7).
- The restriction of reporting: whether it is a partial suspension of the service that owes an Art. 17 statement (3e gives one), whether a voluntary system on the model of Art. 23 may stand beside Art. 16(6), and the GDPR basis of the per-account counts (rows 20, 21; LAW E6, unfinished in (b) and (e)).
- How long ledger rows of strikes, suspensions and warnings are kept: not researched (row 25; LAW section 7, E28).
- No hold on deletion (row 27). Researched after the model was written (`korbat-scratch/research-3e/gap-E-authorities.md`, E22, 2026-10-09): a hold of registration data can rest on GDPR Art. 6(1)(c) and 17(3)(b) only where DSA Art. 18 obliges a report, only for what the notification carries and only until it is sent or declined, and the person must be told within a month; whether a sealed image is such a case is counsel's. 3e builds no hold; 3f, which has the record of reports a hold would read, decides whether to build one.
- From the research, on a row the model does not flag: whether national contract law asks more for a strike, a bare warning, than the DSA does (LAW E1 (b), What stays open).

A threat posted before the person was told, and a first threat that was overruled, never count. | 18 | The lock | A decision that holds `suspend` or `terminate`, and a decision that answers the reply to an announced measure and may leave it in force (a `kept` correction or review, or a review `partly_reversed`), take the subject's profile `FOR UPDATE` as their first lock; the trigger takes it again (free when held) so the writer never runs without it. Every other decision, a lift and an overruling included, stays at 3c's `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, which the trigger also takes again for a `strike`. The predicate reads `clock_timestamp()`, not `now()`. `follow` and `unfollow` take both profile rows `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` in id order, `createPost` its author's row in that mode; each is tried once more when a purge of the other account deadlocks with it (section 5, the lock order). | `FOR UPDATE` for every decision: every like and follow of the subject waits for every decision. `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` for suspensions: 260 to 667 late writes a minute (G5 V3). `now()` in the predicate: a transaction that began before the start and waited is admitted after it. | G5 Answer; 3c 2 row Lock order; LAB B, C. | - |
| 19 | Staff | No 3e measure reaches a staff account: all are measures of final decisions, which `staff_target` refuses. `grant-role` refuses to promote an account that holds a standing suspension, termination or reporting restriction, through `auth.account_standing` read after its row lock (6.2). | Suspending staff like anyone: a suspended moderator keeps the console (T54). | 3c 6.2 step 8; G5 6. | - |
| 20 | The restriction of reporting | Three steps, each a person's. (1) Deciding `no_violation`, the moderator may mark an answered item "manifestly unfounded": only a signed-in person's rule report, never one that alleged illegality. The key signs the marks. A mark stops counting when a re-examination of its item ends in a restriction, or a correction of the decision that set it withdraws it. (2) A warning, `warn_reporting` on an account case: allowed when at least 5 rule reports the account sent in the last 90 days hold a mark that counts and are at least half of its rule reports of that time. No effect. (3) A restriction, `restrict_reporting`, at once (the warning was its notice), at most 30 days, in force only while that warning stands: allowed when a warning at most 90 days old stands and at least 3 reports sent after it hold a mark that counts. It refuses rule reports only. Notices of illegality, reports of child sexual abuse material and re-examination requests stay open. Rulebook version 3 adds to R6 "repeated reports that are plainly unfounded". | The outcome `no_violation` as the record: "manifestly" is a separate judgement. A stored score: 3c forbids standing as a queue key; these counts feed one measure and nothing else. Automatic restriction: Art. 23(3) asks case by case. The cost: signed out, the same person reports on, within the signed-out limits. | LAW E6 (a), (c), (d), provisional; 3c 16; DSA Art. 16(6), 23(2) to (4) as adopted by choice. | owner (every number), counsel |
| 21 | What the counts are | `unfounded_marks` (one row a marked item, with the reporter's account, the report it came in and the time that report was sent) and `report_tallies` (one row an account and day: how many rule reports). Both sides of every threshold count one unit, the report (one request, whatever it names), by the day it was sent: a report counts as marked when at least one of its items holds a mark that counts. So the marked reports are never more than the reports, and one report can never reach a threshold alone. Both tables are emptied 90 days after the report's day by the retention job; an account's rows go with its deletion. The person sees them in the export. | Keeping `reporter_id` on the intake for 90 days: it breaks 3c's promise to forget who reported 30 days after the release. Counting marked items against sent reports, or marks by the day of the decision: one report that names ten posts would meet the warning alone, and "5 of 0" would pass the share. | 3c 6.4 `erase_intake`; LAW E6 (c); ruling 4 of the review round. | counsel (with row 20) |
| 22 | The retired handle's placeholder | `retired_` and 12 hex digits made from the measure's id (6.2): a valid handle, so every reader works, that nobody can register (the prefix is reserved in the handle rule and refused by a trigger to every role but the migrator). The retirement marks `handle_history.retired_at` and, when the profile still holds the handle, swaps it. The holder may pick a new handle without the usual wait (`handle_changed_at` is cleared): at once, or, when the account is suspended, once that suspension ends. Any moderator may retire, in a final decision only, and the retirement is the decision's first action. | A value outside the handle pattern: every route and link that parses a handle needs a special case. No placeholder and a locked account: a suspension by another name. Retiring while deciding: a handle swap is not undone cleanly when the case closes. | PRAC E4 3 to 5; 3c 16; LAB E. | - |
| 23 | What a retirement's reversal gives back | `unretire_handle` clears `retired_at` and `handle_changed_at`. A profile that still holds the placeholder gets the handle back. A holder who has moved on may return to the old handle at once by the ordinary change. It is never given to anyone else. | Always writing the old handle back: it overwrites a handle the person chose since. 3c 16's rule alone: a holder who obeyed gets nothing from winning. | 3c 16; PRAC E4 4. | - |
| 24 | Narrower restrictions | Out of scope. No field lock, no posting-only or images-only restriction. The predicate is one boolean. | In scope: a set of capabilities on every write path, with a statement and an Explore rule for each. The cost of leaving them out: an author can set a reset field again at once (3c's flag stands). | The orchestrator's lean; 3c 16 names none; G10. | owner |
| 25 | How long the rows are kept | Ledger rows (strikes, suspensions, warnings, retirements, their reversals) stay as rows for good, as 3c 6.6 keeps every action: ids, enums, times. What ends is their use: a strike counts 90 days, a suspension counts toward the next rung 365 days, a warning 90 days; no function reads them after that, and the console's standing summary lists nothing older. A reply's text is erased once the reply is answered and its decision is final for the author, like an appeal's statement; an unanswered reply keeps its text, so none is erased before somebody has read it (6.5). A handle retired under R4 stays in `handle_history`, as a handle and nothing else, because refusing it needs it (6.7). | Deleting or nulling ledger rows: the ledger is insert-only and an auditor recomputes state from it. The cost of keeping: one German court ordered counted notes erased once no sanction could rest on them; korbat keeps the row and ends the counting. | 3c 6.6; LAW section 7 (pointers for E28). | counsel |
| 26 | Explore's resume rule | `posts.hidden_at`, `restored_at` and `explore_from` are written by `moderation_apply`, the one writer of the level, in the statement that moves the level (6.4). Every level change happens in a decision's transaction at that decision's time, so the spans are those of the ledger and an auditor can recompute them. It is keyed on the level, so 3f's `disable_by_order` needs no change to it. | G10's separate trigger on `posts.state`: `state` also moves on the author's deletion, and a second writer of moderation columns breaks 3c 6.5's pin. A function that sums spans from the ledger at each round: slow. | G10 Answer 3; 3c 6.5; LAB F. | - |
| 27 | Sealed images and account deletion | No hold. A person's deletion is never refused or delayed, also while their images are sealed or their account is suspended. 3c's rule stands, and the runbook makes it part of the seal for good: whoever has the server runs `staff.js account-facts` before the admin confirms. 3f, which has the record of reports a hold would read, may revisit it. | A hold on the auth row until korbat has reported: a refusal of an erasure request that needs a legal ground nobody has given, and the fact "korbat has reported this seal", which has no record before 3f. The cost of no hold: an author who deletes first takes the address with them when the command was not run. | 3c 2 row Suspected child sexual abuse material; LAW E5 (c). | owner, counsel |

## 3. Research (facts the design depends on)

The labs are in `korbat-scratch/research-3e/lab-3e/` (scripts, generated pgbench files, raw outputs in `raw/` and `b/out-*.txt`), run on 2026-10-09 in one throwaway container, PostgreSQL 18.6, READ COMMITTED. Their schema is the scratch clone's migrations 0000 to 0016, then `10-proto.sql`, a prototype of 6.1 and 6.2 that copies four functions of 0016 and patches 3e's branches in (`make_proto.py`). Every suspension, lift, retirement and strike in them is a real 3c decision made as `korbat_api`, through `decisions_record`, the ledger's triggers, `append_confirmed_audit` and the deferred `decision_holds`. The machine was shared, so absolute times are high; the counts and comparisons are what the runs are for.

**The code that 3e changes** (scratch clone, read 2026-10-09; line numbers are the working tree's and may move).

- The ledger. `action_kind` has seven measures and five reversals (`0015_greedy_zuras.sql:4`). `decision_actions` allows one row a decision, kind and image (`decision_actions_once`, `:106`) and one reversal a measure (`decision_actions_reversed_once`, `:359`). A decision on the law has no rule and one on a rule has one (`decisions_law_no_rule`, `decisions_ground_rule`, `:156, 161`); a review or correction has neither (`decisions_reversal_plain`, `:162`). `measures_in_force` knows a reversal, a closed case for an interim measure and the seal's rule, and no clock (`:679`).
- `decision_actions_check` (`0016_decisions_and_ledger.sql:495`) accepts a row only in the transaction of its own decision (the `xact` and `decided_at` pair, `:513`), fits kinds to decision kinds and case kinds (step 3), names the one measure a reversal lifts (step 6) and refuses a final measure on a staff account (step 8). `decision_holds` (`:759`) compares the signed list of actions with the ledger rows as a set (`:817` to `:827`) and the outcome with the rows. `moderation_apply` is the only writer of a post's level (`:104`).
- Gates on writes. `lockLive` locks the profile rows of a write `FOR KEY SHARE` and refuses a deleted one; it knows no suspension (`apps/api/src/social/repo.ts:1303`). `createPost`, the engagement writes, `follow`, blocks and mutes call it (`:497` to `:687`). `changeHandle` takes its row `FOR UPDATE` (`:1087`); `lockAccounts` has the modes `key share` and `no key update` (`:1113`); a decision takes its subject `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` first (`apps/api/src/moderation/decide.ts:129`).
- Handles. A handle is 3 to 20 of `a-z`, `0-9` and `_` (`packages/contracts/src/auth.ts:74`; `checkHandle`, `apps/api/src/auth/handles.ts:25`), so `retired_` and 12 hex digits is a valid handle of the greatest length. Onboarding reserves the first handle in `handle_history` (`apps/api/src/profiles/store.ts:18`). `changeHandle` reserves the handle it leaves, not the one it takes (`repo.ts:1094`), so **the handle a profile changed to and still holds has no `handle_history` row**. The model assumed one; 6.2's retirement reserves the handle first.
- The statement's mail (ruling O4, read in the working tree). `sendStatement` runs after the decision's commit and again from the dispatch job while the mail is `pending` (`apps/api/src/moderation/decide.ts:54`, `dispatch.ts:418`). It records `sent` when korbat's mail relay accepted the mail, `failed` when the relay refused the address for good, `no_address` when the account is gone; a failure of the relay leaves it `pending` (`statement.ts:109` to `:119`). `author_mail_at` is written only with `sent`, as `now()` of the recording statement (`repo.ts:1457` to `:1461`). A mail still `pending` after `RETENTION.mailGiveUpDays` (5) is set `failed` by one bulk update (`repo.ts:1602` to `:1606`). **A statement's mail is never `folded` and never `suppressed`**: `folded` belongs to receipts, and a stop of the address never holds a statement back (`statement.ts:116`). The model assumed `folded` meant "handed over with another mail"; 6.2 reads `sent` alone as sent. "Sent" is all korbat records: its own relay accepted the mail. The relay is korbat's outbound service, not the person's mail provider, and korbat reads no bounce (`apps/api/src/mail/smtp.ts:55` to `:65`). `no_address` is what `mailAccount` answers for an account that is deleted or was never there (`apps/api/src/auth/flows/notifiers.ts:201` to `:207`): a live account always has an address.

**Measured** (LAB, with the model's letters).

- **A new enum value in the transaction that added it** (LAB A, 14 cases). drizzle runs every pending migration in one transaction. Naming a new value as an enum literal fails with `55P04` in a view, an SQL-language function (string body or `BEGIN ATOMIC`), a partial index, a select, and in a PL/pgSQL function that is called. A PL/pgSQL function that is only created is accepted. Comparing `kind::text` works in a view, an SQL function and a called PL/pgSQL function; a partial index on `kind::text` is refused (not immutable). The whole prototype ran in one transaction on a database at 0016.
- **A suspension at once under `FOR UPDATE`** (LAB B1, 60 s, 8 clients over 15 weighted scripts). 77 suspensions, 701 gated writes by the subject committed, 0 inside a suspension. With the API's lock left at `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, so that the trigger upgrades it behind the case lock: 5 deadlocks against 1 (B2).
- **The purge's order** (LAB B3 to B5). With 3c's purge order and the purge's share raised eight times: 30 deadlocks in 45 s and a decision of 8.0 s (B4). With the purge taking its counterpart profiles first: 0 deadlocks, 69 suspensions, 0 late writes of 523; decision p50 167 ms, p95 528 ms (B5). B5's purged accounts were never a party of its follow, unfollow and quote loops. The review round ran that case on the same database (`review-3e-ledger.md`, experiments X1 and X1b): the purge of an account against an unfollow of it by an account with a lower id, and against a quote of its post by a follower, each ended in `deadlock detected`. Section 5 answers with one retry, and the lab on the real code repeats both (section 12).
- **Deletion against suspension** (LAB B6, 60 accounts, both fired together). 22 decided before the deletion, 38 after it (`no_effect` `deleted`), 0 errors. The prototype's `standing_apply` wrote the columns of a deleted account's row; 6.2's does not.
- **A start by the clock** (LAB C). With the predicate reading `clock_timestamp()`: 25 starts, 1,258 gated writes, 0 admitted at or after a start (C1). With `now()`: 60 admitted after a start, the latest by 244 ms (C2).
- **The reply around the start** (LAB D, 60 accounts). Replies landed from 193 ms before to 303 ms after the start; `on_time` equalled `filed_at < start` in all 60; after an on-time reply no write was refused, after a late one none was admitted at or after the start.
- **The retirement against a concurrent handle change** (LAB E, 60 accounts). 60 retirements committed and no profile was left holding a retired handle; of 30 accounts changing back to the judged handle, 17 were refused by the guard and 13 won the race and got the placeholder.
- **Explore's columns** (LAB F). Over a withhold, a closing decision, a removal with a strike and a correction, `explore_from - created_at` equalled the ledger's hidden spans to the microsecond; the API role's update of the columns was refused. The round's select on 60,000 posts and 300,600 likes: median 2,089 ms for version 1 and 2,035 ms for version 2, which keeps 32 restored posts older than the window.
- **Earlier labs that still hold.** A suspension under `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` let 260 to 667 writes a minute commit after it, and a predicate that read another table in the lock's statement about 900 (G5, key findings and `:413`); the predicate may read only columns of the row its statement locks. A definer bridge reading `auth.users` `FOR SHARE` decides "is this account staff" against a concurrent promotion in both orders (G5 6). Explore's round at 2.7 million likes: medians of 410 ms for version 1 and 376 to 419 ms for version 2 (G10 `:444`).

**Not measured.** 0017 and 0018 are not built, so no review, no `appeals_check` and no `decision_final_at` ran. The prototype holds no ladder or basis check, no `standing_basis_broken_at`, no `kept` answer, no reporting measure and no `auth.account_standing`. The loops are the repository's statements copied into pgbench, not its code. The lab of the enforcement task (Task 3) repeats B5 and C1 on the real code on a quiet machine (ruling O6), and Tasks 1, 4 and 5 test what the prototype lacks (section 12).

**The law and practice** (LAW and PRAC; each with what the design takes from it).

- A strike with no effect of its own is not a restriction under DSA Art. 17(1), but the person must be told with reasons and the way to contest; the statement's sentence on the strike is recital 64's prior warning (LAW E1 (b) 1, 2). A reversed finding must stop counting at once, and a strike's expiry must not close the way to contest its finding (LAW E1 (b) 3, 4).
- No surveyed service lets a strike be reversed apart from its decision, and the appeal windows of the two with the shortest strikes outlast those strikes (PRAC E1 (a) 5, 6).
- On an answer on unassessed images (row 15), the sources carry at most a temporary, protective measure, "with doubt", and never a termination (LAW E2 answers 1, 2, 4). No source requires a suspension to hide the profile or the posts (answer 5).
- The DSA knows no reply window. Under the German Federal Court of Justice's standard the chance to reply must be real, and terms cannot deem a notice received; no court has fixed a length; every statutory benchmark found is in days or weeks (LAW E3 (d), (f)). The statement is owed at the latest at the start and may be given earlier (LAW E3 (e)).
- A termination's grounds are closed and written in the terms; the person keeps sign-in, the export and deletion until the purge; the content is not destroyed before the appeal is answered or its window ends; nothing lets korbat refuse a deletion meanwhile (LAW E5 (a) to (e)).
- "Manifestly unfounded" is a judgement apart from the outcome `no_violation`; a count may open an assessment and cannot be the decision; DSA Art. 16(6) binds, so no restriction may stop the processing of a notice of illegality (LAW E6 (a), (b), (e)).
- A burned or placeholder username is common practice, and one surveyed service lets the holder revert after a successful appeal (PRAC E4 3 to 5).

**Corrections between the research and this design.** G7's 72 hours become 168 (row 10). G7's "interim hold" that hides the profile and posts on an R2 match is not adopted (row 15; `inputs.md` P3-10). G5's single column `suspended_until` becomes two columns with a lower bound (P3-16). G10's trigger on `posts.state` is replaced by `moderation_apply` (row 26; P3-15). 3c 16's `suspensions` tables and `auth.account_suspended` are replaced by the ledger and `auth.account_standing` (rows 1, 19). G7 5.1's flood, "a volume no person types", is an admin's finding here with a count as its floor, and its "published target time" for answering is an aim (row 14). G7 6.3's termination "after S3" is three ladder suspensions of any length (row 6).

**What the review round changed against the model** (`review-3e-rulings.md`; each is stated where it applies). The protective suspension rests on a seal for good and is given once for that seal (rows 15, 15a). A termination at once needs a threat posted after the person was told of the earlier suspension (row 16). The reporting counts have one unit, the report, and a mark can be withdrawn (rows 20, 21). A `kept` answer never starts a suspension before its earliest start, and starts the six months anew (rows 12, 13). `terminations_due()` answers rows, not ids, and the take-down is the moderation module's step (section 5). The predicate lives in `apps/api/src/lib` (section 5).

## 4. Scope

**In (3e).**

- Five migrations (section 6 numbers them): ten values of `action_kind`; seven columns of `decision_actions`; three derived columns of `profiles` and three of `posts` behind column grants; `handle_history.retired_at`; the tables `suspension_replies`, `unfounded_marks` and `report_tallies`; the constants `standing_rules()`; four new views; `standing_apply`, `strike_due` and the other new functions and triggers; one view and twelve functions of 0015 to 0018 replaced.
- The contracts and the rulebook (section 10): `STANDING_RULES`, the ten `MEASURES` entries, the grown params of `case_decided`, the new error codes, `EXPLORE_V2`, rules version 3 and redress version 2.
- The API: the one predicate and every write route that obeys it; the reordered locks of `follow`, `unfollow`, `createPost`, `purgeProfile` and the upload, with one retry after a deadlock for the first three; the decide and review routes taking the new kinds; the reply route; the console's lists; the dispatch job's part; the purge job's take-down of a terminated account; the retention job's two new calls; `grant-role`'s refusal; Explore's round under formula version 2 (sections 5 and 7).
- The console's controls, lists and messages (section 8); the app's banner, decision, reply and Cases screens, the refusal sentence and the report form (section 9); seven mail templates; the statement's new sentences; the draft terms' new sections; the export's new members.
- The tests, the lab on the real code, and the README's runbook (sections 12 and 13).

**Out, by decision.** Narrower restrictions: a field lock, a posting-only or images-only restriction (row 24). A hold on an account's deletion (row 27). Any automatic writer of a measure (section 1). A termination under R2 (row 15). Strikes for profile fields (row 4). Recognising a returning person after a termination (row 16). A slow lane for anyone's reports (row 9). A new step-up action: every new act is a `case_decided` or an `appeals_reviewed` whose signed params grow.

**What sub-project 3f receives** (authorities' orders and recovery; scope in section 16). The contact mailbox, the `notify` service and ntfy; `legal_orders`, `disable_by_order` and its reversal; preservation and productions; the records under DSA Art. 18 and TCO Art. 14(5); `reverse-window`; the off-server copy and the restore steps. 3e leaves it four seams:

1. The enum-literal rule of section 6: `disable_by_order` and its reversal are added to `action_kind` the same way.
2. Explore's resume rule is keyed on a post's level (row 26), so a new measure that hides a post needs no change to it.
3. The predicate is one exported fragment (section 5).
4. `append_confirmed_audit`'s action list and `STAFF_STEP_UP_ACTIONS`, which 3e does not touch, are the constants 3f extends.

3f may also add the hold of row 27 beside its record of reports. A protective measure that is longer than 14 days or repeated on one seal waits for a fact 3e does not have: 3f's record of an authority's answer, or 3d's hash match that a person confirmed (row 15).

**What sub-project 3d receives** (transparency and automation; section 17). The hash match and the automatic request for a protective suspension: 3e builds the measure a person confirms (row 15), never an automatic writer. The Transparency Database export of account statements: 3e's decisions keep 3c's shape, with `days`, `basis` and the times as data. The public pages.

**Later sub-projects.** 2d and 3b import the predicate for their own writes. Sub-project 4 has the reviewed terms with counsel and the first non-draft `TERMS_VERSION`; 3e writes its sections under the draft.

## 5. Architecture

3e adds no module and no service. It adds rows to 3c's ledger, columns that one database function derives from it, and one predicate that the write paths read. The division of 3c 5 stands: the API decides which rows to write; the database decides whether they can stand and what they mean.

**The database does the sanctioning.** A moderator's decision inserts a ledger row of a new kind. `decision_actions_check` decides whether that measure is allowed now (the ladder, the closed list, the counts) and forces its basis and its times; `decision_holds` refuses at commit a decision whose strike is missing or not due; `standing_apply`, the one writer of `profiles.suspended_from`, `suspended_until` and `reports_restricted_until`, derives those columns from the ledger, the reply and the notice mail's outcome. No job writes a measure. Time alone starts and ends a suspension: the predicate compares the two columns with the clock, and nothing is written at either moment.

**What each module gains.**

| Module | Files | What they hold |
|---|---|---|
| `packages/db` | `src/schema/moderation.ts`, `identity.ts`, `content.ts`; the five migrations | Section 6. |
| `packages/contracts`, `packages/rules` | `moderation.ts`, `explore.ts`, `staff.ts`; `rules/src/index.ts`, `statement.ts` | Section 10: `STANDING_RULES`, the `MEASURES` entries, the params, the codes, `EXPLORE_V2`; rulebook version 3, redress version 2, the statement's new sentences. |
| `apps/api/src/lib` | new `standing.ts` | The predicate and nothing else, shared as `lock-bounds.ts` is: no module's index has to export it, so no two modules import each other for it. |
| social | `repo.ts`, `flows/graph.ts`, `flows/posts.ts`, `flows/explore.ts`, `flows/profile-edit.ts`, `index.ts` | `repo.ts`: `lockLive` with the acting account, the reordered `follow`, `unfollow`, `createPost` and `purgeProfile`, `updateProfile` and `changeHandle` with the predicate, `lockAccounts` with the mode `update`, and formula version 2 in `exploreRules` (`repo.ts:424`; section 7). `explore.ts` holds only the job's timer and does not change. The flows of `follow`, `unfollow` and `createPost`: one retry after a deadlock (the lock order, below). |
| media | `repo.ts` | The upload's `insertProcessing` as one statement that locks the owner's row and inserts only when the predicate holds. |
| auth | `handles.ts`, `repo.ts`, `admin.ts`, `context.ts`, `index.ts`, `flows/deletion.ts`, `flows/badge.ts`; with it `apps/api/src/profiles/store.ts` and `apps/api/src/mail/templates.ts` | The reserved prefix `retired_`; `changeRole`'s third answer for `grant-role`; `takeDownTerminated`, the system take-down of a terminated account with its mail; the seven new mail templates. `profiles/store.ts`, which is outside the module and may name `profiles`: `setVerified`'s write of the badge with the predicate, and the read `suspended`. The auth module itself names no public table and never imports the predicate. |
| moderation | new `standing.ts` and `standing-repo.ts`; `decide.ts`, `appeals.ts`, `cases.ts`, `statement.ts`, `dispatch.ts`, `retention.ts`, `author.ts`, `intake.ts`, `export.ts`, `routes.ts`, `index.ts` | `standing.ts`: the reply, the console's lists, the case answer's `strikeDue` and `standing`, `standingFor`, and `takeDownDue`, the step that takes down the accounts due for termination; its SQL in `standing-repo.ts`. `decide.ts` and `appeals.ts`: the new kinds, the lock mode, the marks. `statement.ts` and `dispatch.ts`: the mail's outcome recorded under the profile lock. `retention.ts`: two new calls. `intake.ts`: the two refusals. |
| staff | `cases.ts` | The routes of the console's lists. |
| account | `purge.ts` | One call of `takeDownDue` before the round's purge, in a try of its own. The module still has no pool and names no table. |
| `apps/api/src/app.ts` | | `/api/me` composed from the auth module's `meFor` and the moderation module's `standingFor`. |
| `apps/admin`, `apps/app` | | Sections 8 and 9. |

**Interfaces that change.** Modules still reach each other only through their indexes (3c 5; `apps/api/test/architecture.test.ts:28`).

- **The predicate**, exported by `apps/api/src/lib/standing.ts` as `liveNotSuspended(profile, at)`: an SQL fragment over a profile alias and a time, `<p>.deleted_at is null and not (<p>.suspended_from is not null and <p>.suspended_from <= <t> and <t> < <p>.suspended_until)`. It reads only columns of the profile row. In every write the caller puts it in the statement that locks or updates that row and nowhere else (section 3, G5); Explore's round and its reads apply it to rows they only read (section 7). `social/repo.ts`, `media/repo.ts` and `profiles/store.ts` import it; 2d and 3b will. The auth module cannot use it (it may name no public table and its role holds no grant on `profiles`), so the badge goes through `ProfileStore`: `setVerified(id, at): Promise<boolean>`, false when the predicate refused the write, and `suspended(id): Promise<boolean>`, a plain read that `startBadge` asks first as a courtesy. Section 7 says where each route puts the fragment; the architecture test pins the files that may name it and the three derived columns (section 12).
- **`SocialForModeration.lockAccounts(tx, ids, mode, live)`**: `mode` gains `update`. Each row of its answer gains the account's standing read in the lock's statement: whether it is suspended now and whether its reporting is restricted now, both by `clock_timestamp()`. The decide and review routes ask for `update` exactly when the signed actions hold `suspend` or `terminate`, or the reviewed decision holds an announced measure and the decision answers its reply in `decisions_apply` (6.2): the outcome is `kept`, or it is a review with the outcome `partly_reversed` (whether a reply exists and was on time is read inside, after the lock); the intake reads the reporter's standing from the row it already locks.
- **`AuthForAccount.takeDownTerminated(account, facts)`**, with `facts = { decidedAt, imagesSealed }` (the decision's time, and whether a seal of the account's images is in force): 1b's take-down as the system actor. As `confirmDeletion` does (`apps/api/src/auth/flows/deletion.ts:79` to `:83`), it first reads the account's row with its address, then marks the account deleted and revokes every session in one transaction, without a session or a code, and writes the auth event; only after that commit it sends `termination_final` with the decision's day, and with its sentence on sealed images when `imagesSealed` is true, to the address it read, best effort, where 1b sends `account_deleted`. The take-down does not replace the address (`finishPurge` does, later: `apps/api/src/auth/repo.ts:511`), so the mail needs no earlier moment: it goes once, and only for an account that was taken down. **It does nothing a second time:** an account whose row is already marked deleted (a round whose purge failed after the take-down committed lists it again, since the profile is marked deleted only by the purge) is left as it is, no event is written and no mail is sent, and the function answers `false`; it answers `true` only for the take-down it made. The round's ordinary purge then finds the account among `pendingPurges()`, as it finds any deletion a crash left unfinished.
- **`AuthForModeration`**: `mailAccount`'s closed template union gains six templates: `suspension_announced`, `suspension_started_at_once`, `reply_answered`, `suspension_lifted`, `reporting_warned` and `reporting_restricted`. The seventh new template, `termination_final`, is no decision's mail and no member of that union: it is a flat template of the auth module that `takeDownTerminated` alone sends, and the moderation module never sends it. Nothing else changes: a suspended account signs in as before, and no auth route reads the standing.
- **`ModerationForStaff`** gains the lists and the reply's answer in the case read (section 7). **`ModerationForAccount`** gains the export's new members and `takeDownDue(takeDown: (account: string, facts: { decidedAt: Date; imagesSealed: boolean }) => Promise<void>): Promise<{ terminated: number; failed: number }>`: the step of the lock order's item 5, in the module that has the API pool, `lockAccounts` and the right to name tables. The account module's `purgePending` calls it with `(account, facts) => deps.auth.takeDownTerminated(account, facts)` and adds the two counts to its `PurgeReport`, which gains `terminated`. **`ModerationForSocial.authorFacts`** gains a post's strike for the chip.
- **`/api/me`.** `createModeration`'s result gains `standingFor(userId): Promise<AccountStanding | null>`, the read behind `MeResponse.standing` (not to be mistaken for the SQL function `auth.account_standing`, which `grant-role` asks). `Auth` gains `meFor(session)`, which answers the `MeResponse` without `standing`, beside its finished handler. `createApp` (`apps/api/src/app.ts:73`) composes the route from the two and answers `standing: null` when it was built without the moderation module, as thirteen HTTP test files build it, or the account has no profile.
- **`grant-role`** (`apps/api/src/cli/grant-role.ts`; the auth repository's `changeRole`, `apps/api/src/auth/repo.ts:405`) calls `auth.account_standing` on the auth role, in a statement of its own after its `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` on the `auth.users` row, and changes nothing when it answers true for a promotion. `changeRole` then answers `"standing"`, a third answer beside a change and `undefined` (no live account has the address); `grantRole` (`apps/api/src/auth/admin.ts:38`) passes it on, and the command line prints `grantRole.standing` and exits 1.

**Wiring** (`apps/api/src/index.ts`) does not change in shape: `createModeration`, `createSocial`, `createAccount` and `createStaff` receive what they receive today, and `createApp` already receives the auth and the moderation module. No configuration is added: every number is a constant of `standing_rules()` and `STANDING_RULES`.

### The lock order

3c's order stands (3c 2, row Lock order): profile rows first, sorted by id, each in the strongest mode the transaction will need; then cases; then, inside a decision's triggers, the deciding account's and the subject's `auth.users` rows `FOR SHARE` and the cap's advisory lock; then the case's own post; then image rows in id order; then inserts, the audit row and the deferred checks. 3e adds:

1. **The subject's profile `FOR UPDATE`** in place of `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, as the first lock, in exactly three transactions: a decision whose signed actions hold `suspend` or `terminate`; a decision that answers the reply of an announced measure in `decisions_apply`, which is a correction or review with the outcome `kept` or a review with the outcome `partly_reversed` (it starts the suspension when the reply was on time and the measure is left in force); and the reply itself, which takes the account's own row. The API takes it and the trigger takes it again. Left at `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, the trigger's lock is an upgrade behind the case lock and deadlocks with followers' counter updates (LAB B2), so the decide route's test asserts the mode. Every other decision, a lift and an overruling included, stays at `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`: `standing_apply` then writes a row its transaction already holds in a mode that allows the write. For a `strike` the trigger takes the subject's row in that mode again (free when the API holds it, as it always does), so the strike rule of 6.2 never runs without it.
2. **`follow` and `unfollow`** take the pair lock and then both profile rows `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` in one statement ordered by id, before the insert or the delete. **`createPost`** takes its author's row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` when its counter will change and `FOR KEY SHARE` otherwise, and a replied-to or quoted author's row `FOR KEY SHARE`; those two modes never wait for each other.
3. **`purgeProfile`** takes, right after its own row, the profile rows of every account whose counter it will write (the accounts it follows and its followers) `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` in id order, before any post. With 3c's order a `FOR UPDATE` decision, a third account's reply and a follower's purge form a cycle (LAB B4: 30 deadlocks in 45 s; B5 with this order: none). This closes the residue 3c 2 flags for a decision and a third account. It does not close it for the purged account's own counterparts, because the purge holds its own row before rows with lower ids, and items 2 and 3 disagree for that one pair: an unfollow or a second follow between the purged account and an account with a lower id holds the lower row and waits for the purged one, and a quote of the purged account's post by a follower holds its own row and waits for the quoted author's (the review's experiments X1 and X1b: `deadlock detected` in both; section 3). PostgreSQL detects each. The purge is tried again by its job at the next round (`apps/api/src/account/purge.ts:18`). `follow`, `unfollow` and `createPost` get the one retry on `40P01` that a confirmed transaction has (`isDeadlock`, `apps/api/src/lib/lock-bounds.ts`), in their flows: the second try finds the purge committed and answers as for any deleted account. The lab on the real code runs a loop in which the purged accounts are themselves followed, unfollowed and quoted by lower and higher ids (section 12).
4. **Recording a statement mail's outcome.** For a decision that holds an announced measure, `sendStatement` and the give-up record the outcome in a transaction that takes the subject's profile `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` first and then updates the decision row, whose trigger calls `standing_apply`. The bulk give-up of `repo.ts:1602` leaves such decisions out and gives each up alone in that form. For every other decision the recording stays one statement.
5. **The take-down of a terminated account** is one function of the moderation module, `takeDownDue`, since the account module has no pool and may name no table (`apps/api/test/architecture.test.ts:129`). It reads `terminations_due()` once, unlocked, for the candidates. Then, for each candidate, in a transaction of its own on the API pool and in a try of its own:
   1. the account's profile `FOR UPDATE` (`lockAccounts`);
   2. the case of the `terminate`'s decision `FOR UPDATE`, profile before case as in every decision. An appeal being filed holds that case `FOR KEY SHARE` and takes no profile (3c 6.3 `appeals_check`), so it either commits first and is read below, or waits here and is then refused by its own deadline check;
   3. `terminations_due()` again for that account, in a statement of its own;
   4. only when the account is still there, a read of whether a `seal_media` on a case about the account or about one of its posts is in force (`imagesSealed`), and then the callback `takeDownTerminated(account, { decidedAt, imagesSealed })`, awaited while the transaction is open, so that the profile is held until the take-down has committed on the auth pool.

   The mail `termination_final` is sent by `takeDownTerminated` after its own commit, so never before the second read and never for an account that was not taken down. The transaction's lock waits are bounded. A failure for one account (a wait past its bound, the auth pool down) is caught, logged with the account and counted in `failed`; it never stops the next account, and the account module's round goes on to `pendingPurges()` in every case, also when `takeDownDue` itself throws: a person's own deletion never waits on the moderation module's health. A correction that lifts the termination commits first and takes the account out of the list, or waits at the profile and then finds the account taken down: the auth module has marked it deleted and revoked its sessions, while the profile row is marked deleted only by the round's purge. Such a late correction changes the record only (7.8 step 4). A decision takes its subject's profile before that account's `auth.users` row, and no terminated account is staff, so no transaction that holds the account's `auth.users` row waits for its profile row: the wait across the two pools closes no cycle.
6. `standing_apply` writes only the profile row of the account it is called for. `decision_holds` only reads, so the audit row stays the last statement that writes.

The one retry on `40P01` of 3c 7.5 stays, and `boundLockWaits` bounds every wait of a confirmed transaction by its challenge's life. `follow`, `unfollow` and `createPost` gain the one retry of item 3 and no other change of behaviour.

## 6. Data model (migrations 0019 to 0023)

Five migrations, none changing a file that exists, each run by drizzle's migrator in one transaction with the others pending. Only 0019 changes tables: it is generated from the schema and completed by hand in the same file, as 0015 was. 0020 to 0023 are hand-written (`pnpm --filter @korbat/db generate:custom --name=...`) and change no table, so the generate gate prints "No schema changes" after each task.

**The numbers.** The five are numbered from the first number free after 3c: 0019 to 0023 unless 3c's review task adds a migration, in which case each moves up by one. This section's headings and its shorthand ("0020") use the first numbering; no other section writes a migration's number, and a file is named by its name. Before Task 1, 3c is complete (its twelve tasks), and `--upgrade-from` names 3c's last migration.

| Migration | Name | Task | Holds |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0019 | generated | 1 (the ledger and the strikes) | the schema (6.1) |
| 0020 | `standing_ledger` | 1 | the constants, the views, `standing_apply`, `strike_due`, the replaced ledger functions, the new triggers (6.2) |
| 0021 | `reporting_counts` | 5 (reporting) | the writers of the reporting counts (6.3) |
| 0022 | `explore_resume` | 6 (Explore) | `moderation_apply` and `posts_guard` replaced (6.4) |
| 0023 | `standing_retention` | 7 (the last) | two retention functions (6.5) |

The tasks are seven (section 13): the standing ledger and the strikes are one task, because 0020 makes a strike compulsory at commit and the same commit must bring what signs one (the contracts' params for every new kind, `decideForTest`, the console's strike line, the case answer's `strikeDue`).

**Conventions**, as 3c 6: every function is owned by `korbat_migrate`, has its `search_path` pinned (`public, pg_temp` unless said), names every object schema-qualified and has EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC; a trigger function fires for every role all the same. Times written by the database are `now()`, the transaction's start, unless `clock_timestamp()` is named. A "forced" column is set by a `BEFORE INSERT` trigger whatever the insert supplied. An error carries its code as the message under SQLSTATE P0001 unless a SQLSTATE is named. Under READ COMMITTED a read that must see what committed while a lock waited is a statement of its own after that lock.

**Replacing a function.** `CREATE OR REPLACE` keeps owner and grants; the migration states each replaced function whole, as the built text with the changes this section lists and no other. 0017 and 0018 are not built yet, so where this section replaces one of their functions (`decisions_record`, `decisions_apply`, `review_needs_admin`, `appeals_check`, `decision_final_at`, `forget_reporter`) or relies on one it does not replace (`redact_r4_body` and the retention job's candidate rule for it), it means the function as 3c 6.3 or 6.4 designs it, and the task's first step is: check the built text of 0017, 0018 and any later migration of 3c (ruling O3). 3c's own tests of each replaced function run unchanged and pass (6.6), which is how a change outside the lists is caught.

**The rule for the new enum values** (section 3, LAB A). A value added by `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE` cannot be used as an enum literal before the migrating transaction commits (`55P04`), in anything that is parsed when created or executed by the migration. So in 0019 to 0023:

1. The ten new values are named as enum literals **only inside PL/pgSQL bodies**.
2. Views and SQL-language functions compare `kind::text`.
3. No index predicate, CHECK or default names one.
4. No migration statement executes a function that touches one.

With that rule the five migrations run in one transaction on a database at 0018 and on a fresh one. A source test refuses a new value as a literal outside a PL/pgSQL body in these files (section 12). The first decision after the commit is the first to store a new value.

### 6.1 Migration 0019: the schema (Task 1)

**`action_kind`** gains ten values with `ALTER TYPE public.action_kind ADD VALUE`, in this order:

| Value | Is | Lifts | Case | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `strike` | measure | | post | final |
| `suspend` | measure | | account | final |
| `terminate` | measure | | account | final |
| `retire_handle` | measure | | account | final |
| `warn_reporting` | measure | | account | final |
| `restrict_reporting` | measure | | account | final |
| `overrule` | reversal | `strike` | post | review, correction (outcome `reversed` only) |
| `lift_suspension` | reversal | `suspend`, `terminate` | account | review, correction |
| `unretire_handle` | reversal | `retire_handle` | account | review, correction |
| `lift_reporting` | reversal | `warn_reporting`, `restrict_reporting` | account | review, correction |

None of them is ever interim. In this section a **standing measure** is a `suspend` or a `terminate` row; it is **announced** when its `announced` is true and **taken at once** when it is false.

**`decision_actions`** gains seven nullable columns. The API supplies three, which the key signs; `decision_actions_check` forces the other four and clears all seven on kinds that do not take them (6.2).

| Column | Type | Set by | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `days` | `smallint` | the API | the length of a `suspend` or a `restrict_reporting`; null on a `terminate` |
| `announced` | `boolean` | the API | on a standing measure: announced with the reply window, or taken at once; always false on a `restrict_reporting`, which starts at once |
| `basis` | `text` | forced | what the measure rests on: `strikes`, `threat`, `terrorist`, `flood`, `protective`, `threat_repeat`, `ladder_end` or `reports` |
| `rests_on` | `uuid[]` | forced | the ids of the decisions it rests on; null when it rests on none |
| `starts_at` | `timestamptz` | forced | the decision's time for a measure taken at once; the decision's time plus the reply window for an announced one, which is its earliest start |
| `ends_at` | `timestamptz` | forced | a strike's and a warning's last moment of counting |
| `replaced` | `boolean` | the API | on a `lift_suspension` only: true when a correction withdraws an announced measure that had not started, to make room for another measure (6.2, `decision_holds`); no view and no trigger derives anything from it, and only the statement, the mail and the export read it |

CHECKs, on columns only: `decision_actions_days` (`days between 1 and 30`), `decision_actions_basis` (`basis` is one of the eight), `decision_actions_announced` (`announced is null or basis is not null`).

**`profiles`** gains three columns, all null by default, written only by `standing_apply`:

- `suspended_from timestamptz`, `suspended_until timestamptz` (`infinity` for a termination): the one interval in which the account's writes are refused.
- `reports_restricted_until timestamptz`: the end of the account's reporting restriction.
- CHECK `profiles_suspended`: `(suspended_from is null) = (suspended_until is null) and suspended_from < suspended_until`.
- Grants: `REVOKE UPDATE ON profiles FROM korbat_api`, then `GRANT UPDATE (handle, display_name, bio, avatar_path, verified_at, follower_count, following_count, post_count, deleted_at, handle_changed_at, show_in_explore)`: every column the API writes today. Drizzle's updates name only the columns they set, so they keep working (section 3, G5).
- INSERT stays a table privilege, since an insert names every column. The plain `BEFORE INSERT` trigger `profiles_standing_insert` forces the three columns null for every role, the pattern of 0015's `post_media_insert`.

**`handle_history`** gains `retired_at timestamptz`. `REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE ON handle_history FROM korbat_api`: the API only inserts there (`repo.ts:1094, 1253`; `store.ts:18`). The plain `BEFORE INSERT` trigger `handle_history_insert` forces `retired_at` null for every role, so a retirement is always an update by `decision_actions_apply`.

**`suspension_replies`**: one reply a standing measure. Insert-only for the API (`REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE`), RLS on with the allow-all policy `suspension_replies_api_all`.

| Column | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `action_id` | `uuid` primary key | references `decision_actions`, on delete restrict |
| `account_id` | `uuid not null` | references `profiles`, on delete restrict |
| `statement`, `statement_salt`, `statement_digest` | `text` | optional; the CHECKs of `appeals` under the names `suspension_replies_statement_len` (at most 2000 characters) and `suspension_replies_statement` (`0015_greedy_zuras.sql:61, 62`) |
| `filed_at` | `timestamptz not null` | forced, `clock_timestamp()` |
| `on_time` | `boolean not null` | forced |
| `answered_by` | `uuid` | references `decisions`; forced null; set by the ledger's triggers |
| `answered_at`, `erased_at` | `timestamptz` | forced null |

Index `suspension_replies_pending_idx (filed_at) where answered_by is null`.

**`unfounded_marks`**: one row a marked item. Insert-only for the API, and not before 0021: 0019 revokes INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE from `korbat_api`, and 0021 grants INSERT together with the trigger that checks every mark (6.3). RLS with `unfounded_marks_api_all`. Index `unfounded_marks_account_idx (account_id, reported_at)`.

| Column | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `item_id` | `uuid` primary key | references `intake_items` |
| `decision_id` | `uuid not null` | references `decisions`: the decision that set the mark |
| `account_id` | `uuid not null` | forced: the reporter |
| `intake_id` | `uuid not null` | forced: the report the item came in (references `intakes`); the unit the counts count |
| `reported_at` | `timestamptz not null` | forced: that report's `received_at`; its UTC day is the day the tally counted it |
| `marked_at` | `timestamptz not null` | forced, `now()` |
| `withdrawn_by` | `uuid` | references `decisions`; forced null at insert; set by `decisions_apply` when a correction withdraws the mark (6.3) |
| `withdrawn_at` | `timestamptz` | forced null at insert; set with `withdrawn_by` |

**`report_tallies`**: one row an account and UTC day, counting reports (one request is one report, whatever it names). No API write at all (`REVOKE INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE`), RLS with the read-only policy `report_tallies_api_select`. Columns: `account_id uuid`, `day date`, `reports integer not null`; primary key `(account_id, day)`.

**`posts`** gains `hidden_at`, `restored_at` and `explore_from` (all `timestamptz`, null), with the index `posts_explore_from_idx (explore_from) where explore_from is not null`. They lie outside the API's column grants of 0015, so the API cannot update them; 0022 forces them null at insert and gives them their writer.

### 6.2 Migration 0020: the standing ledger (Task 1)

Three words are used below with one meaning each. A measure **stands** while no ledger row reverses it. Its **basis holds** as `standing_basis_broken_at` says. Its **period** is the interval `suspension_periods` gives it.

#### The constants

`public.standing_rules()`: an IMMUTABLE SQL function that returns one row of named values, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`. The contracts' `STANDING_RULES` holds the same values and a test compares the two (3c 2, row One statement per rule). Every function and view below reads its numbers from it and from nowhere else.

| Name | Value | Used for |
|---|---|---|
| `strike_days` | 90 | how long a strike counts |
| `ladder_strikes` | 3 | the active strikes a ladder measure needs |
| `ladder_days` | `{3,7,30}` | the rungs |
| `ladder_memory_days` | 365 | how long a ladder suspension counts toward the next rung |
| `reply_window_hours` | 168 | the reply window |
| `undelivered_hours` | 336 | the window when korbat could not send the notice mail |
| `threat_days`, `terrorist_days` | 30, 30 | the most days of a suspension at once on those bases |
| `flood_days`, `flood_posts`, `flood_minutes` | 7, 20, 60 | a spam flood: its most days, and what counts as one |
| `protective_days` | 14 | the most days of a protective suspension |
| `basis_age_days` | 30 | the most age of the decision a `threat` or `terrorist` measure rests on, and of the seal for good a `protective` one rests on |
| `report_frame_days` | 90 | the frame of the reporting counts, and a warning's life |
| `report_warn_marks`, `report_warn_share` | 5, 0.5 | the threshold of a warning: marked reports, and their share of the reports (6.3; the names are the model's, the unit is the report) |
| `report_restrict_marks`, `report_restrict_days` | 3, 30 | the threshold of a restriction, in marked reports sent after the warning, and its most days |

#### The views and the basis

Each view is `security_invoker`, with SELECT granted to `korbat_api` only, and compares kinds as text.

- **`measures_in_force`**, replaced with `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW`, same columns: its kind list gains the six new measures; nothing else changes. For them it means "stands" (they are never interim). It does not read the clock (row 2).
  - **The R4 redaction does not read the new kinds.** 3c's retention job calls `redact_r4_body` for a final decision citing R4 "one of whose R4 measures is in force" (3c 7.9), and the function refuses a decision that holds none of `remove`, `remove_media`, `reset_bio` and `reset_display_name` (3c 6.4). 3e adds final decisions citing R4 whose measures in force are none of those: a ladder `suspend` or `terminate` whose newest strike is under R4, and a `retire_handle` under R4. So for the R4 redaction an R4 measure stays one of those four kinds: the retention job's candidate rule and the overview's `erasureOverdue` name the four and never read the view's new kinds. Task 1's first step checks this in the built text of 0018 and of the retention job. A handle retired under R4 is therefore outside that redaction and is kept (6.7).
- **`active_strikes (action_id, account_id, decision_id, created_at, ends_at)`**: the `strike` rows that stand with `ends_at > clock_timestamp()`; `account_id` is the row's `subject_account_id`.
- **`public.standing_basis_broken_at(action uuid) returns timestamptz`**, a STABLE SQL function, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`: null while the basis of that standing measure, or of that `restrict_reporting`, holds, else the `created_at` of the reversal that broke it. It reads the ledger by primary key and by `reverses_action_id`.
  - Basis `strikes` or `ladder_end`: it holds while at least `ladder_strikes` of the decisions in `rests_on` still hold a `strike` that stands. A strike's expiry does not break it. The breaking reversal is the one that left fewer than `ladder_strikes` standing: of the `overrule` rows on those strikes, in the order of `created_at` and `id`, the one at position `cardinality(rests_on) - ladder_strikes + 1`.
  - Basis `threat` or `terrorist`: it holds while the decision in `rests_on` still holds a measure in force. The breaking reversal is the latest reversal of that decision's measures.
  - Basis `threat_repeat`: `rests_on` names two decisions (the one the earlier suspension rests on, and the new one), and it holds while each of them still holds a measure in force. So a first threat that is overruled after the termination ends the termination. The breaking reversal: for each of the two that holds none, the latest reversal of its measures; of those, the earliest.
  - Basis `protective`: it holds while the decision in `rests_on` still holds a `seal_media` in force. The breaking reversal is the `unseal_media` that lifted that seal. So a lifted seal ends the suspension by derivation, as an overruled strike ends a ladder suspension.
  - Basis `reports`, on a `restrict_reporting`: it holds while the decision in `rests_on` still holds a `warn_reporting` that stands. The breaking reversal is the `lift_reporting` of that warning. So a restriction never outlives the warning that was its only notice. A `warn_reporting` has no `rests_on` and always holds.
  - Basis `flood`: it always holds.
- **`suspension_periods (action_id, account_id, kind, decision_id, basis, starts_at, ends_at)`**: when each standing measure runs. `a` is the ledger row, `d` its decision, `r` its row of `suspension_replies`.
  - **Its start** is the first of these that applies:

    | Case | Start |
    |---|---|
    | taken at once | `a.created_at` |
    | announced, with an on-time reply | `greatest(a.starts_at, r.answered_at)`; null while the reply is unanswered |
    | announced, `d.author_mail` is `sent` | `greatest(a.starts_at, d.author_mail_at + reply_window_hours)` |
    | announced, `d.author_mail` is `pending` or `none` | null |
    | announced, any other state of `d.author_mail` | `greatest(a.created_at + undelivered_hours, d.author_mail_at)` |

  - **No start of an announced measure lies before `a.starts_at`**, the earliest start its statement and its mail name: every row above is bounded by it. So an on-time reply answered `kept` before `a.starts_at` starts the suspension at `a.starts_at`, not at the answer, and nobody is suspended sooner than a person who stayed silent for having used the hearing. And no start lies in the past at the moment it is first written: the answer's time and the mail's recorded time are `now()` of the transaction that fixes it.
  - An answered on-time reply fixes the start whatever the mail's state is: the person has read the notice.
  - **Its end**: `infinity` for a `terminate`, else the start plus `days`; cut to the `created_at` of the reversal that lifts the measure; and cut to `standing_basis_broken_at`.
  - A measure with no start, or one lifted or broken at or before its start, has no row.
  - The code records `sent`, `failed` or `no_address` for a statement's mail and nothing else (section 3). `sent` means that korbat's own relay accepted the mail; korbat records no delivery and reads no bounce (row 10). `no_address` is recorded only for an account that is deleted, for which nothing starts (`standing_apply` writes no deleted row). So for a live account the last row of the table means `failed`: the relay refused the address for good, or the mail was given up after five days. A state the code does not write would fail toward the person, with the later start.
  - `d.author_mail_at` in the last row is the time the outcome was recorded (`decisions_mail_forward`, below). The dispatch job gives a mail up after five days, but that is the job's rule and not the database's: when the job was down for longer, the start is the time of the give-up and never a time already past.
  - A late reply does not change the start: the mail's rows apply.
- **`standing_live (account_id, action_id)`**: every standing measure that stands, whose basis holds, and that has no row in `suspension_periods` with `ends_at <= clock_timestamp()`. One that has no start yet is in it; one whose period has ended, or whose basis is broken, is not. `standing_conflict` (below) keeps it at one row an account.

#### `standing_apply`

`public.standing_apply(account uuid) returns void`, PL/pgSQL, SECURITY DEFINER, EXECUTE to nobody: the one writer of the three derived columns of `profiles`. Its caller holds the account's profile row. In this order:

1. One statement reads the account's period from `suspension_periods` whose `ends_at > clock_timestamp()` (the earliest start first; there is at most one) and the latest `starts_at + days` among the account's `restrict_reporting` rows that stand and whose basis holds.
2. One statement updates the profile row, only when it is not deleted and a value differs: `suspended_from` and `suspended_until` become the period's two times or both null, and `reports_restricted_until` that latest end or null.

It never writes a deleted account's row, as 3c's resets write nothing into a row the purge anonymised. Expiry needs no call: the predicate compares the columns with the clock, and a stale ended interval refuses nothing. Who calls it:

| Caller | When |
|---|---|
| `decision_actions_apply` | a row of kind `suspend`, `terminate`, `lift_suspension`, `overrule`, `restrict_reporting` or `lift_reporting`; and any reversal in a review or correction of a decision that a standing measure or a `restrict_reporting` of the subject names in `rests_on` (an `unseal_media` of the seal a protective suspension rests on among them) |
| `decisions_apply` | an answer to an on-time reply: a `kept` correction or review, or a review `partly_reversed` |
| `suspension_replies_apply` | a reply |
| `decisions_mail_standing` | the outcome of an announcing decision's mail |

#### `strike_due`

`public.strike_due(case_id uuid, ground decision_ground, rule text, suspect boolean, leave_out uuid) returns boolean`, PL/pgSQL, STABLE, SECURITY DEFINER, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`. The console asks it before the form is sent, with `leave_out` null; `decision_holds` asks it at commit with its own decision, which both reads below leave out. (The model names the last parameter `except`, a reserved word that cannot name a parameter.) It answers true when all of these hold, read in this order:

1. The case is a post case.
2. The ground is `incompatible`, the rule is not R2, and `suspect` is false.
3. No `strike` that stands belongs to a decision of any case about this post.
4. The post's `created_at` is later than the latest `informed_at` among the decisions that hold a standing strike of the post's author, or the author has none.

A strike that has expired still stands for 3 and 4, so a post gets at most one strike for as long as it is not overruled, and a post written before the person was told of their newest strike never gets one. Two decisions about posts of one author run one after the other, since each holds the author's profile row: the API takes it first, and `decision_actions_check` takes it again for a `strike` (below), so the rule does not rest on the caller alone.

#### The ledger's functions, replaced

Step numbers are those of the comments in `0016_decisions_and_ledger.sql`. Only what changes is written; every other step stays as built.

**`decisions_record`** (as 3c 6.3 designs it). Two changes.

- **Step 2**: a correction may also have the outcome `kept`, in two cases and no other (`invalid_decision` otherwise).
  1. *It answers a reply.* The corrected decision holds a standing measure whose reply is unanswered and whose basis holds. A measure whose basis is broken has no reply left to answer (the reversal that broke the basis answered it, in `decision_actions_apply`), and a `kept` answer to it would state a start that does not exist.
  2. *It withdraws marks.* The corrected decision is a final decision of the same case with the outcome `no_violation` that holds at least one mark that is not withdrawn (6.3). The built text accepts a correction of a restricting decision only; this is the one correction of a decision that restricted nothing.
- **Step 10**: a `kept` correction that answers a reply is mailed, like every correction of a decision the author was told of. One that withdraws marks tells the author nothing (`author_mail` `none`): it concerns the people who reported.

Like every correction, a `kept` one carries a reason and takes the corrected decision's `needs_admin`. `informed_at` stays `decided_at`.

**`decisions_apply`** (as 3c 6.3 designs it). Added for a decision that answers a reply: a review or correction with the outcome `kept`, and a review with the outcome `partly_reversed`, whose reviewed decision holds a standing measure with an unanswered reply. In this order:

1. When the reply was on time: the subject's profile row `FOR UPDATE` (free when the API holds it; section 5).
2. The reply's `answered_by` becomes this decision and its `answered_at` `now()`.
3. When the reply was on time: `standing_apply(subject)`. The suspension starts at the later of this decision's time and the measure's `starts_at`, the earliest start it was announced with (`suspension_periods`).

So a review always answers the reply of the decision it reviews, whatever it decides: the reviewer read the case, and no start stays held by a reply that nobody will answer in an appeal. The trigger runs before the decision's own rows exist. When a `partly_reversed` review goes on to lift the measure, its `lift_suspension` finds the reply answered by this same decision and derives the columns again: a measure lifted at or before its start has no period. A correction with the outcome `partly_reversed` (one that only gives a handle back, say) does not answer the reply: among corrections a lift answers it in `decision_actions_apply`, and a `kept` one here.

Added for a `kept` correction of a `no_violation` decision: one statement sets `withdrawn_by` to this decision and `withdrawn_at` to `now()` on every mark of the corrected decision that is not withdrawn (6.3).

**`decision_actions_check`.**

- **Step 2** also clears `basis`, `rests_on`, `starts_at` and `ends_at`, clears `days` on every kind but `suspend` and `restrict_reporting`, and clears `announced` on every kind but those two and `terminate`. It clears `replaced` on every kind but `lift_suspension`, where a null becomes false. `is_measure` gains the six new measures. `post_id` stays null on all ten kinds.
- **Step 3**, fit, gains:
  - `strike` and `overrule` only on a post case; the other eight kinds only on an account case.
  - The six measures only in a final decision; the four reversals only in a review or correction (the built `ELSE NOT is_measure`).
  - `overrule` only when the decision's outcome is `reversed`.
  - `retire_handle` only as the decision's first row (`invalid_decision` when the decision already holds an action), so that a `reset_display_name` after it copies the placeholder, never the retired handle.
  - A `suspend` names `days` and `announced`; a `terminate` names `announced`; a `restrict_reporting` names `days`, and its `announced` is false.
  - A decision that holds a `seal_media` holds no standing measure, and the other way round (`invalid_decision`, raised for whichever row comes second). A seal's appeal waits for the authorities (3c 9), and a suspension's must never wait with it, so a protective suspension always has a decision, a statement and an appeal of its own (row 15a).
- **Step 6**: the map of what a reversal lifts grows by the four lines of 6.1's table. A decision holds at most one standing measure and at most one reporting measure (the rules below make a second impossible), so each reversal still names exactly one measure of the reviewed decision.
- **Step 8** needs no change: `staff_target` refuses every new measure on a staff account, since none is interim (row 19).
- **New step 9a**, for a standing measure only, in this order. Each refusal is `invalid_decision` with a detail unless a code is named.
  1. The subject's profile row `FOR UPDATE` (free when the API holds it).
  2. `standing_conflict` when `standing_live` has a row for the subject, read in a statement of its own. The termination on the basis `threat_repeat` does not meet this rule through the suspension it counts as the first threat: its second threat is a post made after the person was told of that suspension, a suspended account cannot post, so that suspension has ended and is no longer in `standing_live` (row 17).
  3. The basis, by the first row of this table that fits the decision's own columns; the measure is refused when none fits.

     | The decision | The measure | `basis` | What must hold | `rests_on` | Most days |
     |---|---|---|---|---|---|
     | cites R2 | `suspend` at once | `protective` | a seal for good of the subject's images: a `seal_media` of a final decision whose `subject_account_id` is the subject is in force, was decided at most `basis_age_days` ago, and no `suspend` on the basis `protective`, standing or lifted, names its decision in `rests_on` | the decision of the newest such seal | `protective_days` |
     | cites R2 | any measure the row above does not fit | none: refused, with `ground_not_established` for a `terminate` (row 15) | | | |
     | ground `illegal` with `KEYWORD_TERRORIST_CONTENT` | `suspend` at once | `terrorist` | a final decision on the law with that keyword restricts a post of the subject, still holds a measure in force, and was decided at most `basis_age_days` ago | the newest such decision | `terrorist_days` |
