     | ground `illegal` | any measure the row above does not fit | none: refused | | | |
     | cites R5 | `suspend` at once | `threat` | the same with a final decision under R5 | the newest such decision | `threat_days` |
     | cites R5 | `terminate` at once | `threat_repeat` | a `suspend` of the subject on the basis `threat` stands, its basis holds, and it was decided within `ladder_memory_days`; and a final decision under R5, as in the row above, restricts a post of the subject that was created after the `informed_at` of that suspension's decision | two decisions: the one that suspension rests on, then the newest such R5 decision | none |
     | cites R6 | `suspend` at once | `flood` | at least `flood_posts` posts by the subject, in any state, were created in the last `flood_minutes`. The count is a floor under the admin's finding, which the decision's facts state, and never the finding (row 14) | null | `flood_days` |
     | cites any rule but R2 | `suspend`, announced | `strikes` | the ladder (below) | the decisions of the subject's active strikes, oldest first | the rung |
     | cites any rule but R2 | `terminate`, announced | `ladder_end` | the ladder, and `n` at least the number of rungs | the same | none |

     A row whose "what must hold" fails does not fit. The rows for `protective`, `terrorist`, `threat`, `threat_repeat` and `flood` fit only a measure taken at once. So an announced measure under R5 or R6 reaches only the two ladder rows, whatever R5 decisions exist: a ladder suspension whose newest strike is under R5 gets the basis `strikes`, the rung's days, and ends when its strikes are overruled. A measure taken at once under R5 or R6 that lacks its own basis is refused, an announced measure on the law is refused by the row "ground `illegal`, any measure the row above does not fit", and a decision under R2 or on the law never reaches the ladder.

     **The protective row** (rows 15, 15a). `decisions_record` requires a decision under R2 to rest on a suspect item, so the account's case holds an R2 finding; that alone does not fit. The seal is read from `measures_in_force`: a `seal_media` whose decision is `final` and whose `subject_account_id` is the subject, which covers a seal on a case about the account and one on a case about any of its posts. One protective suspension for one seal for good, also after a lift: a second needs a newer seal for good that none has rested on. The decision never holds the seal itself (step 3). The account's R2 finding freezes every post of the account while it is unanswered (3c's `frozen_for`, its second arm); this decision answers it, and from then on the freeze is the seal's own (the third arm): what the seal lists stays sealed and frozen, and what it does not list is as it was before the finding. The decision opens nothing and asserts nothing about the images.

     **The second threat** (row 16). The earlier suspension must stand with its basis: one that was lifted, or whose R5 decision was overruled, counts for nothing. The post of the new R5 decision must have been created after the person was told of that suspension: two threats from one burst, decided one after the other, never meet the row, as `strike_due` rule 4 never gives a second strike for a post written before the first was told. Both R5 decisions are in `rests_on`, so overruling either ends the termination (`standing_basis_broken_at`).
  4. **The ladder.** `active_strikes` holds at least `ladder_strikes` rows for the subject; at least one of them is newer than the subject's newest measure on the basis `strikes` or `ladder_end` that stands, or there is none; and the decision's `rule_id` is the rule of the decision of the newest of them (row 8). `n` is the number of the subject's `suspend` rows on the basis `strikes` that stand, whose basis holds, and that were decided within `ladder_memory_days`. The rung is `ladder_days[least(n + 1, 3)]`.
  5. The days: a `suspend` has at most the basis's number.
  6. The times: `starts_at` is `now()` for a measure taken at once and `now()` plus `reply_window_hours` for an announced one.
  7. A measure taken at once, and every `terminate`, marks the decision `needs_admin` and refuses a decider who is not an admin (`admin_required`), as step 9 does for a lifted seal.
  8. **Findings only, for the ladder and the protective suspension.** A `suspend` on the basis `strikes` or `protective` and a `terminate` on the basis `ladder_end` are refused (`invalid_decision`, detail `open_reports`) when their decision answered an item of a `user` intake: an item whose `answered_by` is this decision and whose intake's kind is `user`. Such a measure rests on strikes from posts, or on a seal in another decision, and judges nothing a report in this case named; whoever reported the account's display name or bio must not read "access restricted" under a rule they never named (for a protective suspension: under R2, of a person about whom korbat has found nothing) and then follow the account's standing. The reports in the case are decided first, on their own merits, and the measure gets a finding and a decision of its own. 6.3 applies the same rule to `warn_reporting` and `restrict_reporting`. A measure a report can properly lead to (`retire_handle`; a `suspend` on `flood`, `threat` or `terrorist`; a `terminate` on `threat_repeat`) may answer one.
- **For a `strike`**: the subject's profile row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` (free when the API holds it, as it always does), so that two decisions on two posts of one author can never both read "no strike yet" at their commits; then `ends_at := now() + strike_days`. Whether one is due is `decision_holds`'s question, at commit.
- **For `warn_reporting` and `restrict_reporting`**: 6.3.
- **Step 10**, `no_effect`: `deleted` when the account is deleted, for the five account measures; for a `retire_handle` also `already` when the snapshot's handle is retired. A `strike` never has one, also on a deleted post (row 4).

**`decision_actions_apply`.** The built steps stay; `moderation_apply` is not called for the new kinds, which name no post. Added:

- **`retire_handle`** with no `no_effect`. `h` is the handle of the decision's snapshot, in lowercase.
  1. When the subject's profile still holds `h`: insert its row into `handle_history` (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`). `changeHandle` reserves a handle only when it is left (section 3), so a handle the account changed to and still holds has no row yet.
  2. `UPDATE handle_history SET retired_at = now()` for `h` under the subject's profile where it is not retired; `profile_changed` when no row was written.
  3. By compare-and-set, the profile gets the placeholder and `handle_changed_at` null where `lower(handle)` still equals `h`. A profile that has moved on is left alone.
  - The placeholder is `'retired_' || substr(encode(sha256(convert_to(<the measure's id>::text, 'UTF8')), 'hex'), 1, 12)`, the form LAB E ran: a valid handle of 20 characters. Whoever needs it outside the database computes the same from the measure's id.
- **`unretire_handle`**, when the lifted measure had no `no_effect`: clear `retired_at` of that measure's handle; then, on a profile that is not deleted, clear `handle_changed_at` and give the handle back where the profile still holds that measure's placeholder (row 23). A measure that changed nothing is lifted without a write, so it never undoes another measure's retirement.
- **`lift_suspension`**: the lifted measure's unanswered reply, if any, gets this decision as `answered_by` and `now()` as `answered_at`.
- **A reversal that breaks a basis**: when a reversal in a review or correction leaves a standing measure that names the reviewed decision in `rests_on` with its basis broken (`standing_basis_broken_at` is no longer null), that measure's unanswered reply, if any, gets this decision as `answered_by` and `now()` as `answered_at`, as a lift does. The measure the person replied to has ended or will never start, so the reply leaves the queue and the alert, and no `kept` answer to it is possible (`decisions_record`, step 2).
- **The rows `standing_apply`'s table names**: `standing_apply(subject)`, as the last act. The subject of a decision that a standing measure rests on is that measure's own subject, so the row is already held.
- The built last step stands for the new reversals too: a reversal in a review or correction of a final decision marks the items that decision answered (`revision_pending_at`).

**`decision_holds`.**

- (a) Its two kind lists gain the six measures. The signed action entry is `{kind, mediaId}` and, for a row whose `announced` is not null, also `{days, announced}`, compared as today. For `case_decided` two members of the params are left out of the comparison with the decision's columns and checked on their own:
  - `unfounded` (item ids; a missing member counts as `[]`) is compared, as a set, with the rows of `unfounded_marks` whose `decision_id` is this decision. The check is queued again by every mark (`unfounded_marks_recheck`, 6.3), so no mark is added after it has run.
  - `replaced` (a boolean; a missing member counts as false) is compared with the `replaced` column of the decision's `lift_suspension` row (false when the decision holds none), and is accepted as true only on a correction whose one row is a `lift_suspension` of an announced measure that had not started at this decision's time: its start, by the table of `suspension_periods`, was null or later (`invalid_decision` otherwise). It says that the measure is withdrawn to make room for another (row 17). It changes nothing in the ledger's derivations: the lift, the reply's answer and the period are those of any lift; only the statement, the mail and the export read the column (sections 10 and 18).
- (b) gains four rules:
  - A final `restricted` decision holds a `strike` exactly when `strike_due` says so for its case, ground, rule and `suspect`, with itself left out (`strike_mismatch` otherwise).
  - A `restricted` decision holds at least one measure that is not a `strike`.
  - `partly_reversed` leaves in force a measure other than the `strike`, so a strike never outlives everything it was given for.
  - A correction `kept` holds no row, as a review `kept` does.

**`review_needs_admin`** (0017): also true for a decision that holds a `terminate` or a `suspend` taken at once. A `restrict_reporting` is not meant, although its `announced` is false: any moderator may decide it, so any other may review it.

**`public.appeal_informed_at(decision uuid) returns timestamptz`**, new: a STABLE SQL function that compares kinds as text, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`. It is the one statement of the moment from which a decision's six months run (3c 2, row One statement per rule). For a decision that holds a standing measure whose reply was on time and was answered by a correction with the outcome `kept`: the later of the decision's `informed_at` and that correction's `informed_at`. For every other decision: its `informed_at`.

**`appeals_check`** (0017), replaced: its deadline test reads `clock_timestamp() < appeal_deadline(appeal_informed_at(decision))` where 3c 6.3 reads `appeal_deadline(informed_at)`. Nothing else changes.

**`decision_final_at`** (0018), replaced, for a `final` decision with the outcome `restricted`: null while its author's appeal is pending, and also while a standing measure it holds has an unanswered on-time reply; its appeal's `answered_at` once answered; and `appeal_deadline(appeal_informed_at(decision))` when none was filed. Every other decision as 3c 6.4 has it.

So a person whose reply held the start, and who waited for its answer before appealing, has six months from that answer, and the answer's redress text prints that date (section 10's `appealUntil` reads the same function). A decision is never final while a reply holds its start, so nothing that follows finality (3c's erasure of the case's evidence, the purge of a terminated account) runs in a hold and is then overtaken by an appeal. Between the first deadline and the answer no appeal can be filed, and none is needed: nothing is restricted while the reply holds the start. Row 13.

**`item_current_outcome`**: its kind list gains `retire_handle`, `suspend` and `terminate`, compared as text, and never `strike`, `warn_reporting` or `restrict_reporting`. The reporting measures, the ladder's measures and a protective suspension never answer a report (step 9a item 8; 6.3), so the account measures a reporter's item can meet are a retirement, and a suspension or termination taken at once on a threat, on terrorist content or on a flood. For those the function does what 3c says of every decision changed on review or by a correction, and no more: the item reads "reversed" when every listed measure of its answering decision was lifted. It reads reversal rows only. A suspension that starts or ends by the clock, a basis that breaks, a reply and its answer change no item's line, so nothing lets a reporter follow the author's standing; what the line says of an account measure is section 10's (ruling 3). An item's line is the same with and without a strike (T43).

**`append_confirmed_audit`**: unchanged. 3e adds no step-up action, and its action list stays the constant 3f extends.

#### New triggers and functions

**`profiles_handle_guard`**, plain, `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF handle ON profiles`. For every role but the migrator it refuses:

- a handle of the placeholder's form, `^retired_[0-9a-f]{12}$` (`handle_reserved`);
- a handle whose `handle_history` row is retired (`handle_retired`), its former holder included.

Inside `decision_actions_apply` the current role is the migrator, so the retirement itself passes. `changeHandle` and onboarding check the same first, to answer with a code, and `checkHandle` reserves the whole prefix `retired_`; the trigger is what holds under a race (LAB E).

**`suspension_replies_check`**, `BEFORE INSERT ON suspension_replies`, SECURITY DEFINER. In this order:

1. It forces `answered_by`, `answered_at` and `erased_at` null.
2. The measure is a standing measure whose `subject_account_id` is the row's `account_id` (`reply_not_open` otherwise). A reply is open to a measure taken at once as to an announced one (row 14).
3. The account's profile row `FOR UPDATE`.
4. In statements of their own: `reply_not_open` when the profile is deleted, the measure does not stand, its basis is broken, or it has a reply already.
5. The measure's period from `suspension_periods`; then `filed_at := clock_timestamp()`; `reply_not_open` when the period has ended; and `on_time :=` the period has no start yet, or `filed_at` lies before its start. A reply to a measure taken at once is therefore never on time.

Every writer of a start holds the profile row, so between steps 3 and 5 only the clock can bring the start, and the clock is read last (LAB D).

**`suspension_replies_apply`**, `AFTER INSERT`, SECURITY DEFINER: the measure's case gets `state_version + 1`, so a correction drafted before the reply is refused `case_changed`; then `standing_apply(account)`. After an on-time reply the two columns are null before the commit.

**`decisions_mail_forward`**, plain, `BEFORE UPDATE OF author_mail, author_mail_at ON decisions`. For every role but the migrator, on a decision that holds an announced measure:

- from `pending` the state moves only to `sent`, `failed` or `no_address`;
- every move out of `pending` gets `author_mail_at = now()` whatever was supplied: for `sent` the time korbat's relay accepted the mail, for `failed` and `no_address` the time that outcome was recorded, which the last row of the start table reads;
- a state other than `pending` never changes again, nor does its time.

Each refusal is `moderation_column` under SQLSTATE `42501`, as `posts_guard` raises. So the API role can neither un-send a notice nor choose its time: it can make a signed suspension start as signed, or delay it by leaving the mail pending, and never start it before `starts_at`.

**`decisions_mail_standing`**, `AFTER UPDATE OF author_mail ON decisions`, SECURITY DEFINER, when the state changed: `standing_apply` for the subject of the decision's announced measure. The caller holds that profile row (section 5, the lock order, item 4).

**`auth.account_standing(account uuid) returns boolean`**, PL/pgSQL, SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path = public, pg_temp`, EXECUTE to `korbat_auth` only. True when `standing_live` holds the account or its `reports_restricted_until` is later than `clock_timestamp()`. `grant-role` calls it in a statement after its lock on the account's `auth.users` row. A deciding transaction reads that row `FOR SHARE` (`account_is_staff`, in `decisions_record`), so it either waits for the promotion and is refused `staff_target`, or commits first and is read here: in both orders no staff account holds a standing measure (G5 6).

**`public.terminations_due() returns table (account_id uuid, case_id uuid, decided_at timestamptz)`**, PL/pgSQL, STABLE, SECURITY DEFINER, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`. It only reads. It answers one row for each account that is not deleted and holds a `terminate` that:

- stands, and whose basis holds;
- has a period that has started;
- has no unanswered reply, on time or late;
- belongs to a decision that is final for its author: `decision_final_at(decision) <= clock_timestamp()` (above), which is null while an appeal is pending.

The row carries the case of that `terminate`'s decision, which the take-down locks before it reads again (section 5, the lock order, item 5), and the decision's time, which the mail `termination_final` prints. (The model's function answered ids alone.)

So a pending appeal, an unanswered reply or a lift keeps the account out, and the purge follows finality only (row 16). An unanswered on-time reply already keeps it out through the second condition, since its period has no start. The third condition is what holds for a late reply, and a reply to a termination at once is always late: nobody is deleted while the only hearing they were given lies unread. Sections 7, 11 and 12 say "an unanswered reply, on time or late" wherever they name what keeps an account out.

### 6.3 The reporting measures inside 0020, and migration 0021: the counts (Task 5)

0020's ledger functions already hold the two reporting measures and the withdrawal of marks, so no ledger function is replaced twice. Until 0021 exists (Tasks 1 to 4) no route sends the two kinds and no mark can be stored: 0019 gives `korbat_api` no write on `unfounded_marks` at all, and 0021 grants INSERT in the statement after it creates the trigger that checks every mark. Their tests are the reporting task's.

**The unit is the report.** One request is one report, one row of `intakes`, whatever it names: one to ten items (3c 7.1). `report_tallies` counts reports by the UTC day they were received. A mark is a row an item, and it is counted through its report: a report is a **marked report** when at least one of its items holds a mark that counts. Both sides of every threshold are reports, by the day they were sent. So the marked reports are never more than the reports, and one report can never reach a threshold alone (row 21).

A mark **counts** while all of these hold: the UTC day of its `reported_at` lies within `report_frame_days`; it is not withdrawn (`withdrawn_by` is null); and no item that re-examines its item has the outcome `restricted_rule` or `restricted_legal`. The re-examination is a derivation: nothing is written. A withdrawal is written, by a decision: a correction with the outcome `kept` of the decision that set the mark withdraws that decision's marks, all of them together (6.2, `decisions_record` step 2 and `decisions_apply`). That is the way back for a mark that was wrong. A reviewer who lifts a warning because its marks were arguable, or any moderator after them, corrects the decisions that set those marks, and the same warning cannot then be given again on them.

- **`warn_reporting`** (in `decision_actions_check`). The decision cites R6 and answers findings only (`open_reports`, the rule of step 9a item 8). `reporting_record` shows the subject at least `report_warn_marks` marked reports, and they are at least `report_warn_share` of its reports in the frame. No `warn_reporting` of the subject stands with `ends_at` in the future. Forced: `basis = 'reports'`, `ends_at = now() + report_frame_days`. It has no effect and nothing is applied.
- **`restrict_reporting`** (in `decision_actions_check`). The decision cites R6 and answers findings only. A `warn_reporting` of the subject stands with `ends_at` in the future; its decision is `rests_on`. At least `report_restrict_marks` marked reports have a `reported_at` later than that decision's `informed_at`: reports the person sent after korbat told them of the warning, never reports sent before it and decided after. `days` is at most `report_restrict_days`. No `restrict_reporting` of the subject stands, with its basis holding, and `starts_at + days` in the future. Forced: `basis = 'reports'`, `starts_at = now()`. `decision_actions_apply` calls `standing_apply`, which writes `reports_restricted_until`. It is in force only while its warning stands (`standing_basis_broken_at`): a `lift_reporting` of the warning ends the restriction in the same transaction, without a second appeal.
- Neither needs an admin, and neither has a reply: the warning is the notice, and each can be appealed (row 20).

0021 adds the writers of the counts:

- **`unfounded_marks_check`**, `BEFORE INSERT ON unfounded_marks`, SECURITY DEFINER. Each refusal is `invalid_decision` with a detail. In this order:
  1. The decision was inserted by this transaction (the `xact` and `decided_at` pair of `0016_decisions_and_ledger.sql:513`), and is `final` with the outcome `no_violation`.
  2. The item is one that decision answered, is no re-examination, and comes from an intake of kind `user` with a `reporter_id` that does not allege illegality. An intake on the path for child sexual abuse material has no reporter, so it can never be marked.
  3. It forces `account_id` (the intake's `reporter_id`), `intake_id`, `reported_at` (the intake's `received_at`) and `marked_at` (`now()`), and `withdrawn_by` and `withdrawn_at` null.
- **`unfounded_marks_recheck`**, a deferred constraint trigger, `AFTER INSERT ON unfounded_marks DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED`: it calls `decision_holds(NEW.decision_id)`, the pattern of 0016's `decision_actions_recheck`. A "no breach" decision usually holds no ledger row, so without it a transaction could make the decision's check run early (`SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE`) and add marks afterwards that no key signed (the review's experiment X2: committed with two marks against one signed).
- **`report_tallies_count`**, `AFTER INSERT ON intakes`, SECURITY DEFINER: for an intake of kind `user` with a `reporter_id` that does not allege illegality, one upsert adds 1 to the reporter's row of the UTC day of its `received_at`. Nothing else writes a tally, so the API cannot lower or raise one.
- **`reporting_record (account_id, reports, unfounded)`**, a `security_invoker` view, SELECT to `korbat_api`: for each account, `reports` is the sum of its tallies of the days within the frame, and `unfounded` is the number of its marked reports: the distinct `intake_id` among its marks that count. So `unfounded` is never more than `reports`.
- **`forget_reporter`** (0018, as 3c 6.4 designs it) replaced: at an account's deletion it also deletes the account's rows of `unfounded_marks` and `report_tallies`.

The counts feed these two measures and the console's list "Reporting records" (section 8), and nothing else: no queue order, limit or weight reads them (3c 2, row Queue order and weight).

### 6.4 Migration 0022: Explore's columns (Task 6)

- **`moderation_apply`** replaced. The statement that moves `posts.moderation_level` also sets, in the same UPDATE:
  - when the level leaves `visible`: `hidden_at = now()`;
  - when it returns to `visible`: `restored_at = now()` and `explore_from = coalesce(explore_from, created_at) + (now() - hidden_at)`;
  - on a move between `withheld` and `removed`: none of the three.
- It stays the one writer of the level and becomes the one writer of the three columns (6.6).
- **`posts_guard`** replaced: on INSERT by any role but the migrator it forces the three columns null. The API holds no UPDATE grant on them.
- No function reads the clock for a post. `now()` here is the deciding transaction's start, which is the `decided_at` of the decision that moved the level and the `created_at` of its ledger rows. So `explore_from` equals `created_at` plus the hidden spans read off the ledger: from a measure's row to its reversal's row, or to the `decided_at` of the final decision that closed the case of an interim measure (LAB F: equal to the microsecond).
- The rule is keyed on the level alone. A seal changes no level and so no span; a measure that 3f adds to the level's derivation needs no change here.
- A post that was hidden before this migration has no `hidden_at`. Restored, its `explore_from` stays null and it keeps its original age. No backfill is written: nothing is public before 3e.

Section 7 says how the round reads `coalesce(explore_from, created_at)`.

### 6.5 Migration 0023: retention (Task 7)

- **`public.erase_reply_statements() returns integer`**, SECURITY DEFINER, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`. It locks, in `action_id` order, the replies that still hold a text, that are answered (`answered_at` is not null) and whose measure's decision is final for its author (`decision_final_at(decision) <= now()`), blanks their `statement` and `statement_salt`, keeps the digest and sets `erased_at`, as 0018 does for an appeal's statement. It answers how many it erased. An unanswered reply keeps its text however old its decision is: a reply filed after the appeal was answered or after the six months, and one left unanswered past them, is still there for whoever answers it. No reply is erased before somebody has read it.
- **`public.forget_report_counts() returns integer`**, SECURITY DEFINER, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`: deletes the `unfounded_marks` whose `reported_at` lies on a UTC day before the frame and the `report_tallies` rows of days before the frame, so that both sides of a count leave on the same day. It answers how many rows it deleted.
- The retention job calls both in every round (section 7); `RETENTION` and the terms' retention list gain 6.7's three rows (section 10).

### 6.6 What the schema, grants and function tests pin

- **The schema test** (`packages/db/test/schema.test.ts`): 42 tables in total, up from 39; `action_kind`'s 22 values in order; the seven columns of `decision_actions` and its three new CHECKs by name; the three columns of `profiles` and `profiles_suspended`; the three columns of `posts` and their index; the columns, keys and CHECKs of the three new tables. The 1b test's exact column list of `handle_history` gains `retired_at`.
- **The grants test** (as `korbat_api`): on `profiles` exactly the eleven columns of 6.1 are updatable, and an update of `suspended_from`, `suspended_until` or `reports_restricted_until` is refused (`42501`); the same for `posts.hidden_at`, `restored_at` and `explore_from`; no UPDATE, DELETE or TRUNCATE on `handle_history`, `suspension_replies` and `unfounded_marks`; SELECT only on `report_tallies`; EXECUTE on `standing_apply` refused; `auth.account_standing` not executable by `korbat_api`.
- **The function test** (3c 6.5: it reads every definition owned by `korbat_migrate`) gains these pins:
  - only `standing_apply` writes the three derived columns of `profiles`;
  - only `moderation_apply` writes `posts.hidden_at`, `restored_at` and `explore_from`, beside the columns 3c pins;
  - only `decision_actions_apply` writes `handle_history.retired_at` and gives a profile the placeholder;
  - only `decisions_apply` and `decision_actions_apply` write `suspension_replies.answered_by`, and only `erase_reply_statements` its `erased_at`;
  - only `decisions_apply` writes `unfounded_marks.withdrawn_by` and `withdrawn_at`;
  - only `report_tallies_count` inserts or updates `report_tallies`;
  - no function or view that 0020 to 0023 create, and no line they add to a replaced function, holds an interval literal: each of 3e's numbers comes from `standing_rules()`. The pin reads the new objects by name. The replaced `decisions_record` keeps the two interval literals of its caps as built (`0016_decisions_and_ledger.sql:350, 351`).
- **The early check**: a transaction that runs `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE` after its decision and then inserts one more mark is refused at commit (`unfounded_marks_recheck`), as one that adds a measure is (0016's `decision_actions_recheck`).
- **3c's own function tests** of every replaced function run unchanged and pass: that is how a change outside this section's lists is caught.
- **The upgrade test**: `scripts/test-db.mjs --upgrade-from` with 3c's last migration (18 unless 3c's review task adds one) and `--fresh` run the five migrations in one transaction; the source test of the enum-literal rule reads the five files.
- **The constants test** compares `standing_rules()` with the contracts' `STANDING_RULES`, name by name.

### 6.7 Retention

What 3e keeps, and for how long. Every period is a design choice published in the terms (row 25; flagged for counsel).

| Record | Kept | Then | Anchor |
|---|---|---|---|
| Ledger rows of the ten new kinds, with their seven columns | for good, as rows: ids, enums, times | their use ends: a strike counts `strike_days`, a ladder suspension counts toward the next rung `ladder_memory_days`, a warning `report_frame_days`; no function reads them after that, and the console's standing summary lists nothing older | 3c 6.6, last row; row 25 |
| A reply's text | until the reply is answered and its measure's decision is final for its author; an unanswered reply keeps its text | erased; the digest and the row stay | 6.5; as an appeal's statement |
| `unfounded_marks`, `report_tallies` | `report_frame_days` after the day their report was sent; until the account's deletion | deleted | 6.5; 6.3 `forget_reporter`; row 21 |
| `handle_history.retired_at` | while the retirement stands | cleared by its reversal | 6.2 |
| A handle retired under R4 (a doxxed name, say) | as a handle and nothing else, in `handle_history`, while the retirement stands: refusing it needs it. 3c's R4 redaction does not reach it (6.2), and a snapshot keeps `handle` and `author_handle` through an erasure as 3c built it (`0015_greedy_zuras.sql:185`) | released by `unretire_handle`; not erased while retired | 6.2 `measures_in_force`; flagged for counsel (section 2) |
| The derived columns of `profiles` and `posts` | with their row | a purged profile's columns are not read: every reader asks first whether the row is deleted | 6.2, 6.4 |

### Names sections 1 to 6 introduce

The model gives every other name used in sections 1 to 6.

- Migration names: `standing_ledger`, `reporting_counts`, `explore_resume`, `standing_retention`.
- CHECKs: `decision_actions_days`, `decision_actions_announced`, `suspension_replies_statement_len`, `suspension_replies_statement`.
- Trigger: `handle_history_insert`.
- Policies: `suspension_replies_api_all`, `unfounded_marks_api_all`, `report_tallies_api_select`.
- Index: `unfounded_marks_account_idx`.
- The parameter `leave_out` of `strike_due` (the model's `except`).
- The predicate's exported name: `liveNotSuspended`.
- Interface members: `AuthForAccount.takeDownTerminated(account, facts)` with `facts = { decidedAt, imagesSealed }`; `ModerationForAccount.takeDownDue` (in place of the first draft's `terminationsDue`); `standingFor` on `createModeration`'s result (the first draft's `accountStanding`); `Auth.meFor`; `ProfileStore.suspended`; `PurgeReport.terminated`; `changeRole`'s answer `"standing"`.
- Files: `apps/api/src/lib/standing.ts` (the predicate), `apps/api/src/moderation/standing.ts`, `apps/api/src/moderation/standing-repo.ts`.
- The term "standing measure" (a `suspend` or `terminate` row), with "announced" and "taken at once".

Added in the review round:

- Function: `appeal_informed_at`. Trigger: `unfounded_marks_recheck`.
- Columns of `unfounded_marks`: `intake_id`, `reported_at`, `withdrawn_by`, `withdrawn_at`. The term "marked report".
- The rows of `terminations_due()`: `account_id`, `case_id`, `decided_at`.
- The signed params member `replaced` and the column `decision_actions.replaced` that stores it; the refusal detail `open_reports`.
- Section 2's row 15a.

## 7. Flows

Every decision below is 3c's confirmed transaction (D 7.5) and every review 3c's review (D 7.7). Only what differs is written. Nothing in 3e writes a measure without a staff key: a suspension starts and ends by the clock, from columns that the deciding transaction, the reply or the notice mail's outcome wrote.

### 7.1 What changes in the confirmed transaction

`ModerationForStaff.decide` (`apps/api/src/moderation/decide.ts:109`) and the review of D 7.7 change in seven places.

1. **Versions.** The route refuses (`invalid_decision`, 422) a `redressVersion` other than 2 and, for an `interim` or `final` decision, a `rulesVersion` other than 3 (section 10). A correction and a review still carry the rulebook version of the decision they answer, and render its rule in that wording.
2. **The kinds a route accepts.** The decide and review routes hold one constant, the list of the action kinds they accept, and refuse any other with `invalid_decision` before the transaction. The params schema of 10.4 knows every kind from the first task on; the list holds 3c's twelve and the strike's two until a later task adds its own (section 13), so no route records a measure whose flow is not built. The test fixtures that decide below the route pass a flag that lifts the list. When the last task is built the list holds all 22 kinds.
3. **The first lock.** `lockAccounts(tx, [subject], mode)` is still the first statement after `boundLockWaits`. `mode` is `update` when the signed actions hold `suspend` or `terminate`, or when the answered decision holds an announced `suspend` or `terminate` and the outcome is `kept`, or is a review's `partly_reversed` that leaves that measure in force (7.11; the decision is read before the transaction; whether its reply was on time is read inside, after the lock); else `no key update` as today. The reason is section 5's lock order: the trigger takes `FOR UPDATE` in any case, and an upgrade behind the case lock deadlocks (LAB B2).
4. **The marks.** After the `decisions` row and before the actions, one `unfounded_marks` row for each id of the signed `unfounded` list (7.9). Its trigger forces the reporter and the time. A correction that withdraws marks inserts nothing and signs no list: `decisions_apply` writes `withdrawn_by` on every mark of the corrected decision that is not yet withdrawn, all of them together (7.9 step 9).
5. **The actions.** `insertAction` (`repo.ts:373`) also passes `days` and `announced` for `suspend`, `terminate` and `restrict_reporting`, and the signed `replaced` for a `lift_suspension` (7.6 step 12). Every other column of the new ones is forced by `decision_actions_check`.
6. **The commit.** The audit row is still the last statement that writes. `decision_holds` then compares the signed actions, with `days` and `announced` where a row has them, the signed `unfounded` list and `replaced` with what the transaction stored, and refuses a strike that is missing or not due (`strike_mismatch`). It runs again after any later insert into `unfounded_marks` (the deferred trigger `unfounded_marks_recheck`, section 6, as `decision_actions_recheck` runs it for the ledger), so no mark can be added behind a check that was made to run early.
7. **After the commit.** `sendStatement` mails the statement under the template 7.15 names, and records the outcome as 7.14 says.

`DECISION_WAITS` stays 15: the profile lock was already counted, `standing_apply` writes a row the transaction holds, and the new checks only read. The one retry on `40P01` or `55P03` stays. Every code a trigger raises is thrown as its `ModerationError` (section 10).

### 7.2 The one predicate

"Live and not suspended" is one SQL fragment over a profile alias and a time, `liveNotSuspended(p, t)`, held in `apps/api/src/lib/standing.ts` (section 5), where the social module's repository, the media module's repository and `profiles/store.ts` import it without one module importing another. The auth module never names it: it may name no public table, and its part goes through `ProfileStore`. The fragment:

`<p>.deleted_at is null and not (<p>.suspended_from is not null and <p>.suspended_from <= <t> and <t> < <p>.suspended_until)`

Rules for every use:

1. `<t>` is `clock_timestamp()` in every write, never `now()`: a transaction that began before a start and waited for a lock must be judged when it gets the row (LAB C: 60 writes admitted after a start with `now()`, none with the clock). In Explore `<t>` is the round's `at` (7.13).
2. In every write it reads only columns of a profile row that its own statement locks or updates. A subquery or a second statement would read a row another transaction is about to change (G5). Explore's round and its two reads (7.13) apply it to rows they neither lock nor update: they admit nothing, and are judged again at the next round or read.
3. It is applied to the **acting** account. Any other party of the write (the author of a liked post, a followed account) need only be live, as today.
4. The session cache never carries it. A session opened before a suspension is refused at its next write, with no wait for the cache's minute.
5. A refused write answers 403 `account_suspended` with no detail. The app then reads `/api/me`, which carries the standing fresh from the row (7.12).

Where it sits:

| Place (today's code) | Change |
|---|---|
| `lockLive(tx, accounts, code)` (`social/repo.ts:1303`) | Gains a fourth argument, the acting account. Its statement answers for each row `deleted` and `suspended` (the fragment's second half at `clock_timestamp()`); it refuses `account_suspended` when the acting account's row is suspended, and refuses a deleted row with `code` as today. Callers: `like`, `repost`, `bookmark` (acting account: the engager); `block`, `mute` (no acting account: allowed). |
| `createPost` (`repo.ts:486`), `follow` (`:646`) | Their own lock statement of section 5's order, with the fragment on the author's (the follower's) row. Section 5 also says what a follow, an unfollow and a quote do when they meet the purge of the other account, where the two lock orders can cross: PostgreSQL detects the deadlock, and the flows of `follow`, `unfollow` and `createPost` try once more on `40P01` (section 5, the lock order, item 3); the predicate is not part of that. |
| `updateProfile` (`repo.ts:1065`) | The fragment joins the UPDATE's WHERE. When no row changed, the flow reads the row once to answer `account_suspended` or `not_found`; the UPDATE itself decided. |
| `changeHandle` (`repo.ts:1087`) | Its `FOR UPDATE` statement also answers `suspended`; the flow refuses before anything is written. |
| `ProfileStore.setVerified(id, at): Promise<boolean>` (`profiles/store.ts:39`) | With a time (setting the badge) the fragment joins the WHERE and the call answers whether a row changed; with null (removing it) it is unchanged. `verifyBadge` (`auth/flows/badge.ts`) clears the phone hash it wrote a moment before and answers `account_suspended` when the call answers false. `ProfileStore` gains `suspended(id): Promise<boolean>`, a plain read of the same fact: `startBadge` asks it first, as a courtesy only, so that no code is sent to a suspended account. The write decides. |
| `insertProcessing` (`media/repo.ts:120`) | One statement: `with ok as (select id from profiles where id = $1 and <fragment> for key share) insert into post_media ... select ... from ok`. No row inserted answers `account_suspended`. `MediaErrorStatus` gains 403. |
| The notice intake, `recordIn` step 1 (`moderation/intake.ts:202`) | `lockAccounts` already locks the reporter's row; its answer now carries `suspended` and `reportsRestricted`, and 7.9 step 7 reads them. |

`lockAccounts` (`repo.ts:1113`) answers, for each row, `deleted`, `suspended` and `reportsRestricted` (`reports_restricted_until > clock_timestamp()`), all from the locking statement.

### 7.3 Every write route

The table lists every route of the API that writes, by its full path, with what a suspended or terminated account gets. A route that only reads is allowed and not listed. The route-walk test (section 12) is driven from this table, so a route added later fails until it has a row.

| Route | Answer | How, and why |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /api/posts` (post, reply, quote) | refused | `createPost`'s lock; authoring |
| `DELETE /api/posts/:id` | allowed | erasure of one's own content |
| `POST /api/posts/:id/like`, `/repost`, `/bookmark` | refused | `lockLive` with the acting account; a bookmark goes with the other two so that `lockLive` has one rule |
| `DELETE /api/posts/:id/like`, `/repost`, `/bookmark` | allowed | withdrawing |
| `POST /api/profiles/:handle/follow` | refused | `follow`'s lock; engagement |
| `DELETE /api/profiles/:handle/follow` | allowed | withdrawing |
| `POST` and `DELETE /api/profiles/:handle/block`, `/mute` | allowed | self-protection (D 16) |
| `PATCH /api/me/profile` | refused | `updateProfile`; a suspended account renamed "korbat moderation" is the next harassment (T54). The refusal is whole: a patch that only blanks the bio is refused too, and the person's ways to erase are deleting posts and deleting the account (11.2 names the limit, flagged for the owner) |
| `POST /api/me/handle` | refused | `changeHandle`; an account whose handle was retired keeps the placeholder until the suspension ends |
| `POST` and `DELETE /api/me/explore` | allowed | a private switch; a suspended author is no candidate either way |
| `POST /api/media` | refused | `insertProcessing`; authoring |
| `DELETE /api/media/:id` | allowed | erasure |
| `POST /api/auth/badge/start`, `/badge/verify` | refused | `setVerified` writes `profiles.verified_at`, a public mark |
| `DELETE /api/auth/badge` | allowed | withdrawing one's phone hash |
| `POST /api/auth/onboarding/handle` | untouched | an account without a profile holds no measure; the handle rules of 7.10 apply |
| every other `/api/auth/*` route (sign-up, sign-in, sessions, passkeys, password, MFA, e-mail change and revert, reauth, not-me, sign-out) | untouched | 1a's and 1c's; `passkey_suspended` and `staff_key_suspended` keep their meaning |
| `POST /api/account/delete/start`, `/delete/confirm`, `POST /api/account/export` | allowed | GDPR Art. 17 and 20; no hold (section 2) |
| `POST /api/notices`, signed in, ground `rule` | refused when suspended (`account_suspended`) or under a reporting restriction (`reporting_restricted`) | 7.9 step 7 |
| `POST /api/notices`, ground `illegal` or `csam`; any ground signed out | allowed, unchanged | DSA Art. 16(6): a notice of illegality is never restricted; the path for child sexual abuse material stores no sender (`intake.ts:500`) |
| `POST /api/notices/status`, `/contact`, `/reexamine` | allowed | no account acts |
| `POST /api/moderation/decisions/:id/appeal`, `/items/:item/reexamine`, `/report-mail/resume` | allowed | redress is never suspended |
| `POST /api/moderation/decisions/:id/reply` (new) | allowed | the hearing (7.6) |
| `/api/staff/*` | untouched | no staff account can hold a 3e measure (7.16) |

What others do toward a suspended account (follow, like, repost, bookmark, reply, quote, block, mute, report) is unchanged: its row is locked as before and need only be live. Its posts and its profile stay visible, with no label (section 2).

### 7.4 A strike with its decision

1. **The console is told, not asked.** The case read (`GET /api/staff/cases/:case`) carries `strikeDue` on a post case: `due`, the answer of `strike_due` for a final restriction of this case on a korbat rule other than R2 that rests on no suspect item, and `why` (`due`, `already_struck`, `before_last_strike`), which the API reads for the console's words only. The database's answer at commit decides.
2. **The decision.** A final `restricted` decision on a post case, ground `incompatible`, rule not R2, not `suspect`. Its signed actions hold the post's or image's measure and, exactly when `strikeDue.due` is true, `{ kind: "strike", mediaId: null }`. The console adds it; the moderator has no choice, so two equal breaches are treated alike.
3. **The database.** `decision_actions_check` accepts `strike` only on a post case and only in a final decision (`invalid_decision` otherwise), takes the subject's profile row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` (free: the route holds it; taken again so that two decisions on posts of one author run one after the other whoever the caller is), and forces `ends_at` to the decision's time plus `strike_days`. A strike never has `no_effect`: it counts also when the post was already removed or deleted, so deleting before the decision is no way out. At commit `decision_holds` asks `strike_due` again, this decision left out, and refuses a missing or an undue strike with `strike_mismatch` (409). The console then reads the case again.
4. **Never a strike:** a decision on the law alone, an interim decision, a decision on an account case, a decision under R2 or resting on a suspect item, a review, a correction.
5. **The statement** carries the sentence `strike` (that this is one, until when it counts, how many were active at the decision) and the redress text `strikeNext` (section 18). The second is the prior warning of DSA recital 64, and it names both steps: at three active strikes a moderator may suspend the account for a limited time, after hearing it, and after three suspensions in 365 days an admin may terminate it.
6. **What reads it.** `active_strikes` and the ladder, nothing else. No queue key, no weight, no limit. A notifier's or reporter's line never mentions it: `item_current_outcome` never reads a strike, so an item's line is the same with and without one (T43).
7. **Its end.** After 90 days it stops counting, by the clock, with nothing written. Before that only a review or correction whose outcome is `reversed` may hold `overrule`: a strike falls whole, with its finding. `partly_reversed` must leave in force a measure other than the strike, so no strike outlives everything it was given for. The row stays; the screens say "overruled".
8. **What an overruling touches.** When a standing suspension or termination names the overruled decision in `rests_on`, `decision_actions_apply` calls `standing_apply` for the account in the same transaction (7.6 step 14).

### 7.5 The ladder reaching its number

1. `GET /api/staff/standing` (behind `requireStaff`) answers `StandingLists`. Its first list holds every account with at least `ladder_strikes` active strikes, one of them newer than its newest standing measure of basis `strikes` or `ladder_end`, and no row in `standing_live`. No system item is written and no case is opened by a job: a second case touched inside a post decision would break the lock order, and D has no system intake.
2. A moderator opens an own finding on the account: 3c's `POST /api/staff/findings` with `{ target: { kind: "account", handle }, ground: "rule", ruleId, category }`, `ruleId` the rule of the account's newest active strike. It opens the account's case, under the daily findings limit, and that moderator can then not review an appeal in the case (D 7.7).
   - **The measure gets a case of its own.** A decision that holds a `suspend` on the basis `strikes` or `protective`, a `terminate` on `ladder_end`, a `warn_reporting` or a `restrict_reporting` answers findings only: `decision_actions_check` refuses it (`invalid_decision`, with the detail `open_reports`) while its case holds an unanswered item of a `user` intake up to the decision's `through_seq`, an item this decision would answer (6.2, step 9a item 8, checks it so). When the account's case is open and holds a report, the moderator decides the reports first, which closes that case, and opens the finding afterwards; a report that joins the finding's case before the decision is met the same way. The console reads this from the case read (`standing.openReports`, 10.3) and says "Decide the reports in this case first; the measure gets a case of its own." (section 8).
   - So none of these five measures ever answers a report, and no reporter's line can follow the account's standing through them (7.11 item 7). The protective suspension is in the list although a report may lead to it: its decision cites R2, and a reporter's line under R2 about the account would be the finding that its own statement refuses to make (7.7 step 8).
3. The case read carries `standing` (section 10): the active strikes with their decisions, the most days the database would accept, whether a termination may be announced, and whether a measure already stands and has not ended (`live`).
4. The moderator decides: 7.6, or a final decision without a measure, which closes the case. The account stays in the list while the condition of step 1 holds.

### 7.6 An announced suspension

**Announcing.**

1. A final `restricted` decision on the account case, ground `incompatible`, `ruleId` the rule of the newest strike, with the action `{ kind: "suspend", mediaId: null, days, announced: true }`. Other account measures may stand beside it; a `retire_handle` comes first (7.10).
2. The first lock is the subject's profile `FOR UPDATE` (7.1).
3. `decision_actions_check` runs its step for `suspend` and `terminate` in this order:

| Check | Refusal |
|---|---|
| The profile row `FOR UPDATE` again (free: the transaction holds it) | |
| No row of `standing_live` for the subject: one standing measure at a time | `standing_conflict` (409) |
| The basis `strikes`: at least `ladder_strikes` rows of `active_strikes`, one newer than the subject's newest standing `strikes` or `ladder_end` measure; the decision's `rule_id` is the rule of the newest of them. `rests_on` becomes their decisions. An announced `suspend` reaches only this row, whatever rule it cites: `threat`, `terrorist`, `flood` and `protective` fit a measure taken at once and nothing else, so three strikes whose newest is a week-old R5 strike give the basis `strikes` and the rung's days; an announced measure on the law is refused | `invalid_decision` (422) |
| The case holds no unanswered item of a `user` intake up to the decision's `through_seq` (7.5 step 2) | `invalid_decision` (`open_reports`) |
| `days` at most the rung: `ladder_days[least(n + 1, 3)]`, n the standing `strikes` suspensions whose basis holds and that were decided within `ladder_memory_days` (3, then 7, then 30, and 30 for any later one); at least 1 | `invalid_decision` |
| `starts_at` becomes the decision's time plus `reply_window_hours`: the earliest start | |
| 3c's rules stand: no final measure on a staff account; a final decision citing R5 is an admin's | `staff_target`, `admin_required` (403) |

4. The case closes. The measure stands (`standing_live`), has no start (`suspension_periods` has no row) and the three profile columns stay null. **Nothing is restricted.**

**The notice and the start.**

5. After the commit `sendStatement` mails the statement as `suspension_announced` (7.15). The statement is complete at this moment: its ground, facts, the strikes it rests on, the days, the earliest start, the reply and its effect, redress, the Explore effect. The ground is the terms' section "Suspension and termination" (an account with three active strikes may be suspended), named as the contractual ground of DSA Art. 17(3)(e); the rule the decision cites is named as the rule of the newest of those strikes, never as a breach this decision found (10.6). `informed_at` stays `decided_at`, so the six months of the appeal run from the announcement; when an on-time reply is answered `kept` they run from that answer (step 11).
6. The mail's outcome is recorded under the profile lock (7.14). `decisions_mail_standing` then calls `standing_apply`, which writes `suspended_from` and `suspended_until`:

| `author_mail` | The start |
|---|---|
| `pending` (the relay could not be reached yet) | none yet |
| `sent` | `greatest(starts_at, author_mail_at + reply_window_hours)`: 168 hours after korbat's own relay accepted the notice mail, never earlier than 168 hours after the decision |
| `failed` (the relay refused the address for good, or the mail was given up after five days) | `greatest(the decision's time + undelivered_hours, author_mail_at)`: 336 hours after the decision. The app alone carried the notice, so the person gets twice the time. The second term counts only when the give-up was recorded later than that (the dispatch job was down for days): no start is in the past at the moment it is written |
| `no_address` | the same derivation, and nothing starts: `mailAccount` answers `no_address` only for an account that is deleted (`auth/flows/notifiers.ts:201`), the predicate already refuses a deleted account and `standing_apply` never writes its row |

   `author_mail_at` is the database's time at which the mail's outcome was recorded. For `sent` that is the moment korbat's own relay accepted the mail (`repo.ts:1460`); for a decision that holds an announced measure `decisions_mail_forward` stamps it on every move out of `pending`, `failed` included. A decision's `author_mail` is never `folded` or `suppressed` in the code (`statement.ts:116`): the view names both for completeness only. `decisions_mail_forward` refuses any later change of the two columns, so the API role can neither un-send a notice nor move its time.

   **This is all korbat records.** `sent` means that korbat's relay accepted the mail; korbat reads no bounce, so a mail that the person's provider rejects afterwards still starts the 168 hours (11.2; flagged for counsel in section 2). The statement, the mail and the terms therefore say "sent", never "handed to your mail provider" or "delivered", and say that the notice is always shown in the app (section 18).
7. The end is the start plus `days`. Both are shown as a date and time on the decision's screen and in the banner from the moment they are fixed (7.12).
8. **No reply.** From `suspended_from` the predicate refuses the account's writes; nothing is written at that moment, and nobody acts. From `suspended_until` it stops refusing; nothing is written.

**The reply.**

9. `POST /api/moderation/decisions/:id/reply { statement? }`, behind `requireSession`; 404 unless the viewer is the decision's subject, as for the decision's read (D 7.6). The text is optional, at most 2000 characters under the text rules; the server makes its salt and digest as for an appeal (D 7.7). One transaction:
   1. `lockAccounts(tx, [viewer], "update")`: the account's own profile row `FOR UPDATE`, the first lock.
   2. The insert into `suspension_replies` for the decision's `suspend` or `terminate` row.
   3. `suspension_replies_check` refuses `reply_not_open` (422) unless that row is a `suspend` or `terminate` of this account that stands and whose basis holds; takes the profile again; then, in statements of their own, reads the period, sets `filed_at := clock_timestamp()`, refuses `reply_not_open` when the period has ended, and sets `on_time := no start yet or filed_at < start`.
   4. `suspension_replies_apply` raises the case's `state_version` (a correction drafted before the reply is then refused `case_changed`) and calls `standing_apply`: after an on-time reply the columns are null again.
   5. A second reply meets the primary key and is answered `reply_not_open`.
   6. 201 with `ReplyResponse { filedAt, onTime }`.
10. An on-time reply holds the start, without limit, until a decision answers it. A late reply (the suspension runs) changes nothing by itself. Either appears in the console's queue (7.12), and one unanswered for 24 hours joins the queue-age alert (7.14). No mail goes for the reply itself.

**The answer.**

11. One decision answers a reply: a correction of the announcing decision, with a reason the person is shown.

| Answer | Signed | After an on-time reply | After a late reply |
|---|---|---|---|
| Keep | correction, outcome `kept`, no action | the suspension starts at the later of this decision's time and the earliest start it was announced with (`greatest(starts_at, answered_at)`), and runs its days | nothing changes: a suspension that runs goes on to its end, one that has ended had ended as decided |
| Reverse | correction, outcome `reversed`, `lift_suspension` | it never starts | it ends at this decision's time; one that had ended no longer counts as a rung |

   - **Never earlier for having replied.** An answer given before `starts_at` starts nothing before `starts_at`: a person who used the hearing is never suspended earlier than the notice said, nor earlier than one who stayed silent. The statement's `repliedTo` carries the start and the end this answer fixes (10.2), and the console shows them before the key is asked for.
   - `kept` is a correction's outcome only here and in 7.9 step 9 (a decision's marks withdrawn). For a decision that holds a `suspend` or `terminate`, `decisions_record` refuses it (`invalid_decision`) unless that measure has an unanswered reply, and refuses it when the measure's basis is broken: such a reply is already answered (step 14), and a `kept` answer would state a start that does not exist.
   - The first lock of a `kept` answer is the profile `FOR UPDATE`: it may start a suspension. `decisions_apply` sets the reply's `answered_by` and `answered_at` and calls `standing_apply`.
   - A reply is answered by a `kept` decision, by the decision that lifts its measure, or by the decision that breaks its measure's basis (step 14). A `partly_reversed` correction that lifts another measure of the same decision leaves the reply unanswered and the start held. A `partly_reversed` review that leaves the measure in force answers the reply as a `kept` review does: the reviewer read the case, and no start stays held by a reply that nobody will answer after the appeal (7.11 item 4).
   - The same moderator may answer: the reply is the hearing, not the appeal. A decision that needed an admin is corrected by an admin (D 7.7).
   - A review of the person's appeal answers a pending reply the same way (7.11).
   - The answer is mailed as `reply_answered`. The appeal stays that of the announcing decision. **Its six months:** when a `kept` answer to an on-time reply starts the measure, `appeals_check`, `decision_final_at` and every `appealUntil` read `appeal_deadline` of the later of the two decisions' `informed_at`, the announcing decision's and the answer's (one function states that moment, `appeal_informed_at`, 6.2). So a start that a reply held for months never arrives with the appeal already closed, and a termination that a `kept` answer starts after the first six months is not final at the next purge round. The answer's statement prints that date (`repliedTo.appealUntil`). In every other case the six months run from the announcing decision, as D has them.

**Ending early.**

12. **Lifting**, at any time: a correction, or a review, that holds `lift_suspension`. It runs at 3c's `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`; the measure's period ends at the lifting decision's time and `standing_apply` clears both columns. That the suspension ran, and when it ended, is read from `suspension_periods`, never from the columns. The lifting statement says which of three things was true at its time (`periodThen`, 10.2): the suspension had not started and never will; it was running and ends with this decision; or it had already ended, and then what the lift changes is that it no longer counts toward a later suspension.
   - **Making room for a measure at once.** `standing_conflict` refuses a suspension at once while an announced measure stands and has not ended, one whose start a reply holds included. An admin who must act at once (a threat or a flood by the account whose reply holds the start) lifts the announced measure by a correction that holds `lift_suspension` and the signed member `replaced: true` (10.4). The database accepts `replaced` only when the lifted measure is announced and has not started (`invalid_decision` otherwise).
   - Such a correction closes the measure's reply (`answered_by`), and its statement says, in place of a reversal's sentences, that korbat withdrew the suspension it announced because it is taking another measure on the account, and that this is not a decision on the reply. It is mailed as `suspension_lifted` with that sentence as its lead, never as `reply_answered` (7.15). The console offers `replaced` only from the conflict's message (section 8). The withdrawn measure is lifted, so it is no rung. Flagged for the owner (section 2), who may prefer that a measure taken at once supersedes an announced one that has not started.
13. **Strikes that expire** do not end it: a strike "stands" while no row reverses it, whatever the clock says.
14. **Overruling a strike it rests on.** When fewer than `ladder_strikes` of the decisions in `rests_on` still hold a standing strike, the basis is broken: the suspension ends in the overruling transaction, with no second decision and no wait for a moderator. `decision_actions_apply` gives the measure's unanswered reply that decision as `answered_by`, as a lift does, so the reply leaves the queue and the alert. That decision's statement names the measure in `endedStanding` with its `periodThen`, and carries the form of `basisEnded` that fits: it will not start; it ended with this decision; or it had run from its start to its end and no longer counts toward a later suspension. It goes out as `suspension_lifted` (7.15).
15. A deletion of the account goes its 1b way at any moment (section 2: no hold). The measure stays in the ledger and does nothing to a deleted account.

### 7.7 An immediate suspension

1. Only an admin, only on an account case, and only on one of four grounds that the database checks. The admin opens a finding on the account under R5 or R6 (7.5 step 2; a finding for a measure at once may join the account's open case), or under R2 for a protective suspension (step 8), or works an account case that holds a notice of terrorist content, and confirms `{ kind: "suspend", mediaId: null, days, announced: false }` in a final `restricted` decision.
2. `decision_actions_check` finds the basis from the decision's own columns, by the first row of section 6's table that fits. These four fit a measure taken at once and no announced one (7.6 step 3), and a decision that fits none is refused:

| Basis | The decision | What the database requires | Most days |
|---|---|---|---|
| `threat` | cites R5 | a final decision under R5 that restricts a post of the subject, still holds a measure in force and is at most `basis_age_days` (30) old; it becomes `rests_on` | 30 |
| `terrorist` | ground `illegal` with `KEYWORD_TERRORIST_CONTENT` | the same, for a decision on the law with that keyword | 30 |
| `flood` | cites R6 | at least `flood_posts` (20) posts by the subject created within `flood_minutes` (60). The count is a condition and never the finding: the admin finds that the account is flooding korbat with repetitive, unsolicited posts, which is what R6 forbids, and says in the facts what made them so | 7 |
| `protective` | cites R2 and holds no `seal_media` itself | a seal for good (a `seal_media` of a final decision) that is in force, on a case about the subject or about one of its posts, decided at most `basis_age_days` (30) before, and that no protective suspension of the subject has rested on; its decision becomes `rests_on` | 14 |

3. Refusals: `standing_conflict` as in 7.6 (an announced measure that has not started is first withdrawn with `replaced`, 7.6 step 12); `invalid_decision` when the basis's facts are not there or `days` exceeds its number; `admin_required` for a decider who is not an admin. The decision is marked `needs_admin`, so its review and its correction are an admin's (7.11).
4. The profile is held `FOR UPDATE` and `standing_apply` writes `suspended_from` (the decision's time) and `suspended_until` before the commit. Writes of the account in flight finish first; every later one is refused (LAB B: no late write).
5. The statement goes at once as `suspension_started_at_once` and names the basis in plain words; its paragraph of the rule or the term is chosen by the basis (10.6). For `threat` and `terrorist` it names the decision that found the content. For `flood` it says that an admin found the account flooding korbat with repetitive, unsolicited posts (rule R6), and its facts say how many posts the account made between which two times and what they were: the case read carries that count and interval (`standing.flood`, 10.3), so the signed facts stay true whenever the decision commits. For `protective`, step 8.
6. The reply (7.6 step 9) and the appeal are open from the first minute. A reply here is always late: it is read and answered, and only the answer can end the suspension. korbat aims to answer a reply to a suspension taken at once within 24 hours; the terms say so (section 18.7), the queue-age alert goes at that time (7.14), and the aim is flagged for the owner (section 2).
7. `threat` and `terrorist` are in force only while the decision in `rests_on` holds a measure in force, and `protective` only while the seal it rests on is in force: reversing that decision, or lifting that seal, ends the suspension in the same transaction, as in 7.6 step 14.
8. **The protective suspension** is kept, and narrowed to what korbat knows and can check.
   - **Its basis is a fact of the ledger:** the seal for good of the table. "Under R2 and resting on a suspect item" alone is not enough: without such a seal the decision is refused (`invalid_decision`), and the console says "Seal first, in the case the report is in; then suspend here." (section 8).
   - **A decision of its own, on the account's case.** The admin opens a finding on the account under R2, and the final decision that answers it holds the `suspend` and no `seal_media` (`invalid_decision` when it holds one). It answers findings only, as a ladder decision does (`open_reports`, 7.5 step 2): a report that waits in the account's case is decided first, so no reporter reads a line under R2 about the account. For D's freeze and for `suspicion.earlier` (D 7.3) a decision that holds a protective `suspend` does not count as an answer without a seal: what the seal's case froze stays frozen, and nobody may open the images because of this decision.
   - **One for one seal.** A second protective suspension needs a newer seal for good that no protective suspension of the subject, lifted or not, has rested on. So one seal can cost an account at most 14 days, once.
   - **It ends by itself** after its days, and earlier when its seal is lifted (step 7).
   - **Its statement** never says "It breaks korbat rule R2" and asserts nothing the database has not checked. Its ground is the terms' clause on protective suspensions (DSA Art. 17(3)(e): a contractual ground, with its explanation). Its facts name the seal for good and say that korbat's staff do not open images reported this way, so korbat has not assessed them (the fixed words are section 18's; staff may have seen an image in an earlier case, so no text says that nobody has seen them). It says in plain words that it is not a finding that the person broke the law or a rule, and it promises no later look by staff: none takes place before 3f records an authority's answer.
   - **Its appeal does not wait for the authorities.** It is appealed and reviewed at once, by an admin who took no part, on what korbat knows; the review may lift the suspension while the seal stays. Only the seal's own appeal waits, as D has it: this decision holds no seal, so D's sentence on a seal's appeal is not in its statement.
   - **It carries no strike, is no rung of the ladder and supports no termination.** Flagged for the owner and for counsel; section 2 says what the choice costs either way.

### 7.8 Termination

1. **The measure** is `{ kind: "terminate", mediaId: null, days: null, announced }`: a suspension without end (`suspended_until` is `infinity`). Always an admin's (`admin_required`), always on an account case, and `standing_conflict` as in 7.6, with no exception. A termination on `threat_repeat` never meets that rule through the `threat` suspension it counts as the first threat: its second threat is a post made after the person was told of that suspension, a suspension at once stops posting from its first moment, so that post can only have been made after the earlier suspension ended, and an ended suspension is no row of `standing_live` (6.2 step 9a item 2).

| Way | Basis | What the database requires |
|---|---|---|
| Announced, as 7.6 steps 1 to 11 | `ladder_end` | the strikes of 7.6 step 3, with its rule on reports in the case, and at least three standing `strikes` suspensions whose basis holds, decided within `ladder_memory_days`, whatever their length: a moderator who chose fewer days still used that step |
| At once, as 7.7 | `threat_repeat` | the decision cites R5; a `suspend` of the subject on the basis `threat` stands, its basis holds, and it was decided within `ladder_memory_days`; and a final R5 decision as in 7.7's `threat` row restricts a post of the subject that was created after the `informed_at` of that suspension's decision. `rests_on` becomes both R5 decisions: the one the earlier suspension rests on, and the new one |

   **A second threat means a second one.** The termination at once is accepted only while the earlier threat suspension stands with its basis, and only for a post the person made after they were told of that suspension. A first threat that was overruled supports none; neither do two posts of one burst, of which the second was written before the person had read anything. `threat_repeat` holds while every decision in `rests_on` still holds a measure in force, so reversing either R5 decision ends the termination in that transaction (7.6 step 14). The words follow the check: the statement names the decision that found the threat, says that the post was made after korbat suspended the account for an earlier threat, and names the decision that found that earlier threat by its day (`{earlierDecidedAt}`: the older entry of `restsOn`, 10.2). It never says "while a suspension stood".

   A termination under R2 is refused `ground_not_established` (422): korbat has not assessed the images, and the facts that would establish it (an authority's answer, a hash match a person confirmed) are 3f's and 3d's records. Any other termination at once is `invalid_decision`.
2. **Until it is final** the account is suspended and nothing else. It signs in, replies, appeals, exports and may delete itself; its posts stay visible. The reply and its answer are 7.6's.
3. **When it is final.** The account module's purge round (`purgePending`, `apps/api/src/account/purge.ts:18`, every five minutes) first calls `ModerationForAccount.takeDownDue(takeDown)` and passes `(account, facts) => deps.auth.takeDownTerminated(account, facts)`. The account module has no pool, may name no table and has no `mailAccount` (`account/context.ts:40`, `architecture.test.ts:129`), so the lock and the re-check sit inside that one function of the moderation module, which has the API pool and `lockAccounts`. It reads `terminations_due()`: the accounts that are not deleted and hold a `terminate` that stands with its basis, has started, has no unanswered reply, and whose decision is final for its author (`decision_final_at`: the six months unused, or the appeal answered `kept`; 7.6 step 11 says from when they count).
   - A pending appeal, an unanswered reply, on time or late, and a lift each keep an account out. The late reply counts: a termination at once has no other kind, and "only the answer can end the suspension" must not become "nothing can".
   - For each account, in a try of its own:
   1. One transaction on the API pool: `lockAccounts(tx, [account], "update")`, the profile `FOR UPDATE`; then the case of the termination's decision `FOR UPDATE` (profile before case, the order of every decision); then `terminations_due()` read again for this account in a statement of its own. A lift, a reply and a `kept` answer wait at the profile or committed first. An appeal in flight at the deadline takes no profile row: it commits first and is read, or waits at the case and is then refused by its own deadline check.
   2. Still there: the callback, `AuthForAccount.takeDownTerminated(account, facts)`, while that transaction is open. It does what 1b's `confirmDeletion` does, as the system: it reads the account's row with its address, runs 1b's take-down (the account marked deleted, every session revoked, the auth event written), and only after that commit sends `termination_final` to the address it read (`notifyAccount`, best effort, 7.15), in place of 1b's `account_deleted`. `facts` is `{ decidedAt, imagesSealed }`: the decision's time and whether a seal of the account's images is in force, which the mail's words need (section 18.6). The take-down does not remove the address (`finishPurge` does, `auth/repo.ts:511`), so nothing is lost by mailing afterwards. An account that an earlier round took down and whose purge then failed is listed again by `terminations_due()`: the callback finds its row already marked deleted, writes and mails nothing and answers `false` (section 5), and the round's purge finishes the deletion; `takeDownDue` counts it under `terminated` only when the callback answered `true`.
   3. No longer there: nothing is done and no mail goes. So the mail goes once, and only to an account that was taken down.
   4. A failure for one account (a lock wait past its bound, the auth pool down) is caught and logged with the account, and the loop goes on. `takeDownDue` answers `{ terminated, failed }`; `PurgeReport` gains `terminated` and adds these failures to `failed`. The call sits in a try of its own in `purgePending`: whatever happens in it, the round goes on to `pendingPurges()`, so no person's own deletion waits on a termination that cannot be taken down.
   5. The round's ordinary purge then finds the account among `pendingPurges()` and runs 1b's purge as 3c changed it: `purgeProfile`, the images (the freeze keeps sealed ones, D 7.10), `forgetAccount`, `finishPurge`.
4. **One clock.** The purge follows finality and nothing else, so no purge runs while an appeal is pending or a reply is unanswered, and an appeal can always restore what it is about. After the purge nothing can come back: a later correction changes the record only, and the screens say so.
5. **Sooner, by the person.** The person may delete the account at any time (1b). That ends the appeal in practice, and the screen says so before they confirm.
6. **Afterwards** korbat keeps nothing to recognise a new account of the person by: no hash of the address, no device mark. The ledger rows stay as ids, enums and times (section 6, retention). Images that korbat sealed stay, for the authorities only, as their seal's decision said (D 7.10); `termination_final` says so when `imagesSealed` is true, and never says that korbat keeps nothing.

### 7.9 The restriction of reporting

Three steps, each a person's, resting on a moderator's signed mark and never on an outcome alone: "no breach" is not "manifestly unfounded". Every count below counts one unit on both sides, a report: one report is one intake, however many posts and accounts it names.

1. **Counting.** `report_tallies_count` adds 1 to the reporter's row of the UTC day for every intake of kind `user` that has a `reporter_id` and does not allege illegality: the day the report was sent. The API role cannot write the table. A notice of illegality and a report on the path for child sexual abuse material are never counted.
2. **The mark.** When a final decision's outcome is `no_violation`, the moderator may tick "manifestly unfounded" on an item whose `markable` is true: an item this decision answers, from a `user` intake of a signed-in reporter, that does not allege illegality. The ticked ids are the signed `unfounded` list (`[]` when none). The API inserts one `unfounded_marks` row an id (7.1); `unfounded_marks_check` refuses (`invalid_decision`) a row whose decision is not this transaction's, not final or not `no_violation`, or whose item the decision does not answer or is not markable, and forces the reporter and the time. `decision_holds` compares the list with the rows at commit, and again after any later insert (`unfounded_marks_recheck`, 7.1).
3. **What a mark does.** Nothing by itself: it restricts nothing, and no mail goes. The reporter reads it on their own Reports page (D 9), on the marked item's line, once that item's outcome is released to them and not before, with the way to ask for a re-examination (`ReportRow`, 10.2), then in the export and in the statement of a warning. So nobody learns of their marks only when a warning lists them.
   - **A marked report** is a report that holds at least one mark that counts. Marks on six posts named in one report make one marked report.
   - **A mark counts** while its report was sent within `report_frame_days` (90), and stops counting when a re-examination of its item ends in a restriction or when a correction withdraws it (step 9). Only the withdrawal writes anything.
4. **The list.** `StandingLists.reportingRecords` (7.12), from the view `reporting_record`: the accounts at the warning's threshold, and the warned ones, as counts in the frame. `reports` is the account's rule reports sent in the frame (the sum of its tallies) and `unfounded` the marked reports among them. Both count reports by the day they were sent, so `unfounded` never exceeds `reports`.
5. **The warning.** A moderator opens a finding on the account under R6 (7.5 step 2) and confirms `{ kind: "warn_reporting", mediaId: null }` in a final `restricted` decision citing R6. The database requires at least `report_warn_marks` (5) marked reports that are at least `report_warn_share` (half) of the account's rule reports in the frame, and no warning of the account that still counts; else `invalid_decision`. One report can never reach the threshold alone, however many posts it names. The decision answers findings only (7.5 step 2: `open_reports`), so nobody who reported this account's profile reads a line about it. It forces `ends_at` to 90 days on. It has no effect on anything. The facts text holds the counts and the marked reports as links: the console drafts it from the case read (section 8), because the counts are deleted after 90 days and a statement must read as it was sent. The draft words its numbers by the day of that read ("in the 90 days up to ..."), never as those of the decision, which the database checks again at commit. Mailed as `reporting_warned`.
6. **The restriction.** `{ kind: "restrict_reporting", mediaId: null, days, announced: false }` in a final `restricted` decision citing R6, by any moderator; it too answers findings only. The database requires a warning of the account that still counts (its decision becomes `rests_on`), at least `report_restrict_marks` (3) marked reports that were sent after that warning's `informed_at`, `days` at most `report_restrict_days` (30) and no restriction running; else `invalid_decision`. One report after the warning is never enough. `standing_apply` writes `reports_restricted_until`. It starts at once: the warning was its notice. Mailed as `reporting_restricted`.
