7. **What it refuses.** In the intake's transaction, from the reporter's locked row (7.2), for a signed-in sender on ground `rule` only: `account_suspended` (403) when the account is suspended, else `reporting_restricted` (403) when `reportsRestricted` is true. Grounds `illegal` and `csam` read neither. Re-examination requests stay open. The refused request stays counted against the sender's hourly limit, as `already_reported` does.
8. **Lifting.** A review or a correction with `lift_reporting` lifts a warning or a restriction; `standing_apply` clears the column. Both measures can be appealed like any final restriction.
   - **A restriction ends with its warning.** The warning was its only notice, so the basis `reports` of a restriction holds only while the decision in `rests_on` still holds a `warn_reporting` that stands. When a review or correction lifts the warning, the restriction ends in that transaction, with no second appeal: `standing_apply` reads only the restrictions that stand and whose basis holds. The lifting decision's statement names the restriction in `endedStanding` (10.2).
   - **The marks behind a lifted measure** are not touched by the lift. Whoever lifts a warning because its marks were wrong corrects the decisions that set them (step 9), and the same warning cannot then be given again on them.
9. **Withdrawing a mark.** A mark that was wrong is withdrawn by a correction of the decision that set it: outcome `kept` (the finding of no breach stands) and no action. It withdraws all of that decision's marks together: no list is signed and the params gain no member for it (10.4).
   - `decisions_record` accepts this second use of `kept` (7.6 step 11) only when the corrected decision is a final `no_violation` decision that holds a mark not yet withdrawn. `decisions_apply` writes `withdrawn_by` and `withdrawn_at` on every mark of that decision that is not yet withdrawn, the one UPDATE the marks ever get.
   - Any moderator may sign it, the one who set the mark included; a decision that needed an admin is corrected by an admin (D 7.7). A reviewer who lifts a warning because its marks were arguable withdraws them this way.
   - A withdrawn mark stops counting at once and for good; its row stays until the retention job deletes it (7.14).
   - It changes nothing for the author of the reported content and is mailed to nobody (`author_mail` `none`). The reporter's line on their Reports page loses its sentence.

### 7.10 A handle retirement and its reversal

1. **The decision.** A final decision on an account case that holds `{ kind: "retire_handle", mediaId: null }`, by any moderator. `decision_actions_check` refuses it elsewhere, and refuses it (`invalid_decision`) when the decision already holds a row: the retirement is the decision's first row, so that a `reset_display_name` after it copies the placeholder and never the retired handle. The signed list is sorted by kind, where `reset_bio` and `reset_display_name` come before `retire_handle`; so the decide route inserts a `retire_handle` first and the other actions after it in the list's order.
2. **What it does** (`decision_actions_apply`). It retires the handle of the decision's snapshot: it sets `retired_at` on that handle's `handle_history` row under the subject's profile, writing the row first when the account took the handle by a change and has not left it (today a handle is reserved in `handle_history` only when it is left, `social/repo.ts:1094`). It raises `profile_changed` (409) when the handle is not this account's to retire. Then, by compare-and-set, a profile that still holds the handle gets the placeholder (`retired_` and 12 hex digits derived from the measure's id, section 6) and its `handle_changed_at` is cleared; a profile that has moved on is left alone. `no_effect` is `already` for a handle already retired and `deleted` for a deleted account.
3. **The statement** carries the sentence `retire` (the handle, the placeholder, and a new handle without the usual wait) and rides on 3c's decision mail. The sentence's end is chosen from the decision's own measures, so it reads as it was sent: a new handle "at once"; or, when the same decision also suspends the account at once, "when that suspension ends"; with a termination at once it offers none. A reporter whose report the decision answers reads 3c's outcome with the scope phrase for a retirement (7.11 item 7).
4. **Refused to everyone, its former holder included.**
   - `checkHandle` (`apps/api/src/auth/handles.ts:25`) answers `handle_reserved` (400) for any handle that begins with `retired_`, for onboarding and the handle change alike.
   - `changeHandle`'s claim statement (`repo.ts:1090`) and onboarding's `create` (`profiles/store.ts:13`) answer `handle_retired` (409) for a handle whose `handle_history` row is retired.
   - The trigger `profiles_handle_guard` refuses both to every role but the migrator, so a path that forgot the check still cannot write one (LAB E: no profile left holding a retired handle).
   - Refusing a handle needs the handle. One retired under R4 (a doxxed name) therefore stays in `handle_history`, as a handle and nothing else, and 3c's R4 redaction does not reach it (7.14; 11.3; flagged for counsel in section 2).
5. **A new handle at once.** `handle_changed_at` is null after the swap, so the 30-day wait of `changeHandle` (`flows/profile-edit.ts:44`) does not apply to the first change. A suspended account waits for its suspension's end (7.3).
6. **The reversal.** `unretire_handle`, in a review or a correction, clears `retired_at` and `handle_changed_at`. A profile that still holds that measure's placeholder gets the handle back. One that chose another handle since keeps it and may change back at once by the ordinary change. The handle is never given to anyone else.

### 7.11 Appeals, reviews and corrections

3c's appeal (D 6.3, 7.7) is unchanged in shape: one appeal a decision, by its subject, within six months of `informed_at`, while a measure of the decision is in force.

1. **In force, for the six new measures, means that no row reverses it**, whatever the clock says (`measures_in_force`). So a decision whose strike expired or whose suspension ended can be appealed for the whole six months. `appeals_check` changes in one point, the deadline of a decision whose measure a `kept` answer to an on-time reply started: it counts from the later of the two decisions' `informed_at`, and `decision_final_at` and every `appealUntil` read the same (7.6 step 11).
2. **Which decision is appealed.** A strike with its post's decision; a suspension, a termination, a retirement, a warning or a restriction with its account decision. The appeals need not be joined: overruling strikes ends a ladder suspension by derivation (7.6 step 14), lifting a warning ends the restriction that rests on it (7.9 step 8), and lifting a seal ends its protective suspension (7.7 step 7). A protective suspension's appeal is its own decision's and never waits for the seal's (7.7 step 8).
3. **What a review or a correction may hold:** only reversals of the answered decision's measures. One signed member rides beside them, in a correction only: `replaced`, with the `lift_suspension` of an announced measure that has not started (7.6 step 12). A `kept` correction of a `no_violation` decision holds no action and no member of its own: it withdraws all of that decision's marks together (7.9 step 9).

| Reversal | Lifts | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `overrule` | `strike` | only when the outcome is `reversed` |
| `lift_suspension` | `suspend`, `terminate` | |
| `unretire_handle` | `retire_handle` | |
| `lift_reporting` | `warn_reporting`, `restrict_reporting` | |

4. **A review and a pending reply.** A review whose outcome is `kept`, or that lifts the measure, answers the decision's unanswered reply as 7.6 step 11 does. So does a review whose outcome is `partly_reversed` and that leaves the standing measure in force (it gave a retired handle back and kept the suspension, say): the reviewer read the case, and the start is no longer held by a reply that nobody will answer in an appeal. A `kept` review, or such a `partly_reversed` one, of a decision that holds an announced `suspend` or `terminate` takes the profile `FOR UPDATE` first (7.1), and after an on-time reply the suspension starts as in 7.6 step 11: never before `starts_at`.
5. **Who.** `review_needs_admin` is also true for a decision that holds a `terminate` or a `suspend` taken at once; a `restrict_reporting`, which also starts at once, needs no admin. D 7.7's rules stand: the reviewer took no part in the case, and a correction of a decision that needed an admin is an admin's.
6. **Order.** `GET /api/staff/appeals` lists first the appeals of a decision that holds a running suspension or a termination that stands, oldest first, then the rest in D's order (C31): the person who can do nothing while waiting is read first.
7. **Telling.** The statement of every review and correction goes to the person, in the app and by the mail 7.15 names, the mark's withdrawal excepted (7.9 step 9). A notifier or reporter is told the outcome of their own report in 3c's words, once, and never anything from which the author's standing can be followed:
   - A strike is in no line (7.4). A ladder suspension, a ladder termination, a protective suspension, a warning and a restriction of reporting answer findings only (7.5 step 2), so no report is ever answered by one, and `item_current_outcome` need not list the two reporting measures (section 6).
   - The measures that may answer a report are a retirement, a suspension at once on `flood`, `threat` or `terrorist`, and the termination at once. An item of an account case that such a decision answers reads as 3c's restricted outcome for an account item, with `ACCOUNT_MEASURE_SCOPE` (10.1) in place of `ACCOUNT_ITEM_SCOPE`: no name of the measure, no sentence about access, duration or suspension. For a retirement the phrase says that korbat retired the handle, which anyone can see.
   - Nothing later reaches the line, beyond what D already says of a decision changed on review or by a correction: no line for a start, an end by the clock, a reply or its answer, a broken basis or a lifted seal. `item_current_outcome` reads no period, no reply and no basis.

### 7.12 What the person and staff read

Every read below is computed from the ledger, the views of section 6 and the three profile columns at the moment of the request. Nothing here is stored a second time.

**The person.**

- `GET /api/me` gains `standing` (section 10). Today `auth.me` is a finished handler (`auth/routes.ts:252`, mounted at `apps/api/src/app.ts:73`), so two interfaces are added (section 5): `Auth` gains `meFor(session)`, which answers the `MeResponse` without `standing`, beside the handler; and `createModeration`'s result gains `standingFor(userId): Promise<AccountStanding | null>`. `createApp` composes `/api/me` from the two, and answers `standing: null` when it was built without the moderation module (as thirteen HTTP test files build it) or the account has no profile.
  - `standingFor` reads for the account: its active strikes, its standing suspension or termination with its state and times, its reply, its reporting warning and restriction. It is not `auth.account_standing`, the SQL boolean that `grant-role` asks (7.16).
  - No session and no cache holds it, so the banner and a refusal's sentence are true of the row.
- `GET /api/moderation/decisions/:id` (D 7.6) answers the statement's data, which never changes, and beside it `standing`: where the measure stands now (start not fixed, announced, held by a reply, running, ended, never started, lifted, ended with its basis), its exact start and end once the database has them, the strikes it rests on with what each is now, and the reply with its answer. So at the start the person holds a text that is true of what started (DSA Art. 17).
- `GET /api/moderation/cases` (D 7.6) names the new measures and reversals in each row, with a strike's `countsUntil` and whether it was overruled, whether a lift was a withdrawal (`replaced`), and the measures whose basis the decision broke (10.2).
- The post chip: `PostView.moderation` gains `strikeUntil`, set while the post's decision holds a strike that stands and still counts (`ModerationForSocial.authorFacts`).
- The reply screen reads the decision's `standing`.
- `GET /api/moderation/reports` (`ReportsPage`, D 7.6): each item of the person's own reports gains `unfounded`, true while a mark on it counts (7.9 step 3). The status page by reference and token does not carry it: a mark exists only on a signed-in person's report.
- **The export.** `ExportFormat.moderation` (D 7.11) gains `strikes`, `standing`, `replies` and `reporting`, and `handleHistory` entries gain `retiredAt`; section 18.8 lists their members. `ModerationForAccount` reads them as it reads D 7.11's. They hold the account's own data only: a mark is listed by the reference and position of the person's own report.

**Staff.**

- `GET /api/staff/cases/:case` (`CaseView`, D 7.3) gains `strikeDue` and `authorStanding` on a post case, `standing` on an account case (with `flood`, the count and interval that a flood's facts print, `seal`, the day of the seal for good a protective suspension would rest on, and whether reports in the case hold a ladder, protective or reporting measure back), `markable` and the mark's state on each item, and on each measure its new columns with `period` (the start and end as they stand) and `reply`. The reply's text is read here and nowhere else, so the read is logged and counted and a recused person is refused (D 7.3).
- `GET /api/staff/queue` (`QueuePage`) gains `replies`: the unanswered replies, oldest first, on its first page, without text.
- `GET /api/staff/standing` (new, behind `requireStaff`, read only, with its row in the staff route-table test) answers `StandingLists`: the accounts at three strikes (7.5), the reporting records (7.9), the notices korbat could not send (announced measures of live accounts whose mail is `failed`, with the start that follows; a deleted account's `no_address` is not listed, since nothing starts for it), and the terminations waiting (with the day each becomes final, or what holds it: an appeal, or a reply, on time or late). People are handles; no row names a reporter of anything.
- `GET /api/staff/appeals` in the order of 7.11.
- `GET /api/staff/overview` gains `repliesWaiting` and `strikeAccounts`.

### 7.13 Explore's round under formula version 2

`EXPLORE_V2` (section 10) replaces `EXPLORE_V1`: the same numbers, two changed rules. `exploreRules` (`apps/api/src/social/repo.ts:424`) builds the same three fragments and throws for any version but 2, so no ranking is stored under a version whose rules it did not apply.

1. **Candidates.** A post's window and age count from `coalesce(p.explore_from, p.created_at)`, selected as `starts_at`. The window test is written in two arms so that each uses its index: `(p.explore_from is null and p.created_at > since and p.created_at <= at) or (p.explore_from > since and p.explore_from <= at)`. The author's row must pass `liveNotSuspended(a, at)`, with `at` the round's time.
2. **Engagement.** Unchanged in time (likes and reposts of the last 48 hours that have stood 10 minutes, by accounts old enough), and the engager's row must pass `liveNotSuspended(u, at)`. It is judged at the round, never at the time of the like: a suspended account cannot like at all, and a ring's earlier likes stop counting at once (T34).
3. **The score** reads `at - c.starts_at` where it read `c.created_at` (`repo.ts:865`).
4. **A restored post resumes.** `moderation_apply` writes `hidden_at`, `restored_at` and `explore_from` in the statement that moves the level (section 6): `explore_from` is `created_at` plus every span the post spent withheld or removed. A restore gains nothing beyond the time lost, so it may happen any number of times.
5. **The skip rule** (`repo.ts:853`) counts only rankings whose `formula` is 2, so the first round after the deploy ranks at once.
6. **Reads.** `explorePage` (`repo.ts:894`) and `listedInExplore` (`repo.ts:967`) add `liveNotSuspended` on the author at `clock_timestamp()`: a suspended author's posts leave the page and the chip at the next read. When the suspension ends they return with the next round, if still in their window.
7. **The status** (`flows/explore.ts:52`). `ExploreStatusKind` gains `suspended`, answered after `not_visible` and before `hidden_by_author` (M's "after deleted": the code has no kind of that name, `not_visible` covers a deleted post). `outside_window` reads `coalesce(explore_from, created_at) <= since`. A post restored after the ranking's time (`restored_at > run.as_of`) answers `pending`, after `new_since_ranking`. While the newest ranking's `formula` is not 2, only "now" is judged (`pending` when met, else `below_threshold`).
8. **No lever on Explore alone.** A suspension stops everything the account writes; Explore follows from it by a published rule, and the statement names that effect (`explore`, section 18).

### 7.14 The dispatch job, the retention job and alerts

**The dispatch job** (D 7.8, `apps/api/src/moderation/dispatch.ts`).

1. Steps 1 to 4 are unchanged but for the statement's template: `sendStatement` picks it with `mailTemplateOf` (7.15), after a commit and in step 2 alike.
2. **Recording the outcome of an announced measure's mail.** For a decision that holds a `suspend` or `terminate` whose `announced` is true, `recordAuthorMail` (`repo.ts:1457`) runs one transaction: the subject's profile `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` first, then the UPDATE, still only while the row is `pending`. `decisions_mail_standing` then calls `standing_apply` on a row the transaction holds. Without the profile first, the job would take the decision's row and then the profile, against a deciding transaction that holds the profile and then touches decisions. For every other decision the recording stays one statement.
3. **Giving up** (step 6, `repo.ts:1602`). The bulk statement leaves those decisions out; each is given up alone, in the form of item 2, and becomes `failed`, which fixes its start at the decision plus `undelivered_hours`, or at the moment the give-up was recorded when that is later. Five days of trying (`RETENTION.mailGiveUpDays`, 10.1) is less than the 168 hours of the earliest start, so no suspension starts while its mail is still `pending`. The five days are the job's rule, not the database's: when the job was down, stuck or switched off so long that the give-up is recorded later than 336 hours after the decision, the later of the two times keeps the start from lying in the past at the moment it is written.
4. `author_mail_at` is written by the database's clock: for `sent`, once korbat's own relay accepted the mail; and, for a decision that holds an announced measure, on every move out of `pending` (`decisions_mail_forward` stamps it). korbat learns nothing of a later bounce at the person's mail provider (section 11), and no text a person reads says more than "sent" (7.6 step 6).

**The retention job** (D 7.9, `retention.ts`) gains two steps, each a transaction of its own that ends with a `moderation_retention` audit row when it changed anything (metadata: the step and the count).

| Step | Call | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `reply_statements` | `erase_reply_statements()` | blanks the text and the salt of every reply that is answered (`answered_at` is not null) and whose decision is final for its author, as an appeal's statement is erased. An unanswered reply keeps its text however old its decision is: no reply is erased before somebody has read and answered it |
| `report_counts` | `forget_report_counts()` | deletes the marks whose report was sent before the frame of `report_frame_days`, withdrawn ones included, and the tallies of days before the frame |

`forgetAccount` (D 7.10) deletes an account's rows of both count tables at its purge, through the replaced `forget_reporter`. The account purge's part is 7.8 step 3.

**3c's R4 redaction is not widened** (D 6.4, D 7.9). An R4 measure stays one of `remove`, `remove_media`, `reset_bio` and `reset_display_name`: the job's candidate rule and the overview's `erasureOverdue` name those four kinds and never read the new kinds that `measures_in_force` now lists. So a final decision citing R4 whose only measures are 3e's (a ladder suspension whose newest strike is under R4, a handle retired for a doxxed name) is no candidate, and `redact_r4_body` is never called for it. The first task checks this against the built text of 0018.

**Alerts** (D 7.12). 3e adds no alert template and no throttle key.

- **Replies.** `waitingCases` (`repo.ts`, read by the dispatch's queue-age step) also counts the replies unanswered for more than 24 hours (`REPLY_AIM_HOURS`, 10.1) and the oldest one's time. `moderation_waiting` carries them as `replyCount` and `replyOldest` and goes when a lane, an interim measure or a reply is past its time, under the same key and 24-hour window.
- **Bursts.** `moderation_burst` counts every decision, the new kinds included, so a stolen key that suspends at the speed of key prompts is told to the other staff account as before.
- **Nothing else.** An immediate suspension is an admin's, sends its statement at once and puts its appeal first in the list; the lists of 7.12 show what waits. The staff alerts stay mails until 3f's phone channel.

### 7.15 Mails

Seven templates join the mailer's closed union (`apps/api/src/mail/mailer.ts`); section 18.6 holds their subjects, leads and fields. Six are a decision's statement mail under a subject and a lead of their own, and are the six that `AuthForModeration.mailAccount` gains. The seventh, `termination_final`, is a flat template of the auth module and no member of `mailAccount`'s union: the moderation module never sends it. `sendStatement` picks with `mailTemplateOf(statement)`: the first row below that fits, and `moderation_decision` otherwise. One decision still gets one mail, recorded in `author_mail`, and a stop of the address never holds it back, as for every statement (`auth/flows/notifiers.ts:209`).

| Order | Template | Sent for |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `suspension_announced` | a final decision with an announced `suspend` or `terminate` |
| 2 | `suspension_started_at_once` | a final decision with a `suspend` or `terminate` taken at once |
| 3 | `reply_answered` | a correction or review whose statement has `repliedTo`: it kept or reversed the measure a reply was filed against |
| 4 | `suspension_lifted` | a review or correction that holds `lift_suspension` and has no `repliedTo` (a lift without a reply, and a withdrawal with `replaced`, whose sentence is then the lead), or whose `endedStanding` holds a `suspend` or `terminate` |
| 5 | `reporting_warned` | a final decision with `warn_reporting` |
| 6 | `reporting_restricted` | a final decision with `restrict_reporting` |

- A strike, a retirement, their reversals and a `lift_reporting` (also one that ends a restriction with its warning) ride on `moderation_decision`.
- `suspension_lifted` has a lead for each `periodThen` (10.2). For a suspension that had already ended, it says that a past suspension no longer counts, never that it "has ended" now. No lead says "You can use your account as before": another measure, a retired handle or a reporting restriction may still stand, so it says that this suspension no longer restricts the account.
- `termination_final` is no decision's mail: `takeDownTerminated` sends it after its take-down committed (7.8 step 3), once, and only to an account that was taken down.
- No mail names a reporter, a count of reports about the person or a staff handle, and none carries a link to act on (D 7.15).

### 7.16 Staff accounts and `grant-role`

1. **No 3e measure reaches a staff account.** Each is a measure of a final decision, which `decision_actions_check` refuses on a staff account (`staff_target`, D 6.2 step 8). Staff are disciplined by demotion. A staff member's linked personal account is an account like any other; recusal (D 7.3) keeps that person out of its cases.
2. **No suspended account becomes staff.** `grant-role` (`apps/api/src/cli/grant-role.ts`) calls `auth.account_standing(account)` in a statement of its own after its `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` on the `auth.users` row. When it answers true and the new role is not `user`, nothing is changed: `changeRole` (`apps/api/src/auth/repo.ts:405`) answers `"standing"`, a third answer beside a `RoleChange` and `undefined` (no live account has the address), `grantRole` (`apps/api/src/auth/admin.ts:38`) passes it on, and the command prints the sentence `grantRole.standing` and exits 1.
3. **Both orders are closed.** A deciding transaction reads that `auth.users` row `FOR SHARE` (`account_is_staff`), so it either waits for `grant-role` and then meets `staff_target`, or commits first and `grant-role` then reads the standing. A suspended moderator who keeps the console (T54) is not a state the database can reach.

## 8. The console (`apps/admin`)

D 8 stays whole: text-only rendering, one CSS file, every answer parsed with the contracts' schemas, the key asked only after the console checked what it signs, no link into the app, people shown as handles. 3e adds one route, one list answer and words. Every sentence below is a member of `STANDING_WORDS` in `apps/admin/src/messages.ts`, under the key in brackets. Every number in one is printed from the answer, from `STANDING_RULES` or from the two constants beside it (`RETENTION.mailGiveUpDays`, `REPLY_AIM_HOURS`, 10.1), never typed, so a table test can hold them (section 12).

### 8.1 What the console reads

- **Router.** `standing` (`/standing`); `ConsolePath` gains `"/standing"` (`apps/admin/src/router.ts:8`); the navigation gains "Standing" after "Appeals" (`Layout.tsx`).
- **Answers** (section 10 defines them): on a post case `CaseView.strikeDue` and `CaseView.authorStanding`; on an account case `CaseView.standing`; on each item `markable` and `unfounded`; on each decision's measures `days`, `announced`, `basis`, `period` and `reply`; `QueuePage.replies`; `StandingLists` from `GET /api/staff/standing`; `StaffOverview.repliesWaiting` and `strikeAccounts`.
- **What the console needs beyond M 6's four members of `standing`** (`activeStrikes`, `maxDays`, `mayTerminate`, `live`), so that each control can say why it is greyed before the key is asked: `ladderRule` (the rule of the newest strike), `suspensionsInYear`, `atOnce` (which of `threat`, `terrorist`, `flood`, `protective`, `threat_repeat` the database would accept now), `flood` (`posts`, `from`, `to`: the count the flood ground reads and the interval it was counted over, for the draft of W.5), `seal` (null, or `decidedOn`: the day of the seal for good that a protective suspension would rest on) and `reporting` (`reports`, `unfounded`, `warnedOn`, `marksSince`, `mayWarn`, `mayRestrict`, `restrictedUntil`). In `reporting` every count is a count of reports (intakes), never of items: `reports` is the account's rule reports sent in the frame, `unfounded` those of them that hold at least one mark that counts, `marksSince` the marked reports sent since the warning; so `unfounded` can never be more than `reports`.
- `standing.openReports` (section 10): the case holds a report that waits, an unanswered item of a `user` intake. The console greys five measures with [reports.first] while it is true: the two announced ones, the protective suspension at once and the two reporting measures (8.3).
- A refusal that these members did not foresee means the facts moved between the read and the key. The console words `invalid_decision` on a standing measure as [changed]: "The database refused this measure: the account's strikes, measures or reports are no longer what this page showed. Read the case again; the controls then say what is possible."

### 8.2 A post case: the strike line and the author's standing

The composer shows one line under the outcome "Restrict" of "Decide the case". It is not a choice: the console adds `strike` to the signed actions exactly when `strikeDue.due` is true, and the database refuses any other list (`strike_mismatch`).

| Key | Shown when | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| [strike.due] | `strikeDue.due` | "This decision gives a strike: it restricts a post under a korbat rule, and the post was made after its author was told of their newest strike, or they have none. You do not choose this. It counts also when the post is already removed or deleted." |
| [strike.none.interim] | "While deciding" | "No strike: a measure taken while deciding never gives one." |
| [strike.none.law] | the ground is the law | "No strike: a decision on the law alone never gives one." |
| [strike.none.r2] | rule R2, or the decision answers a suspect item | "No strike: a decision under R2, or one that answers a report of child sexual abuse material, never gives one." |
| [strike.none.already] | `strikeDue.why` is `already_struck` | "No strike: this post already carries a strike that stands." |
| [strike.none.before] | `strikeDue.why` is `before_last_strike` | "No strike: the author posted this before they were told of their newest strike, so it is no repeat after that warning." |

Above the evidence, from `authorStanding`, in bands, as D 8 words a reporter's summary:

- "Active strikes: none" (or "1", "2", "3 or more"); "Suspensions decided in the last {ladderMemoryDays} days: none" (or "1", "2 or more"); "Now: no measure" (or "a suspension is announced", "suspended", "terminated, waiting for deletion").
- [post.standing] "Decide this post on what it says. The author's standing never makes a post a breach; it is shown so that you know what a strike here leads to."

An account case shows no strike line: no decision about a profile gives one (M 2 row 4).

### 8.3 An account case: the standing measures

They sit in the decision composer under "Decide the case", outcome "Restrict", beside 3c's two resets, and are not offered "While deciding": the database accepts them in a final decision only. The measures are named by `MEASURES[kind].label` (W.1); a suspension and a termination have two controls each, worded as below. A control the database would refuse is greyed and carries its sentence.

| Control | Sends | Who | Greyed with |
|---|---|---|---|
| Retire the handle | `retire_handle`, always the first action | any moderator | [retire.none] |
| Announce a suspension | `suspend`, `days`, `announced: true` | any moderator | [conflict], [reports.first], [suspend.short], [suspend.stale] |
| Suspend at once | `suspend`, `days`, `announced: false` | an admin | [conflict], [atOnce.admin], [atOnce.unmet], [atOnce.noSeal], and on the protective ground [reports.first] |
| Announce a termination | `terminate`, `announced: true` | an admin | [conflict], [reports.first], [terminate.notYet] |
| Terminate at once | `terminate`, `announced: false` | an admin | [conflict], [terminate.noRepeat], [terminate.r2] |
| Warn about reporting | `warn_reporting` | any moderator | [reports.first], [warn.short], [warn.standing] |
| Restrict reporting | `restrict_reporting`, `days`, `announced: false` | any moderator | [reports.first], [restrict.noWarning], [restrict.short], [restrict.running] |

At most one of the four suspension and termination controls can be chosen, and none while `standing.live` is true.

**Shared.**

- [conflict] "Not open: a suspension or termination of this account already stands and has not ended. To change it, lift it first with a correction of its decision. If it is announced and has not started, and an admin must take a measure at once, use "Withdraw it for another measure" on that decision (8.7): the person is then told that korbat withdrew it for another measure, not that their reply succeeded."
- [reports.first] "Not open: this case holds a report that is not answered yet. Decide the reports in this case first; the measure gets a case of its own." It greys the five measures that rest on the account's record, or on a seal in another decision, and not on anything a report in this case said (an announced suspension, an announced termination, a protective suspension at once, the warning and the restriction of reporting), so that none of them ever answers a report: the person who sent one would read an outcome that their report did not lead to, and could follow the account's standing through it (section 10, T43). The database refuses the same decision with `invalid_decision`.
- [facts] "The facts are drafted from the record as this page read it (W.5): they say when. Write the reason yourself: why this measure, and why this length."

**Retirement.**

- [retire.what] "Retiring a handle takes it from everyone for good, its holder included. Use it when the breach lies in the handle itself: impersonation, a doxxed name, a threat. The profile then shows a placeholder (retired_ and 12 characters), and its holder may choose a new handle at once, or, if the account is suspended, when that suspension ends."
- [retire.first] "The retirement is this decision's first measure. A reset of the display name added to it copies the placeholder, never the retired handle."
- [retire.moved] (the live handle differs from the snapshot's) "The account changed its handle after this snapshot. Confirming retires the handle shown in the snapshot and leaves the new one alone. To judge the new handle, take a fresh snapshot."
- [retire.none] "Not open: this profile holds a placeholder, so there is no handle to retire." When the snapshot's handle is already retired the control stays open with D 8's no-effect sentence.

**An announced suspension.**

- [suspend.what] "This announces a suspension of {days} days. Nothing is restricted when you confirm. The account is mailed at once and may reply. With no reply the suspension starts by itself, {replyWindowHours} hours after korbat sent the mail, on your signature: nobody acts at the start. korbat cannot see whether the mail reached the person; the app always shows the notice."
- [suspend.days] "At most {maxDays} days: this would be the account's suspension number {n} in {ladderMemoryDays} days, and the steps are {ladderDays} days. Shorter is allowed, down to 1 day; a shorter suspension still uses its step."
- [suspend.rule] "The decision cites {ladderRule}, the rule of the newest strike; the composer sets it. The statement names korbat's terms as its ground, that rule as the rule of the newest strike, and every strike it rests on."
- [suspend.short] "Not open: an announced suspension needs {ladderStrikes} active strikes. This account has {activeStrikes}."
- [suspend.stale] "Not open: no strike is newer than the account's last suspension under the ladder. A next step needs at least one new strike."

**A suspension at once.** The composer lists the four grounds, each marked "Met now" or [atOnce.unmet] "Not met now", and offers the control when the ground of the chosen rule is met. An announced suspension never takes one of these grounds, whatever rule it cites: under R5 too it is a step of the ladder, at most {maxDays} days (section 6).

- [atOnce.what] "A suspension at once restricts the account when you confirm. Only an admin takes one, and only on a ground the database checks. The statement goes at once; the account may reply and appeal from the first minute, and a reply does not pause it."
- [atOnce.threat] "Direct threat (R5): a final decision under R5 that restricts a post of this account stands and is at most {basisAgeDays} days old. Up to {threatDays} days. It ends by itself if that decision is reversed."
- [atOnce.terrorist] "Terrorist content: a final decision on the law, marked terrorist content, that restricts a post of this account stands and is at most {basisAgeDays} days old. Up to {terroristDays} days. It ends by itself if that decision is reversed."
- [atOnce.flood] "Spam flood (R6): the account made {posts} posts between {from} and {to}; the ground needs at least {floodPosts} in {floodMinutes} minutes. Up to {floodDays} days. The count alone is no flood: say in the facts what made the posts repetitive and unsolicited." (`posts`, `from` and `to` are `standing.flood`.)
- [atOnce.protective] "Protective: an admin sealed images of this account for good on {sealedOn}, at most {basisAgeDays} days ago, and no protective suspension has rested on that seal. Up to {protectiveDays} days, once for that seal. Seal first, in the case the report is in; then suspend here, in a decision of its own. korbat's staff do not open images reported this way, so the facts and the reason never say or imply that the account posted such material, and they promise no later look by staff. It is no finding, gives no strike, is no step of the ladder and supports no termination. It ends on its date, or earlier if the seal is lifted. Its appeal is reviewed at once by an admin who took no part; only the seal's own appeal waits for the authorities." (`sealedOn` is `standing.seal.decidedOn`.)
- [atOnce.noSeal] (the protective ground is not met) "Not met now: a protective suspension rests on a seal for good of images of this account or of one of its posts, decided at most {basisAgeDays} days ago, on which no protective suspension has rested yet. A report alone, or a seal taken while deciding, is not enough."
- [atOnce.admin] "Only an admin suspends at once. You can withhold the content while deciding; the account measure waits for an admin."
- [atOnce.other] (rule R1, R3 or R4) "This rule allows no suspension at once: announce it."

**Termination.**

- [terminate.what] "A termination is a suspension without end. Until the decision is final for the account (six months unused, or its appeal answered and kept) the account is only suspended: it signs in, replies, appeals, exports and may delete itself, and its posts stay visible. Nothing is deleted while its appeal or its reply, on time or late, is unanswered. Then korbat deletes it, and keeps nothing to recognise a new account of the person by; images that korbat sealed stay for the authorities."
- [terminate.notYet] "Not open: an announced termination needs {ladderRungs} suspensions under the ladder decided in the last {ladderMemoryDays} days, whatever their length, and {ladderStrikes} active strikes of which one is newer than the last of them." (`ladderRungs` is the number of entries of `STANDING_RULES.ladderDays`.)
- [terminate.noRepeat] "Not open: a termination at once needs a second direct threat after a suspension for the first. That is: a suspension at once for a direct threat, decided in the last {ladderMemoryDays} days, that still stands with the decision it rests on; and a final R5 decision at most {basisAgeDays} days old about a post the account made after it was told of that suspension. A second post from before the suspension, or a first threat that was overruled, is no ground."
- [terminate.r2] "No termination under R2: korbat's staff do not open images reported this way, so korbat has not assessed them and nothing is established. A protective suspension is the most a sealed report allows." The same sentence words the code `ground_not_established`.

**Reporting.** Both measures cite R6; the composer sets it.

- [warn.what] "A warning restricts nothing. It tells the account that {unfounded} of the {reports} reports it sent under the rules in the last {frameDays} days were marked manifestly unfounded, lists them, and says that {restrictMarks} marked reports among those it sends after the warning may lead to a restriction. A report counts once, however many of its items were marked, and by the day it was sent. A notice of illegal content is never counted."
- [warn.short] "Not open: a warning needs at least {warnMarks} marked reports among those the account sent in the last {frameDays} days, and they must be at least half of its rule reports sent in that time. This account has {unfounded} of {reports}. One report never reaches the number alone."
- [warn.standing] "Not open: the warning of {warnedOn} still counts."
- [restrict.what] "This stops the account's reports under korbat's rules for {days} days, from the moment you confirm; the warning was its notice, and the restriction ends with it if the warning is lifted. At most {restrictDays} days. Notices of illegal content, reports of child sexual abuse material and requests for re-examination stay open to it."
- [restrict.noWarning] "Not open: the account has no warning that still counts. Warn first."
- [restrict.short] "Not open: {marksSince} of the reports the account sent since the warning of {warnedOn} were marked; a restriction needs {restrictMarks}."
- [restrict.running] "Not open: a restriction runs until {restrictedUntil}."

The preview is the statement from `statementParagraphs` (W.2), as for every decision. Below it the composer adds [preview.times] "The exact start and end are fixed by the database after you confirm; the person's screen shows them."

### 8.4 The mark "manifestly unfounded"

With the outcome "No breach" of "Decide the case", the composer shows a tick box beside each item the decision answers whose `markable` is true. The ticked item ids are the signed `unfounded` list (`[]` when none).

- [mark.label] "Manifestly unfounded".
- [mark.rule] "Tick only a report that anyone could see was unfounded without weighing it: it names nothing the rule could cover. A report you had to think about is not manifestly unfounded, also when you found no breach. Your key signs the mark. What counts toward a warning about reporting is the report, once, for {frameDays} days from the day it was sent, however many of its items are ticked. The person who sent it sees the mark on their own list of reports once this answer is released to them, and can ask for a re-examination."
- [mark.never] (once, when an item is not markable) "Only a signed-in person's report under a rule can be marked: never a notice of illegal content, a report on the path for child sexual abuse material, or a report sent without an account."
- [mark.undone] "A mark stops counting when a re-examination of its report ends in a restriction, and when a correction of the decision that set it withdraws it."
- [mark.withdraw] (in "Correct this decision" on a "No breach" decision that set marks, one control for the decision) "Withdraw this decision's marks". Under it: "Use this when the reports were arguable after all: it was wrong to call them manifestly unfounded, also if no rule was broken. All marks this decision set are withdrawn together. The outcome of each report stays. The marks stop counting when you confirm, so a warning lifted because its marks were wrong cannot be given again on them." The correction signs no list: its outcome is `kept`, since the finding of no breach stands, it holds no action, and the database withdraws every mark of the corrected decision that still stands (7.9 step 9). Afterwards each of its marked items reads "Mark withdrawn" (`CaseItem.markWithdrawn`).

The console never shows who sent a marked report; the reveal of D 8 is unchanged.

### 8.5 Replies to answer, and the appeals' order

**Queue.** `/queue` gains a first section "Replies to answer", oldest first, from `QueuePage.replies`. A row holds: how long it has waited; "before the start: the suspension is held", "after the start: it runs until {endsAt}" or, for a late reply whose suspension has run out since, "after the start: it ended on {endsAt}"; "Suspension, {days} days" or "Termination" (for a termination: "nothing is deleted before the reply is answered"); "waiting more than {replyAimHours} hours" when so (`REPLY_AIM_HOURS`, 10.1); and the `CaseLink`. A row carries no text: the reply is read on the case page, where the access log and the recusal of D 7.3 apply (the queue is not recused). A reply leaves the list when a decision answers it: a correction, a review, or the reversal that broke the basis of its measure (section 6).

**Case page.** Under the decision that holds the measure, a block "Reply": the text in a `UserText` frame "Written by the account's holder" ([reply.empty] "The reply has no text: the person asks for a second look."), when it was filed, [reply.onTime] "Filed before the start: the suspension has not started and does not start until someone answers, however long that takes." or [reply.late] "Filed after the start: the suspension goes on while you read." (once its days have run out: [reply.lateEnded] "Filed after the start: the suspension ended on {endedAt}. The reply is still answered."); and "Answer this reply", which opens a correction of that decision with two outcomes:

| Outcome | Signed | After an on-time reply | After a late reply |
|---|---|---|---|
| Keep | correction `kept`, no action | [keep.onTime] "It starts when you confirm, or on {earliestStart} if that is later: never before the time the person was given. It runs {days} days from then, and the person's six months to appeal count again from your answer." | [keep.late] "Nothing changes; it runs until {endsAt}." or, once it has ended, [keep.lateEnded] "Nothing changes; it ended on {endedAt}, as decided." |
| Reverse | correction `reversed` with `lift_suspension` (`partly_reversed` when another measure of the decision stays) | [reverse.onTime] "It never starts." | [reverse.late] "It ends when you confirm." or, once it has ended, [reverse.lateEnded] "It ended on {endedAt}. Reversing it takes it off the record: it no longer counts toward a later suspension." |

- [reply.basisGone] (in place of "Answer this reply", when the measure's basis is broken) "The decision that reversed what this suspension rested on answered this reply: the suspension does not start, or ended with that decision. Nothing is left to keep." The database refuses a `kept` answer for such a measure (`invalid_decision`).
- [reply.reason] "Write the reason for the person: say what you weighed in their reply. They receive it by email, and can still appeal the decision." D 8's sentence on personal data follows.
- [reply.same] "You may answer a reply to your own decision: the reply is the hearing, not the appeal. An appeal goes to another staff member."
- [reply.admin] "A reply to a suspension taken at once, or to a termination, is answered by an admin."
- A correction drafted before the reply arrived is refused `case_changed` (the reply raised the case's version), in D 8's words.

**Appeals.** `/appeals` lists first the appeals of a decision that holds a running suspension or a termination, then the rest as D 8 orders them, under [appeals.order] "Appeals of a running suspension or of a termination come first."

### 8.6 The Standing screen (`/standing`)

Four lists from one answer, each newest first, each row ending in a `CaseLink` where a case exists. No list is a queue key, and none is weighted (D 2 row Queue order and weight).

| List | Row | Control |
|---|---|---|
| "Accounts at {ladderStrikes} strikes" (the number is printed from `STANDING_RULES`; this design calls the list "Accounts at three strikes") | "@{handle}: {activeStrikes} active strikes, the newest under {rule} on {day}. A suspension of at most {maxDays} days can be announced." or, when `mayTerminate`, "... A termination can be announced." | "Open a case on this account": D 8's Own finding with the account and the newest strike's rule filled in. When the account's open case holds reports that are not answered, those are decided first; the measure then gets a case of its own ([reports.first], 8.3) |
| "Reporting records" | "@{handle}: {unfounded} of the {reports} rule reports sent in the last {frameDays} days hold a mark manifestly unfounded." then "At the warning threshold." or "Warned on {warnedOn}; {marksSince} of the reports sent since then are marked." | the same control, under R6 |
| "Notices not sent" | "@{handle}: korbat's mail service could not send the notice of {decidedOn}: it refused the address, or the mail was given up after {mailGiveUpDays} days. Only the app carries the notice, so it starts on {startsAt}, {undeliveredHours} hours after the decision." (`mailGiveUpDays` is `RETENTION.mailGiveUpDays`, 10.1.) The list holds the notices recorded `failed` only. A notice recorded `no_address` belongs to an account that was deleted (`mailAccount` answers it for no other): nothing starts for it and the list leaves it out. korbat reads no bounce, so a mail that korbat sent and the person's provider then refused is not in this list | none |
| "Terminations waiting" | "@{handle}: terminated on {decidedOn}." then "Final on {finalOn}; the account is deleted in the first round after that." or "An appeal is pending: nothing is deleted before it is answered." or, for a reply sent before the start, "A reply is unanswered: it has not started." or, for a late one, "A reply is unanswered: nothing is deleted before it is answered." `finalOn` is read fresh: after a `kept` answer to an on-time reply it is six months after that answer | none |

- [lists.strikes] "An account appears here while it has {ladderStrikes} active strikes, one of them newer than its last suspension under the ladder, and no suspension or termination that stands and has not ended. Nothing happens by itself: a moderator opens a case, reads the strikes' decisions and decides."
- [lists.finding] "Opening the case is a finding of your own, so you cannot review an appeal in it."

### 8.7 Reviews and corrections of standing measures

The review composer and "Correct this decision" (D 8) offer, from `MEASURES[kind].lifts`, the reversal of each standing measure of the reviewed decision that still stands: "Overrule the strike", "Lift the suspension" (or "Lift the termination"), "Give the handle back", "Lift the warning about reporting", "Lift the restriction of reporting".

- [review.strike] "A strike falls only with its whole finding: the console adds its overruling when you choose Reverse, and never with Reverse in part."
- [review.rests] (the reviewed post decision's strike carries a suspension that stands) "A suspension of this account rests on this strike. If you reverse this decision and fewer than {ladderStrikes} of its strikes then stand, the suspension loses its basis when you confirm: one that has not started will not start, one that runs ends, and one that has already ended no longer counts toward a later suspension. This decision's statement says which. A reply to it that nobody answered is answered by your decision."
- [review.basis] (the reviewed decision is the R5 or terrorist-content decision, or the seal for good, that a suspension at once rests on; or one of the two R5 decisions under a termination at once) "A suspension (or termination) of this account rests on this decision. If you reverse it, or lift the seal, that measure ends when you confirm, and this decision's statement says so."
- [review.warning] (the reviewed decision holds a warning that a restriction of reporting rests on) "A restriction of this account's reports rests on this warning. If you lift the warning, the restriction ends when you confirm, and this decision's statement says so."
- [review.reply] (the reviewed decision holds a suspension or termination with an unanswered reply) "This review also answers the person's reply. Keep, and Reverse in part when the suspension stays, answer it as a kept answer does: a suspension held by the reply then starts when you confirm, or at its announced time if that is later. Read the reply before you decide; its text is kept until it is answered."
- [review.handle] "Giving the handle back clears its retirement. A profile that still holds the placeholder gets the handle again; one that chose another handle since may change back at once."
- [review.admin] "A decision that holds a termination, or a measure taken at once, is reviewed and corrected by an admin."
- [review.protective] "A protective suspension is reviewed at once, on what korbat knows: the review does not wait for the authorities, and it may lift the suspension while the seal stays. Only the appeal of the seal's own decision waits."
- [review.gone] (the account was deleted after a final termination) "The account was deleted after this termination became final. A reversal changes the record only: nothing can be brought back."

**Withdraw it for another measure.** "Correct this decision" on a decision whose announced suspension or termination has not started offers, to an admin and only when the composer of the account's case showed [conflict], the control "Withdraw it for another measure". It signs a correction with `lift_suspension` and the signed member `replaced: true` (section 10), and no other action.

- [replace.what] "Use this only to make room for a measure at once: after you confirm, take that measure in the account's case. The person is told that korbat withdrew the announced suspension because it is taking another measure, and that this is no decision on their reply. A withdrawn suspension was never a step of the ladder."
- [replace.reply] (an unanswered reply holds the start) "The person's reply is closed by this withdrawal without an answer on its merits. Say in the reason that their reply was read, if it was, and that they can reply to the new measure."
- It is not offered for a measure that has started, nor to lift a measure for any other reason: those are "Lift the suspension", whose statement says that korbat lifted it.

**Withdraw this decision's marks.** [mark.withdraw] (8.4): a `kept` correction of a "No breach" decision that set marks. It withdraws all of that decision's marks together and signs no list.

### 8.8 Home

The home screen gains two lines, each shown when above zero: "Replies waiting: {n}, the oldest for {age}. A held suspension does not start until its reply is answered, and a terminated account is not deleted before its reply is." and "Accounts at {ladderStrikes} strikes: {n}." Each is a link to its list.

### 8.9 Messages

`CONSOLE_MODERATION_CODES` and `MODERATION_MESSAGES` (`apps/admin/src/messages.ts:28,50`) gain the five codes that section 10 adds to `ModerationErrorCode`. The first three are the ones the composer meets; the other two cannot reach the console today and are worded so that the table has one entry for each code of its type. The list is typed `satisfies readonly ModerationErrorCode[]`, so it cannot hold `handle_retired` and `handle_reserved` (an `AuthErrorCode`) or `account_suspended` (a `SocialErrorCode`); no staff route answers them, and they get no words here.

| Code | Words |
|---|---|
| `strike_mismatch` | "The strike rule changed for this post since this page read it. Read the case again and decide again." |
| `standing_conflict` | [conflict], 8.3 |
| `ground_not_established` | [terminate.r2], 8.3 |
| `reply_not_open` | "This measure takes no reply: it has ended or was lifted." |
| `reporting_restricted` | "That account's reports under the rules are restricted." |

`staff_target` keeps its words: no standing measure reaches a staff account, since each is a measure of a final decision.

### 8.10 The browser check

`apps/admin/test/browser/` gains fixtures for the new answers with hostile strings in the reply's text and in handles. Each run is added by the task that builds what it drives (section 13), so no task runs a screen it has not built:

| Task | Runs |
|---|---|
| 1 | a post decision that signs the strike the answer says is due; a review that overrules a strike with [review.rests] shown |
| 3 | a suspension at once offered to an admin and greyed for a moderator, with its four grounds marked; the protective ground greyed with [atOnce.noSeal] |
| 4 | an account case on which a suspension is announced (the options bind `days` and `announced`), and the same case greyed with [reports.first] while it holds an unanswered report; the Replies section, the reply read on its case and answered Keep; "Withdraw it for another measure" on an announced suspension; the Standing screen's four lists and "Open a case on this account" |
| 5 | a "No breach" decision with one item marked; a correction that withdraws that decision's marks |

It still fails on any policy or Trusted Types report.

## 9. The app (`apps/app`)

D 9 stays. The new sentences live in one table, `STANDING_TEXTS` in `apps/app/src/lib/standing.ts` (keys in brackets), with their numbers from `STANDING_RULES` (the banner's days of waiting for the mail from `RETENTION.mailGiveUpDays`, 10.1). Times are printed by one function, `standingTime(at)`: the device's local date and time, then the UTC time in brackets ("16 Oct 2026, 16:05 (14:05 UTC)"), so the screen and korbat's mails name the same minute.

### 9.1 The banner

`AppShell` (`apps/app/src/components/AppShell.tsx`) renders `StandingBanner` above every signed-in screen while `/me`'s `standing.measure` is not null (section 10: its kind, state, decision, days, earliest start, the start and end once fixed, the reply, the appeal's deadline). It cannot be dismissed, so a deadline is never out of sight. The app reads `/me` again when it gains focus and after any refusal `account_suspended`.

| State | Sentence |
|---|---|
| `unfixed` | [banner.unfixed] "korbat has decided to suspend your account for {days} days. Nothing is restricted yet. It will not start before {earliestStart}; the exact time appears here within {mailGiveUpDays} days, once korbat knows whether its email to you could be sent." (`RETENTION.mailGiveUpDays`, 10.1) |
| `announced` | [banner.announced] "korbat will suspend your account for {days} days from {startsAt}. Nothing is restricted until then. If you reply before it starts, it waits for korbat's answer." |
| `held` | [banner.held] "You replied on {repliedOn}. The suspension does not start while your reply is unanswered. korbat answers you here and by email. If korbat keeps its decision, the suspension starts with that answer, and never before {earliestStart}." |
| `running` | [banner.running] "Your account is suspended until {endsAt}. You can read, reply to korbat, appeal, export your data and delete your account. You cannot post, like, repost, bookmark, follow, edit your profile, upload images or report under korbat's rules." |
| a termination, `unfixed` or `announced` | [banner.unfixed] and [banner.announced] with "terminate your account" in place of "suspend your account for {days} days" |
| a termination, `held` | [banner.held], reading "The termination" |
| a termination, `running` | [banner.terminated] "korbat has terminated your account. You cannot post or interact; your posts stay visible for now. korbat deletes the account once the decision is final: on {appealUntil} if you do not appeal, or when your appeal is answered and the decision kept. Until then you can reply, appeal, export your data, or delete the account yourself." |
| a termination, `running`, its appeal pending | [banner.appealed] the first two sentences of [banner.terminated], then "Your appeal is waiting for a reviewer. Nothing is deleted before it is answered." |
| a termination, `running`, its reply unanswered | [banner.replied] after [banner.terminated] or [banner.appealed]: "Your reply is waiting for an answer. Nothing is deleted before it is answered." |

Buttons, each shown while it applies: "Read the decision" (`/settings/cases/<decisionId>`), "Reply" (`/settings/cases/<decisionId>/reply`, while no reply was sent), "Appeal" (the decision's screen, while the appeal is open), "Export my data" and "Delete account" (`/settings/account`).

### 9.2 The decision's screen (`/settings/cases/[id]`)

The statement is `statementParagraphs` (W.2), which never changes. Below it, for a decision that holds a `suspend` or `terminate`, the block "Where it stands now" from `DecisionView.standing`, read fresh:

| State | Sentence |
|---|---|
| `unfixed` | [now.unfixed] "Start: not fixed yet, and not before {earliestStart}." |
| `announced` | [now.announced] "Starts on {startsAt}. Ends on {endsAt}." |
| `held` | [now.held] "Held by your reply of {repliedOn}: it has not started." |
| `running` | [now.running] "Started on {startsAt}. Ends on {endsAt}." |
| `ended` | [now.ended] "Ran from {startsAt} to {endsAt}." |
| `never` | [now.never] "Never started: korbat reversed it on {liftedOn}." or, when the lift was signed `replaced`, [now.withdrawn] "Never started: korbat withdrew it on {liftedOn} because it took another measure on your account." |
| `lifted` | [now.lifted] "Lifted on {liftedOn}." for one that was running; for one that had already ended, [now.liftedEnded] "Ran from {startsAt} to {endsAt}. korbat lifted it on {liftedOn}: it no longer counts toward a later suspension." |
| `basis_ended` | one of three, by where the measure was when its basis broke (`startsAt` and `endsAt` against `endedAt`): [now.basis.before] "It will not start: korbat reversed a decision it rested on, on {endedOn}."; [now.basis.running] "Ended on {endedOn}, because a decision it rested on was reversed."; [now.basis.after] "Ran from {startsAt} to {endsAt}. On {endedOn} korbat reversed a decision it rested on, so it no longer counts toward a later suspension." For a protective suspension the three read "korbat lifted the seal it rested on" in place of "korbat reversed a decision it rested on" |
| a termination, `running` | [now.terminated] "Started on {startsAt}. It has no end date. The account is deleted once this decision is final: on {appealUntil} if you do not appeal. Nothing is deleted while your appeal or your reply is unanswered." |

- [now.lifted], [now.liftedEnded], [now.never], [now.withdrawn] and the three [now.basis.*] link to the decision that did it (`endedBy`).
- **A restriction of reporting**, on its decision's screen, has the same block in four sentences from `DecisionView.reportingStanding` (section 10), since it too can end before its date (with its warning, section 6): "Runs until {until}.", "Ended on {endedOn}.", "Lifted on {liftedOn}." and [now.warningGone] "Ended on {endedOn} with the warning it rested on, which korbat lifted.", the last two with a link to the decision that did it.
- **The strikes it rests on** (the main design's line 192): one row a strike, "Strike of {decidedOn} under {rule}: counts until {endsAt}" (or "no longer counts", "overruled on {day}"), each a link to that decision's screen, under [rests.hint] "Each strike is appealed on its own decision's screen. If one is overruled and fewer than {ladderStrikes} of these still stand, this suspension ends at once."
- **The decision it rests on**, for a measure taken at once on a basis that has one (`restsOn`, section 10): "It rests on korbat's decision of {decidedOn}." (two rows for a termination at once; for a protective suspension "It rests on the decision of {decidedOn} that keeps images of your account sealed."), each a link to that decision's screen, under [rests.basis] "That decision is appealed on its own screen. If it is reversed, this measure ends at once." For a protective suspension the hint adds "You can appeal this suspension here without waiting for that."
- **Reply.** "Reply" while the reply is open. Afterwards "You replied on {filedOn}." with the person's own text while korbat keeps it ("korbat erased the text on {erasedOn}." after; the text is never erased before the reply is answered), then one of: "korbat has not answered yet."; "korbat answered on {answeredOn}." with a link to the answer's screen; when the measure was withdrawn for another one, [reply.closed.withdrawn] "korbat withdrew this suspension on {answeredOn} for another measure. That was no decision on your reply."; when its basis broke, [reply.closed.basis] "korbat reversed a decision this suspension rested on, on {answeredOn}, so your reply needs no answer." The last two link to that decision.
- **Appeal.** D 9's control, and above it for an announced measure [appeal.early] "You can appeal now; you do not have to wait for the start. An appeal does not hold the start: only a reply sent before the start does." After a kept answer to a reply sent before the start, [appeal.again] "korbat kept this decision after your reply, on {answeredOn}. You can appeal until {appealUntil}: the six months count again from that answer." (`appealUntil` is read fresh.)
- **A strike**, on its post decision's screen: [strike.counts] "Strike: counts until {endsAt}.", [strike.expired] "Strike: no longer counts since {endsAt}.", [strike.overruled] "Strike: overruled on {day}."
- **A termination**: beside the appeal, and in Settings, Account above "Delete account" while `/me` carries a termination: [terminate.delete] "If you delete your account now, your appeal ends with it: nothing is left to restore."

### 9.3 The reply screen (`/settings/cases/[id]/reply`)

Title "Reply to korbat". One of three openings, then the form.

- [reply.before] "korbat has decided to suspend your account from {startsAt}. If you reply before then, the suspension does not start until a moderator has read your reply and answered you. The answer reverses the suspension, or keeps it; a kept suspension starts with the answer, and never before {startsAt}, so replying early costs you nothing."
- [reply.unfixed] the same with "not before {earliestStart}" in place of "from {startsAt}", "before it starts" in place of "before then", and "never before {earliestStart}" in place of "never before {startsAt}".
- [reply.running] "Your account is suspended until {endsAt}. A moderator reads your reply and answers you, and can end the suspension at once. The suspension goes on meanwhile."
- The field "Your reply (optional)", at most 2000 characters, with a count. [reply.hint] "Say anything you want the moderator to weigh: what happened, what the decision gets wrong, what you will do differently. You do not need to cite rules. You can send it empty: that still asks for a second look."
- [reply.once] "You can reply once to this decision. Replying does not use up your appeal."
- [reply.kept] "A moderator reads your reply. korbat keeps its text until it has answered you and this decision can no longer be appealed, then erases it."
- The button "Send reply". Then, from the answer's `onTime`: [reply.sent.onTime] "korbat received your reply on {filedOn}, before the start. The suspension is held until korbat answers." or [reply.sent.late] "korbat received your reply on {filedOn}. The suspension goes on until korbat answers."

For a termination the three openings read "terminate your account" and "terminated", [reply.running] reads "Your account is terminated. A moderator reads your reply and answers you, and can end the termination at once. Nothing is deleted before your reply is answered.", and [reply.sent.late] ends "The termination stays until korbat answers, and nothing is deleted before that."

### 9.4 Cases, chips and refusals

- **Cases** (`/settings/cases`, "About you"). Above D 9's list, the block "Your standing": [cases.strikes] "Active strikes: {n} of {ladderStrikes}. At {ladderStrikes}, a moderator may announce a suspension; you would be told first and could reply." (or [cases.none] "You have no active strikes."), then a line for a measure that is announced, held or running, in the banner's words, and [cases.reporting] "Your reports under korbat's rules are restricted until {until}." (from `/me`'s `standing.reporting.restriction.until`) or "A warning about your reports counts until {countsUntil}." (from `standing.reporting.warning.countsUntil`; section 10 gives `reporting` the two members `warning` and `restriction`, each null when none stands). Each row of the list names what was done from `CASE_ROW_WORDS`: "a strike" (after the post measure, with "counts until {endsAt}", "no longer counts" or "overruled"), "a suspension of {days} days, announced" (or "at once"), "a termination", "your handle retired", "a warning about your reports", "your reports under the rules restricted for {days} days", "strike overruled", "suspension lifted", "suspension withdrawn for another measure", "handle given back", "warning or restriction lifted", and, on a decision whose reversal broke the basis of another measure, "a suspension that rested on it ended" (or "a restriction of your reports that rested on it ended").
- **Your reports** (`/settings/cases`, "Your reports", D 9). On the line of an item that a moderator marked, once its outcome is released: [reports.marked] "A moderator marked this report as manifestly unfounded (Terms, Reporting in bad faith). You can ask for a re-examination." with D 9's "Ask for a re-examination" while it is open. The sentence is shown while the item's `unfounded` is true (section 10): once the mark was withdrawn by a correction, or a re-examination ended in a restriction, it no longer counts and the line loses the sentence. The line names nobody and tells the person nothing about the author beyond the outcome they already read. A mark is shown with its outcome and not earlier, so it tells the person nothing before the release does.
- **Chip.** `StateChip` adds the strike to a removed post's chip while it stands and counts: "Removed: R3, strike until {day}" (from `PostView.moderation.strikeUntil`).
- **Refused writes.** On 403 `account_suspended` the app reads `/me` and shows [refused.suspended] "Your account is suspended until {endsAt}, so you cannot do this now." (for a termination [refused.terminated] "korbat has terminated your account, so you cannot do this.") with the link "Read the decision"; when `/me` then shows no running measure, [refused.over] "The suspension has just ended. Try again." While `/me` shows a running measure, `ComposeSheet` and the forms of Settings, Profile render that sentence in place of their fields; the actions on a card show it on refusal, where `PostCard` shows its other errors.

### 9.5 Settings, Profile: the handle

- While the profile holds a placeholder: [handle.retired] "korbat retired your handle; the decision is under Settings, Cases. Choose a new one: the 30-day wait does not apply this time." The form is open at once (`handleChangedAt` is null). While `/me` carries a running measure the sentence is shown without "Choose a new one", as [handle.retired.suspended] "korbat retired your handle; the decision is under Settings, Cases. You can choose a new one when your suspension ends: the 30-day wait will not apply.", and the form is replaced by [refused.suspended] as 9.4 says (for a termination, by [refused.terminated] alone: a terminated account never chooses one).
- The standing sentence (`apps/app/src/app/settings/profile.tsx:143`) becomes "Your handle is @{current}. You can change it once every 30 days. Handles you used before stay yours, except one that korbat retired."
- `handle_retired`: "That handle was retired by korbat. Nobody can use it." `handle_reserved` keeps its words and now also answers a handle that begins with `retired_`.

### 9.6 The report form

Read from `/me` before anything is sent, and worded the same on a refusal:

- Suspended: the first choice, "It breaks a korbat rule", is disabled under [report.suspended] "Your account is suspended until {endsAt}, so it cannot report under korbat's rules. You can still report illegal content: choose "It is illegal". For the sexual abuse or exploitation of a child, choose the third answer."
- Reporting restricted (`reporting_restricted`; `/me`'s `standing.reporting.restriction` is not null, and `{until}` is its `until`): [report.restricted] "korbat has restricted your reports under its rules until {until}, after a warning, because several of them were plainly unfounded. The decision is under Settings, Cases, where you can appeal it. You can still report illegal content: choose "It is illegal". For the sexual abuse or exploitation of a child, choose the third answer."

### 9.7 Explore

`exploreStatusText` and `exploreRules` (`apps/app/src/lib/explore.ts`) take `EXPLORE_V2`:

- Status `suspended`: "Not in Explore: your account is suspended, and posts of suspended accounts do not appear there. A post still in its window when the suspension ends can appear from the next refresh."
- Status `outside_window`: "Not in Explore: posts can appear only in their first {windowHours} hours, not counting time in which korbat's moderation kept them from being shown."
- "What is ranked" reads "... by accounts that have not switched Explore off and are not suspended when the ranking is computed."
- "What counts" gains, after "its account still exists", ", is not suspended when the ranking is computed".
- "The score" reads "the hours it has been shown since it was posted" in place of "the hours since it was posted", and gains "A post that korbat's moderation hid and then restored gets that time back: its window and its age stand still while it is not shown."
- "What else takes a post out" reads: "Besides the formula, only these take a post out of Explore: its moderation state, set by a moderator for every list at once; a suspension of its author, which a moderator decides with a statement of reasons and which stops everything the account writes, never Explore alone; its author's switch or deletion; a block between you and the author in either direction; your mute. No moderator or automatic lever acts on Explore alone." (The automatic R1 and R2 checks left the sentence: rulebook version 2 has none.)
- The change log's entry: `{ version: 2, date: <the day the task that builds formula version 2 reaches the real repository, 10.1>, summary: "Posts of suspended accounts are not ranked, and a suspended account's likes and reposts do not count. A post that moderation hid and then restored gets back the time it was hidden." }`.

### 9.8 Errors and accessibility

The app's message table (`apps/app/src/lib/api.ts:44`) gains `account_suspended` ("Your account is suspended, so you cannot do this now."; the fallback when `/me` cannot be read), `reporting_restricted` ([report.restricted] without its date), `reply_not_open` ("You cannot reply to this decision: you already replied, or it has ended or was lifted."), `handle_retired` (9.5).

Accessibility, as the existing screens have it (`apps/app/src/components/ui.tsx`):

- The banner is a `role="region"` labelled "Your account's standing"; its first sentence is a level-2 header; amber (announced, held) and red (running) repeat what the words say and are never the only signal.
- Every time is text. Buttons are `Button` or `TextLink` (`accessibilityRole="button"`, `aria-busy` while sending); a disabled choice keeps its reason as text beside it.
- A refusal is an `ErrorText` (`accessibilityRole="alert"`); the sentences after "Send reply" are a `Notice` that receives focus.
- The reply field has a visible label and announces its count as "{n} of 2000 characters".

## 10. Contracts

`packages/contracts/src/moderation.ts`, `staff.ts`, `explore.ts`, `auth.ts`, `social.ts` and `account.ts`, and `packages/rules`. As in D 10, each table is the one source of its words and numbers, and a test pins each. A member that ends in `At` is an instant (ISO, UTC); one that ends in `On` is a UTC day. Sections 8 and 9 print them under the placeholders they name.

### 10.1 Constants

**`STANDING_RULES`** mirrors the SQL function `standing_rules()` (section 6), and a database test compares the two member by member. Every number is a cautious default without a source of its own, flagged for the owner (section 2); each is one line to change in each place.

| Member | Value | Used by |
|---|---|---|
| `strikeDays` | 90 | a strike's time of counting (7.4) |
| `ladderStrikes` | 3 | active strikes that open the ladder (7.5) |
| `ladderDays` | 3, 7, 30 | the most days of the first, second and third ladder suspension (7.6) |
| `ladderMemoryDays` | 365 | how long a suspension counts toward the next rung |
| `replyWindowHours` | 168 | the reply window, from the moment korbat's own relay accepted the notice mail and never less from the decision (7.6) |
| `undeliveredHours` | 336 | the window, from the decision, when the relay refused the address or the mail was given up |
| `threatDays`, `terroristDays`, `floodDays`, `protectiveDays` | 30, 30, 7, 14 | the most days of a suspension at once (7.7) |
| `floodPosts`, `floodMinutes` | 20, 60 | the count without which no flood is found; a condition, never the finding (7.7) |
| `basisAgeDays` | 30 | the most age of the decision a `threat` or `terrorist` suspension rests on, and of the seal for good a `protective` one rests on |
| `reportFrameDays` | 90 | the frame of the reporting counts, by the day a report was sent, and a warning's time of counting (7.9) |
| `reportWarnMarks`, `reportWarnShare` | 5, 0.5 | the warning's threshold, in marked reports and as their share of the rule reports sent |
| `reportRestrictMarks`, `reportRestrictDays` | 3, 30 | the restriction's threshold, in marked reports sent after the warning, and its most days |

No number is added for the protective suspension: one for one seal for good (7.7 step 8) needs none.

**The ledger's kinds.** `ACTION_KINDS` gains ten values in the order of the database's enum: `strike`, `suspend`, `terminate`, `retire_handle`, `warn_reporting`, `restrict_reporting`, then `overrule`, `lift_suspension`, `unretire_handle`, `lift_reporting`. `MEASURE_KINDS` then holds 13 and `REVERSAL_KINDS` 9. `MeasureTarget` gains `account`, `handle` and `reporting`. The labels are section 18.1.

| `MEASURES` entry | `interim` | `final` | `lifts` | `target` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `strike`, `suspend`, `terminate` | false | true | null | `account` |
| `retire_handle` | false | true | null | `handle` |
| `warn_reporting`, `restrict_reporting` | false | true | null | `reporting` |
| `overrule` | false | false | `strike` | `account` |
| `lift_suspension` | false | false | `suspend`, `terminate` | `account` |
| `unretire_handle` | false | false | `retire_handle` | `handle` |
| `lift_reporting` | false | false | `warn_reporting`, `restrict_reporting` | `reporting` |

**Others.**

- `STANDING_BASES`: `strikes`, `threat`, `terrorist`, `flood`, `protective`, `threat_repeat`, `ladder_end`, `reports`; the migration's CHECK list is written from it and tested against it as D 10's lists are.
- `STANDING_DRAFTS`: the composer's drafts of the facts (section 18.5).
- `REPLY_TEXT_MAX` 2000, the bound of an appeal's statement; `UNFOUNDED_MAX` 100, the most marks one decision signs (a default, flagged with the other numbers); a decision's actions stay "at most 8"; `days` is 1 to 30.
- `ACCOUNT_MEASURE_SCOPE` (`moderation.ts`, beside `ACCOUNT_ITEM_SCOPE`, `:500`): the scope phrase of a reporter's line for an item of an account case whose answering decision holds an account measure of 3e, used in place of `ACCOUNT_ITEM_SCOPE` (7.11 item 7). Two phrases: `retired`, "korbat retired the handle.", whenever that decision holds a `retire_handle`; `measure`, "korbat took a measure on the account. It does not report here which, or for how long.", otherwise. Neither names a suspension, an access or a duration, and a table test holds both to that.
- Two numbers the terms print that are no members of `STANDING_RULES`, each a constant of `moderation.ts` that the code which acts on it reads, so that the terms' table test can hold section 18.7 to them: 3c's `RETENTION.mailGiveUpDays` (5, `moderation.ts:171`), the days after which the dispatch job gives up a statement mail it could not send (7.14 item 3), unchanged; and the new `REPLY_AIM_HOURS` 24, the hours after which an unanswered reply joins the queue-age alert, and with them korbat's published aim for answering a reply to a suspension taken at once (7.7 step 6, 7.14).
- `RETIRED_HANDLE_PREFIX` `retired_`. A placeholder is the prefix and 12 lower-case hex digits: 20 characters, a valid handle (`^[a-z0-9_]{3,20}$`), so every reader of a handle works unchanged.
- `RETENTION` gains `replyText`, `reportCountDays` and `standingRecord` (section 6, retention; section 18.7).
