- `EXPLORE_V2` (`explore.ts`): `EXPLORE_V1`'s numbers, `version: 2`, and `changes` with both entries; the second is dated the day the task that builds formula version 2 (section 13) reaches the real repository and worded in 9.7. `EXPLORE_V1` is no longer exported. `ExploreStatusKind` gains `suspended` after `not_visible`.

### 10.2 App requests and answers

- **`ReplyRequest`** `{ statement? }`, strict; the text under `text.ts`'s rules without a transform, at most `REPLY_TEXT_MAX`. **`ReplyResponse`** `{ filedAt, onTime }`.
- **`MeResponse.standing`**: `AccountStanding`; null for an account without a profile, and in an app built without the moderation module (7.12). An onboarded account without any measure gets the object: no strike, `measure` null, `reporting` null, `handleRetired` false.

| Member | Holds |
|---|---|
| `activeStrikes` | the count of `active_strikes` |
| `measure` | null, or the account's one row of `standing_live` (6.2): `decisionId`, `kind` (`suspend` or `terminate`), `days` (null for a termination), `state`, `earliestStart`, `startsAt`, `endsAt` (each null while not fixed; `endsAt` null for a termination), `reply` (`{ filedAt, onTime, answeredAt }` or null; `answeredAt` null while it waits, also for a late reply), `replyOpen`, `appealUntil` (null once the appeal is closed; after a `kept` answer to an on-time reply the later date of 7.6 step 11) and `appealPending` |
| `measure.state` | `unfixed` (no start: the mail's outcome is not known), `announced` (the start is fixed and ahead), `held` (an on-time reply is unanswered), `running` |
| `reporting` | null when neither stands, or `{ warning, restriction }`: `warning` is null or `{ decisionId, countsUntil }` while a warning of the account still counts; `restriction` is null or `{ decisionId, until }` while `reports_restricted_until` is ahead. The refusal's sentence and the report form read `restriction` |
| `handleRetired` | true while the profile holds a placeholder |

- **`DecisionStatement`** (`moderation.ts:1186`), the statement's data, gains only what the ledger fixes for good, so a statement still reads as it was sent:
  - on a measure: `days`, `announced`, `basis`, `earliestStart` (the row's `starts_at`), `endsAt` (a strike's and a warning's end of counting; the end of a measure taken at once), `restsOn` (one entry a decision in `rests_on`: `decidedAt`, `ruleId`, `postCreatedAt`; for `protective` the one entry is the sealing decision, with its `decidedAt` and the two others null; for `threat_repeat` both R5 decisions, the earlier first, so that the statement's `{earlierDecidedAt}` is the first entry's `decidedAt` and needs no member of its own, section 18.2), `activeThen` (on a `strike`: the account's strikes that stood and counted at the decision's time, this one included), `handle` and `placeholder` (on a `retire_handle`), `warnedAt` (on a `restrict_reporting`);
  - `periodThen`: the period of a `suspend` or `terminate` as it was at this decision's time, `{ state, startedAt, endedAt }` with `state` one of `not_started`, `running` (with `startedAt`) and `ended` (with `startedAt` and `endedAt`); a member it does not need is null. A boolean "started" would have no form for a suspension that had already ended, which is the common case of a strike overruled on appeal;
  - on a reversal: the lifted measure's `kind`, its `decidedAt`, its `periodThen` (on a `lift_suspension`), `handle`, and `replaced` (on a `lift_suspension`: true when the correction withdrew an announced measure to make room, 7.6 step 12);
  - `endedStanding`: the measures whose basis a reversal of this decision broke, each with `kind` (`suspend`, `terminate` or `restrict_reporting`), `decidedAt`, `periodThen` (for a `restrict_reporting` too: `running`, or `ended` when its days had already run out), `seal` (true when the basis that broke was a seal: the measure's basis is `protective` and this decision lifts that seal) and `replyFiledAt` (the time of the measure's reply when this decision found it unanswered and so answers it, 7.6 step 14; else null);
  - `repliedTo`, on a correction or review of the decision that holds the measure, when it kept or reversed that measure while a reply was unanswered (never with `replaced`, never for a broken basis): `filedAt`, `onTime`, the measure's `kind` and `days`, its `periodThen`, `startsAt` and `endsAt` (the start and the end as this answer fixes or leaves them: after a `kept` answer to an on-time reply the start is the later of the answer and `earliestStart`; `endsAt` is null for a termination and both are null when the measure never starts) and `appealUntil` (the last day of the announcing decision's appeal as it stands after this answer, 7.6 step 11).
- **`DecisionView`** (the author's screen, D 10) carries the statement and, read fresh:
  - `standing`, for a decision that holds a `suspend` or `terminate`: `state` (`AccountStanding`'s four, and `ended`, `never`, `lifted`, `basis_ended`), `earliestStart`, `startsAt`, `endsAt`, `endedAt` and `endedBy` (the decision that lifted it, withdrew it with `replaced` or broke its basis), `replaced` (true when the lift that ended it was signed `replaced`, so that 9.2 words a withdrawal as one and not as a reversal), `restsOn` (each decision it rests on with `decisionId`, `ruleId` and `decidedAt`, and for a strike `countsUntil`, `state` and `overruledAt`), `reply` (`filedAt`, `onTime`, `text` or `erasedAt`, `answeredAt`, `answeredBy`) and `replyOpen`. Its `appealUntil` (D 10) is the later date of 7.6 step 11 once a `kept` answer to an on-time reply started the measure;
  - `reportingStanding`, the same for a decision that holds a `restrict_reporting` (a member of its own, since one decision may hold a suspension at once and a restriction): `state` (`running`, `ended`, `lifted`, `basis_ended`: it ended with the warning it rested on), `until` (the end as decided), `endedAt` and `endedBy` (the decision that lifted it, or that lifted its warning); 9.2 prints its four sentences from these;
  - `strike`, for a decision that holds one: `countsUntil`, `state` (`counts`, `expired`, `overruled`) and `overruledAt`.
- **`AuthorCasesPage`** rows name the new kinds from `MEASURES`, a strike with `countsUntil` and `overruledAt`, a `lift_suspension` with `replaced`, and the kinds of the decision's `endedStanding` entries, which 9.4's row words read. **`PostView.moderation`** gains `strikeUntil` (an instant or null). **`ExportFormat.moderation`** gains the members of section 18.8.
- **`ReportsPage`** (`moderation.ts:1326`): a `ReportRow`'s items are `NoticeItemStatus` extended by `unfounded`, true while a mark on the item counts (7.9 step 3) and only once the item's outcome is released, so the mark tells the reporter nothing before the release does. `NoticeStatus`, the status page's answer by reference and token, is unchanged. The member tells the reporter of their own report only, and nothing about the reported account.
- **`NoticeRequest`** and every other request are unchanged.

### 10.3 Staff answers

No member is named like `email`, `address`, `userId`, `accountId` or `actorId` (the route-table test's `PERSONAL` runs over these too). People are handles. Every "may" below is an aid to the console, read from the same views the triggers read; the database decides at commit.

| Answer | Gains |
|---|---|
| `CaseView` (post case) | `strikeDue`: `{ due, why }`, `why` one of `due`, `already_struck`, `before_last_strike`. `authorStanding`, in bands: `activeStrikes` (`0`, `1`, `2`, `3_or_more`), `suspensionsInYear` (`0`, `1`, `2_or_more`), `now` (`none`, `announced`, `suspended`, `terminated`). |
| `CaseView` (account case) | `standing`: `activeStrikes`; `strikes` (each `decisionId`, `caseId`, `ruleId`, `decidedAt`, `countsUntil`); `ladderRule`; `suspensionsInYear` and `lastSuspendedOn`; `maxDays` (null when no suspension can be announced); `mayTerminate`; `live`; `openReports` (the case holds an unanswered item of a `user` intake, so a ladder, protective or reporting measure is refused until those are decided, 7.5 step 2); `atOnce` (which of `threat`, `terrorist`, `flood`, `protective`, `threat_repeat` the database would accept now; `protective` only with a seal for good that none has rested on, 7.7 step 8); `seal`; `flood`: `{ posts, from, to }`, the subject's posts created in the last `floodMinutes` and the interval they were counted in, for the facts of a flood (7.7 step 5); `reporting`: `reports`, `unfounded` (the marked reports among them), `warnedOn`, `marksSince` (the marked reports sent since the warning), `mayWarn`, `mayRestrict`, `restrictedUntil`, and `marked` (the counting marks as `ref`, `position`, `sentOn`, the day their report was sent, and `markedOn`, for the draft of W.5). |
| `CaseItem` | `markable`; `unfounded` (the answering decision marked it and the mark is not withdrawn); `markWithdrawn` (a correction withdrew its mark, 7.9 step 9). |
| `CaseDecisionAction` | `days`, `announced`, `basis`, `restsOn` (decision ids), `earliestStart`, `countsUntil`; `replaced` (on a `lift_suspension`); `period` (`startsAt`, `endsAt`, `state` as `DecisionView.standing`); `reply` (`filedAt`, `onTime`, `text`, `answeredAt`, `answeredBy`). |
| `QueuePage` | `replies`, at most `QUEUE_PAGE_SIZE`: `caseId`, `decisionId`, `kind`, `days`, `filedAt`, `onTime`, `endsAt`. No text. |
| `StandingLists` (new) | Four lists, each `{ total, items }` with at most `OVERVIEW_LIST_MAX` rows. `atThreeStrikes`: `handle`, `activeStrikes`, `ruleId`, `newestOn`, `maxDays`, `mayTerminate`. `reportingRecords`: `handle`, `reports`, `unfounded`, `warnedOn`, `marksSince` (all in reports, as 7.9 counts them). `noticesUndelivered`: `handle`, `caseId`, `decidedOn`, `startsAt`; it holds the measures of live accounts whose mail is `failed`, and no `no_address` row (7.6 step 6). `terminationsWaiting`: `handle`, `caseId`, `decidedOn`, `finalOn`, `heldBy` (`appeal`, `reply` or null) and `replyOnTime` (null unless a reply holds it: true when the termination has not started, false when a late reply holds only the deletion). |
| `AppealsPage` | each row `standingFirst`: the appealed decision holds a running suspension or a termination that stands. |
| `StaffOverview` | `repliesWaiting` (`{ count, oldestAt }`), `strikeAccounts` (a count). |

Three members of the account case's `standing` that the table only names:

- `live`: a suspension or termination of the account stands and has not ended, one with no start yet included (the row `standing_conflict` would meet).
- `lastSuspendedOn`: the decision day of the newest of the suspensions counted in `suspensionsInYear`, null when there is none; the draft `facts.ladder_end` prints it as `{day}`.
- `seal`: null, or `{ decidedOn }`, the day of the seal for good that a protective suspension would rest on now (the newest that fits 7.7's `protective` row); 8.3 [atOnce.protective] and the draft `facts.protective` print it.

`noticesUndelivered` cannot tell a refused address from a mail given up after five days: both are recorded `failed` (`repo.ts:1605`), so a row carries no reason. The list's name stays the model's; the console's words for it say "could not be sent", never "not delivered" (section 8).

### 10.4 The step-up's params

No step-up action is added: every new act is a `case_decided` or an `appeals_reviewed`. `STAFF_STEP_UP_ACTIONS` keeps its four keys and `append_confirmed_audit` its list, which 3f extends.

- **An action entry** is one of two strict shapes: `{ kind, mediaId }` as today for every kind but three, and `{ kind, mediaId: null, days, announced }` for `suspend`, `terminate` and `restrict_reporting` (`days` a whole number from 1 to 30 or null, `announced` a boolean). So the key signs the days and whether the measure is announced, and `decision_holds` compares both with the ledger.
- **`CaseDecidedParams`** gains two members, each always present, and its `outcome` gains `kept`:
  - `unfounded`: lower-case uuids, sorted, without repeats, at most `UNFOUNDED_MAX`; `[]` when none. The items this decision marks (7.9 step 2). The withdrawal of marks has no member: a `kept` correction of the decision that set them withdraws them all together (7.9 step 9).
  - `replaced`: a boolean. True when this correction withdraws an announced measure that has not started, to make room for a measure at once (7.6 step 12). The database stores it on the `lift_suspension` row, and `decision_holds` compares both with what was stored.
- **Refinements** (zod, no transform; the triggers check each again):
  - `terminate` has `days` null; `suspend` and `restrict_reporting` have a number; `restrict_reporting` has `announced` false;
  - at most one of `suspend` and `terminate` in a decision, and at most one of each other new kind;
  - the six measures only with `kind: "final"` and `outcome: "restricted"`; `strike` only with ground `incompatible` and a rule other than R2;
  - a correction's outcome is `kept`, `partly_reversed` or `reversed`; `kept` holds no action;
  - `overrule` only with the outcome `reversed`, in a correction and in a review alike;
  - `unfounded` is empty unless the decision is `final` with the outcome `no_violation`;
  - a `kept` correction carries nothing that says which of its two uses it is: the database decides it from the corrected decision (a reply answered, 7.6 step 11; all marks of a final `no_violation` decision withdrawn, 7.9 step 9) and refuses one that is neither;
  - `replaced` is false unless the decision is a correction that holds `lift_suspension`.
- **`AppealsReviewedParams`** keeps its shape; its actions may be the four new reversals. A review withdraws no mark and replaces no measure.
- **Order.** `compareDecisionActions` is unchanged (kind, then image), so one decision has one hash. The decide route alone moves a `retire_handle` to the front when it inserts (7.10).
- **Rows made before 3e** carry neither of the two members. `audit-verify` recomputes the action's hash from the params as stored and parses them with no schema (`cli/staff.ts:351-361`); `text-verify` (D 7.13) and `decision_holds` read a missing list as empty and a missing `replaced` as false.
- **The routes' list of kinds** (7.1) is no part of the schema: the schema knows all 22 kinds and both action shapes from the first task, and each task opens its kinds in the list.
- The schema test of D 10 (C7) still holds: the new members are uuids, whole numbers and booleans, never a free string.

### 10.5 Error codes

| Code | Status | Raised by | Answered on |
|---|---|---|---|
| `account_suspended` | 403 | the predicate (7.2) | every refused route of 7.3 |
| `reporting_restricted` | 403 | the intake (7.9 step 7) | `POST /api/notices`, ground `rule` |
| `reply_not_open` | 422 | `suspension_replies_check`; the table's primary key | the reply route |
| `strike_mismatch` | 409 | `decision_holds` | the decide and review routes |
| `standing_conflict` | 409 | `decision_actions_check` | the decide route |
| `ground_not_established` | 422 | `decision_actions_check` | the decide route |
| `handle_retired` | 409 | the claim statement; `profiles_handle_guard` | `POST /api/me/handle`, `POST /api/auth/onboarding/handle` |
| `handle_reserved` (exists, 400) | | now also the prefix `retired_`; `profiles_handle_guard` | the same two routes |

- `ModerationErrorCode` gains `reporting_restricted`, `reply_not_open`, `strike_mismatch`, `standing_conflict` and `ground_not_established`. `AuthErrorCode` gains `handle_retired`, which the social module's `HandleErrorCode` also names. `account_suspended` joins `SocialErrorCode`, and the media, auth and moderation modules name it among their shared codes, as they name `not_found`.
- 3c's codes gain causes, not meanings: `invalid_decision` (a basis, a number of days, a mark or a withdrawal, a kind out of place or not yet in the routes' list, a `replaced` out of place, a protective suspension without its seal or in a decision that seals, and reports still open in the case of a ladder, protective or reporting measure: the trigger's detail `open_reports`), `admin_required` (a measure at once, a termination), `staff_target`, `profile_changed` (a handle that is not the account's to retire), `case_changed` (a correction drafted before a reply).
- Every refusal's message is one sentence; the app's words are in 9.8 and the console's in 8.9. No answer carries a detail: a trigger's detail is logged, as 3c's are, the console reads `standing.openReports` and its other aids from the case read, and the app reads `/api/me` for dates and the decision's id.
- The console's code table is typed to `ModerationErrorCode` (`apps/admin/src/messages.ts:28`) and gains the five codes above that are one. `handle_retired`, `handle_reserved` and `account_suspended` belong to the auth and social modules, no staff route answers them, and the console needs no words for them.
- Database only, answered by no route: `42501` from `decisions_mail_forward` and from every revoked grant.

### 10.6 The rulebook, the redress texts and the statement

`packages/rules/src/index.ts`:

- `RULES_VERSION` becomes 3 and `RULEBOOKS[3]` is version 2 with one change to R6 (section 18.4). Versions 1 and 2 stay, so an earlier decision renders its rule as it was worded.
- `REDRESS_VERSION` becomes 2. `REDRESS_TEXTS[2]` holds version 1's three texts word for word and six more: `strikeNext`, `reply`, `replyAtOnce`, `replyAnswered`, `warnNext` and `appealNow` (section 18.3). Their numbers are literals of the version, held to `STANDING_RULES` by a table test, so a changed number is a new version and an old statement keeps its own.
- `statementParagraphs` (`statement.ts`) stays a pure function of `DecisionStatement`. It picks the redress texts from the decision's own kind, outcome and measures (the table of W.3) and renders the sentences of section 18.2 from the members of 10.2. It reads no later event: what changes afterwards (the exact start, a reply, a lift) is `DecisionView.standing`, shown beside the statement and never inside it.
- **The rule or the term.** D's paragraph of the rule or the law ("It breaks korbat rule ...", `packages/rules/src/statement.ts:154`) stays for every decision that holds no standing measure. For one that does, `statementParagraphs` chooses the paragraph by the measure's `basis` (the sentences are section 18.2's):
  - `strikes`, `ladder_end`: the ground is korbat's terms, section "Suspension and termination" (three active strikes may lead to a suspension; three suspensions in 365 days to a termination; the numbers are literals of the redress version), followed by the rule of the newest strike, named as that. The decision finds no new breach and its statement claims none.
  - `protective`: the ground is the terms' clause on protective suspensions, with its explanation, and the sentence that this is no finding that the person broke the law or a rule. The paragraph never renders D's sentence for R2.
  - `threat`, `terrorist`, `threat_repeat`, `flood`, `reports`: D's sentence for the rule or the law, with "Your account's conduct breaks" in place of "It breaks", followed for the first three by the `basis` sentence that names the decision which found it.
  - A table test (section 12) renders a statement for every basis and holds that none of a `protective` decision contains "It breaks", and that a ladder statement names the terms' section.

## 11. Security and privacy

D 11 stands whole. This section adds what 3e makes possible to get wrong: what stays impossible even for an admin, in the API and the console, with the mechanism of sections 6 and 7 that enforces it and the test that proves it (section 12 lists the tests by task).

### 11.1 What stays impossible, and what proves it

| Impossible | Enforced by | Proved by |
|---|---|---|
| Give a strike, suspend, terminate, retire a handle, warn or restrict reporting without a fresh assertion of one's own key over exactly that measure, its days and whether it is announced | each is a ledger row of a `case_decided` decision; `decision_holds` compares kinds, `days`, `announced`, `replaced` and `unfounded` with the signed params at commit, and again after a later insert of a mark (`unfounded_marks_recheck`, 7.1) | a decision whose stored days, `announced`, `replaced` or marks differ from the signed ones is refused at COMMIT; a mark added after a check that was made to run early is refused at COMMIT; `audit-verify` as D 11 |
| Give or withhold a strike by choice | `strike_due`, asked again at commit (`strike_mismatch`), under the subject's profile lock, which `decision_actions_check` takes for a strike itself (7.4) | a function test for each condition, and both mismatches; two decisions on two posts of one author in flight together give one strike |
| Write the suspended state, its start, its end or the reporting restriction from the API | column grants on `profiles`; `profiles_standing_insert`; `standing_apply` the one writer, with EXECUTE to nobody; `basis`, `rests_on`, `starts_at`, `ends_at` forced | as `korbat_api`: an UPDATE of each column and an INSERT with one set refused; the function test of 6.6 pins the one writer |
| Start a signed suspension earlier than signed or than announced, lengthen it, or move a recorded notice | `starts_at` forced; no start is before it, the start after a reply's `kept` answer included (`greatest(starts_at, answered_at)`, 7.6 step 11); the end is the start plus the signed days; `decisions_mail_forward` | a second change of `author_mail` refused (`42501`); the start under each mail outcome; an on-time reply answered `kept` before `starts_at` starts at `starts_at`; a give-up recorded after the 336 hours writes no start in the past |
| Suspend at once or terminate as a moderator; suspend at once outside the four grounds; exceed a rung or a ground's days; get a ground's days for an announced suspension; hold two standing measures | `decision_actions_check` (7.6 step 3, 7.7, 7.8), `needs_admin` | one function test a refusal, by its code; three strikes, the newest under R5 and a week old: announced, basis `strikes`, more than the rung refused |
| Suspend protectively without a seal for good in force, twice on one seal, or in the decision that seals; let a protective suspension outlive its seal | the `protective` row and `rests_on` (7.7 steps 2 and 8); the basis holds only while the seal is in force | function tests: no seal, an interim seal only, a seal decided more than 30 days before, a second suspension on the same seal and a `suspend` beside `seal_media` are each refused; lifting the seal ends the suspension in that transaction |
| Terminate at once for a "second" threat when the first was overruled, or for a post made before the person was told of the first suspension | the `threat_repeat` row (7.8 step 1); its basis holds only while both R5 decisions hold a measure | function tests: the earlier suspension's basis broken, and a post created before that suspension's `informed_at`, each refused (so is a second R5 decision on day 2 of that suspension: `standing_conflict`, and its post is from before the suspension); a post made after that suspension ended accepted; a later reversal of either R5 decision ends the termination |
| Terminate on images korbat has not assessed | `ground_not_established` | a function test |
| Take a terminated account down before its decision is final, or while its appeal or a reply, on time or late, is unanswered; mail `termination_final` to an account that was not taken down, or twice; hold up a person's own deletion by a termination that fails | `terminations_due()`, read again under the profile lock and the decision's case lock; the mail only after the take-down committed; a try for each account and one around the step (7.8 step 3) | the purge round against a pending appeal, an appeal filed at the deadline, a held reply, a late reply and a lift, in both orders; a termination at once, final, with a late reply unanswered is not due, and is due after the `kept` answer; a take-down made to throw sends no mail and leaves an ordinary deletion of the same round purged |
| Act as a suspended account through a path nobody checked, or through a session opened before the suspension | the one predicate, in SQL at every write (7.2); nothing on the session | the route walk driven from 7.3's table, which fails for a route without a row; the source pin of section 12, which fails for a new use of the fragment or of the three columns outside its pinned files; the races of LAB B, C and D on the real code |
| Keep the console while suspended, or suspend a staff account | `staff_target`; `auth.account_standing` in `grant-role` (7.16) | each new measure on a staff account refused; `grant-role` against a deciding transaction in both orders |
| Restrict or count a notice of illegality or a report of child sexual abuse material | the intake reads the restriction on ground `rule` only; `report_tallies_count` and `unfounded_marks_check` leave out both | a suspended and a restricted account send both kinds; a mark on either is refused |
| Mark a report one's decision did not answer, mark after the fact, withdraw a mark without a key, or change a count | the signed `unfounded` list; a withdrawal only by a signed `kept` correction of the decision that set the marks, all of them together (7.9 step 9); `unfounded_marks_check` and the deferred `unfounded_marks_recheck`; `withdrawn_by` written by `decisions_apply` alone; no UPDATE or DELETE on the marks; no write at all on `report_tallies` | as `korbat_api`: each write refused; a mark inserted after `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE` is refused at COMMIT; a `kept` correction of a `no_violation` decision withdraws every mark of that decision and no other, and one of a decision that holds no mark left to withdraw is refused |
| Reach a warning or a restriction with one report; count a mark that was withdrawn; keep a restriction after its warning was lifted | `reporting_record` counts reports on both sides, by the day sent; a mark's counting is derived (7.9 step 3); the basis `reports` of a restriction holds only while its warning stands (7.9 step 8) | one report with ten marked items is one marked report and opens no warning; a report sent 95 days ago and marked yesterday counts on neither side; a withdrawn mark stops counting; after a warning is lifted `reports_restricted_until` is null in that transaction; once the decisions that set its marks are corrected the same warning is refused |
| Register a retired handle or a placeholder, or move a profile to one | `profiles_handle_guard`, for every role but the migrator | LAB E on the real code: no profile left holding a retired handle |
| Change or delete a reply, or choose whether it was on time; erase a reply nobody answered | insert-only grants; `filed_at` and `on_time` forced from `clock_timestamp()` after the profile lock; `erase_reply_statements` takes only answered replies (7.14) | a reply sent with either member set; LAB D; an unanswered reply filed after its decision was final keeps its text |
| Tell a notifier or reporter of a strike or of the author's standing, or let them follow it | `item_current_outcome` never reads a strike, a period, a reply or a basis; a ladder, protective or reporting measure answers findings only (`open_reports`, 7.5); `ACCOUNT_MEASURE_SCOPE` for the measures that may answer a report (7.11 item 7); the author's reads still never join the intake tables (D 11) | an item's line with and without a strike is the same (T43); a reporter's line for an item in an account case is the same before and after a ladder suspension of that account is announced, held, started, lifted and ended; a ladder decision on a case with an open report is refused; neither scope phrase names a suspension, an access or a duration |
| Act on Explore alone | no action kind for it; the Explore effect of a suspension is a published rule of version 2; `moderation_apply` alone writes `hidden_at`, `restored_at`, `explore_from` | as `korbat_api`: an UPDATE of each refused; `explore_from` over two hide and restore rounds equals the ledger's spans |
| Use standing as a queue key, a weight or a limit | no read of the queue or of a limit names a standing view (D 2, row Queue order and weight) | a source scan of the queue's query; the queue's order with and without strikes |

**In the database**, for `korbat_api`: UPDATE on `profiles` only on the eleven columns the API writes today; INSERT only on `handle_history`, `suspension_replies` and `unfounded_marks`; SELECT only on `report_tallies`; SELECT on the new views; EXECUTE only on the functions section 6 grants it, which the function-privilege test lists. `korbat_auth` gains EXECUTE on `auth.account_standing` and nothing else. For the migrator, the superuser and anyone with Docker on the host nothing is impossible; the ledger rows are held to their audit rows as D 11 says.

### 11.2 What the controls cannot prove

- **An API under an attacker's control** can record a decision with a confirmation it made up, a suspension included, which `audit-verify` detects afterwards and nothing prevents (D 11). It can delay an announced start by leaving the mail `pending` (five days at most, then 336 hours from the decision), and it can record `sent` for a mail it never sent, so that the start is the earliest one signed. It cannot start a suspension before `starts_at` or lengthen one.
- **"Sent" is korbat's relay taking the mail**, not the person's provider, and korbat reads no bounce. A notice that bounces later still starts the 168 hours, not the 336. So nothing a person reads claims more than korbat records: the statement, the mail and the terms say "sent", never "handed to your mail provider" or "delivered", say that korbat cannot see a later bounce, and say that the notice is always shown in the app. The banner on every screen is the second channel; whether that is delivery, and whether a bounced mail may keep the shorter window, is on counsel's list (section 2).
- **A write is judged when it gets the row, not when it commits.** LAB C1: two writes admitted before a start committed up to 7 ms after it.
- **An on-time reply holds a start without limit.** With one moderator who does not answer, a suspension never starts. That fails toward the person; the queue's first section, the home screen and the queue-age alert show it. The appeal does not run out behind the hold: after a `kept` answer its six months count from that answer (7.6 step 11), and a reply keeps its text until it is answered (7.14).
- **One moderator.** The reply may be answered by whoever decided; an appeal of a running suspension waits for a second person while it runs (D 11, T60). The appeals' order is all 3e adds. With one admin the appeal of a measure taken at once, a protective suspension included, has no reviewer: "reviewed at once" holds only from the second admin on.
- **Mass reporting (T1).** A strike comes only from a person's final decision on the content, and a suspension only from a moderator's own decision after reading the strikes, with notice and reply. What remains is D's residual: a patient group and a persuaded moderator.
- **The mark.** A moderator can mark honest reports "manifestly unfounded". The mark is signed; the reporter sees it on their Reports page and can ask for a re-examination; a correction can withdraw it; five marked reports that are half of the frame's reports are needed, and one report is never enough. Nothing checks one mark when it is made.
- **Signed out, the same person reports on.** The restriction refuses a signed-in sender on the ground `rule` only, and only a signed-in person's report can be counted or marked. Whoever reports signed out is never counted, never marked and never restricted, within the signed-out limits. So the measure reaches only people who report under their account, and the person it is taken against can go on at once. That is the price of never touching a notice from the public (section 2).
- **A protective suspension rests on images korbat has not assessed.** Its basis is a seal for good that an admin decided at most 30 days before; an admin decides it; it lasts 14 days at most, once for each seal; it gives no strike and supports no termination; its statement says it is no finding; its appeal is reviewed at once. A false report that an admin sealed for good can still cost an account 14 days, once for that seal. Nobody checks anything inside those days: LAW E2 answer 4 asks for a check that somebody carries out, and 3e has none before 3f's record of an authority's answer. Flagged for the owner and for counsel (section 2).
- **A suspended person's own bio.** `PATCH /api/me/profile` is refused whole, so a suspended or terminated person cannot blank a bio or a display name that holds their real name or a contact. Their ways to erase are deleting posts and images and deleting the account, which ends an appeal in practice (7.8 step 5). The refusal stays whole because a suspended account must not rename itself (T54). Flagged for the owner (section 2; GDPR Art. 17 through the profile).
- **Evasion and return.** A suspended or terminated person can open another account, and can delete an account during a reply window or before a termination is final. korbat keeps nothing to recognise them and holds no deletion (section 2, with its flags).
- **Explore.** A wrongful suspension costs Explore until it is reversed (T34). A ring's likes under 48 hours old count again when a short suspension ends (G10).
- **The derived columns.** `text-verify` recomputes levels and image states (D 7.13), not the three profile columns or `explore_from`: a change made around their triggers by the migrator is found only by recomputing them from the ledger by hand.
- **A route added later** fails the walk until it has a row in 7.3's table; a write added to an existing route without the predicate does not. What holds those is the source pin that section 12 defines in `apps/api/test/architecture.test.ts`, in the form of 3c's pins: `liveNotSuspended` is named in exactly a pinned list of files, each a pinned number of times, so whoever writes a new write site must add it to the list; and `suspended_from`, `suspended_until` and `reports_restricted_until` are named in `apps/api/src` only in the fragment's file, in `lockAccounts` and in the moderation module's standing repository. A write on behalf of an account that names neither still passes: that residual stays (T54).

### 11.3 What each party may learn

| Party | Learns | Never learns |
|---|---|---|
| The person | every measure with its statement; the strikes a suspension rests on; the exact start and end; their reply and its answer; on their Reports page, which of their own reports a moderator marked; in the export, their own report counts and marks | who reported them; how many reports there were about them; a staff handle |
| Other users | nothing: no label on the profile or its posts, and `account_suspended` is answered to the account alone. A retired handle shows as a placeholder, whose form tells anyone that korbat retired that account's handle; `handle_retired` tells whoever asks for a retired handle that it was retired, not whose it was | the measure, its reason, its dates |
| Notifiers and reporters | 3c's outcome lines, once. For an account item that a retirement or a measure taken at once answered: 3c's restricted outcome with a scope phrase that says a measure was taken, or that the handle was retired, and no more (7.11 item 7) | a strike, a count, the author's standing; a suspension, a termination or a reporting measure by its name; a start, a length or an end; a reply or its answer; a basis that broke or a seal that was lifted |
| Staff | on a post case the author's standing in bands; on an account case the exact standing; the four lists by handle; a reply's text on its case page, logged | a reporter's identity outside D's reveal, with one exception (below) |
| The mail provider | the subject line of a statement mail, as for 3c's | |

**The exception: marked reports.** On the account case of a reporting measure, staff see which reports of that account were marked (by reference and position), and the reporting records list accounts by handle with their counts. That is what the measure is about, and it concerns only reports a moderator marked with a key; no reveal is asked for it. `unfounded_marks` keeps the link between a marked item and its sender for the frame's 90 days from the day the report was sent, longer than the 30 days after which 3c forgets who sent a report; the terms say so (section 18.7). Flagged for the owner and for counsel with the measure's numbers (section 2).

**New personal data and its end.** A reply's text (erased once the reply is answered and its decision is final for its author; an unanswered reply keeps it); the marks and the daily tallies (deleted when their report's day leaves the 90-day frame, and with the account); three derived columns of the profile (gone with the purge); ledger rows as ids, enums, days and times, kept as every action is (section 6, retention; flagged for counsel there). The chain gains `days`, `announced`, `replaced` and the ids of items marked (a withdrawal signs no ids), never text: once the marks are deleted an item's id names no sender. No log line carries a reply's text or a refusal's reason beyond its code and the trigger's detail.

**A retired handle is kept.** `handle_history` must hold a retired handle to refuse it. One retired under R4 (a doxxed name) is therefore kept, as a handle and nothing else, beyond the finality after which 3c erases every other copy of R4 content (D 6.4); it also stays in the snapshot's handle columns, which an erasure does not null. 3c's redaction and its candidate rule keep their four kinds (7.14). Flagged for counsel (section 2).

### 11.4 The threats `inputs.md` names for 3e

| Threat | 3e's control | Residual |
|---|---|---|
| T1 mass reporting to get a target suspended | strikes and suspensions only by a person's signed decision; no count is a key; notice, reply, appeal; a protective suspension only on a seal for good, once for each seal | a patient group, a persuaded moderator; 14 days for a false report that an admin sealed for good |
| T34 suspension and Explore | formula version 2 judged at the round and at each read; the statement names the effect (C26) | a wrongful suspension until its reversal |
| T35 restored posts | the resume rule gives back the time lost and nothing more, written only by `moderation_apply` | none found |
| T36, T60 appeals, with one moderator | appeals of running suspensions and terminations first (C31); 3c's caps | the wait for a second person |
| T43 notices as an oracle | no strike and no standing in any line; ladder, protective and reporting measures answer findings only; nothing after the outcome reaches a line (7.11 item 7) | a reporter of an account whose report a retirement or a measure taken at once answered reads that a measure was taken, never which or for how long |
| T53 silent penalties through reputation | standing is never a key or a weight; the reporting counts feed one measure, which has a statement and an appeal; the reporter sees each mark and a correction can withdraw it | the unchecked single mark (11.2) |
| T54 enforcement gaps and the way back | the one predicate at every write, the route walk, the source pin, the column grants, the bridge to `grant-role`; every end written by the database or by a signed reversal (C6, C25) | a second account; a write added without the predicate that names neither the fragment nor the columns |

T20 (a restored backup), T50 (`reverse-window`) and the threats of the orders path are 3f's (section 16).

## 12. Testing

D 12's kinds and fixtures stay (`decideForTest`, the software authenticator, tests that write what `audit-verify` must refuse run in a transaction that rolls back), with one change that Task 1 makes in the same commit as the migration that makes a strike compulsory: `decideForTest` and `decisionParams` (`apps/api/test/integration/moderation-decide.ts`, `moderation-fixtures.ts`) sign the `strike` that the database computes for a rule removal of a post, and the `overrule` that a full reversal of such a decision needs, and every 3c test, console fixture and smoke step that decides a rule removal gains the same two. Without that, every final removal under a rule is refused `strike_mismatch`.

3e adds four fixtures, each built by the task that first needs it:

- `strikeForTest(account, n)` (Task 1): n real post decisions, each told before the next post is made, through the decide route's own code.
- `ageStandingForTest(account, interval)` (Task 1): as the migrator, moves the times of the account's ledger rows, replies, mails and marks back by the interval and calls `standing_apply`. It is the only way a test meets a start, an end, an expiry, the 90-day frame or finality after six months: the predicate reads `clock_timestamp()`, every time is forced by a trigger, and no configuration shortens a number of `standing_rules()`.
- `suspendForTest(account, { days, announced })` (Task 3 for `announced: false`; Task 4 adds `announced: true`), through the decide route's own code.
- `terminateForTest(account)` (Task 4): an R5 decision, a suspension at once on it, that suspension aged past its end, a post made after it and a second R5 decision on that post, then `terminate` at once; the only path to a termination at once (section 6).

`decideWith` and these fixtures pass the flag that lifts the routes' list of accepted kinds (7.1), so a database-level test can store a kind before its route opens. Tasks 1 and 4 extend `removeDecided`, `removeFixtures` (`moderation-fixtures.ts:636`, `:661`) and `scripts/clean-dev-fixtures.mjs` to delete the rows of `suspension_replies`, `unfounded_marks` and `report_tallies` first: a reply references its profile `on delete restrict`, so one left behind breaks the cleanup of every later file.

**How they run** (the lighter loop). An implementer runs the type check, the unit suites and the integration files its task names in section 13: its own new files and the existing files whose code it changes. The whole integration suite, on a fresh database (`node scripts/test-db.mjs --fresh`) and on one upgraded from 3c's last migration (`--upgrade-from` with that number, which runs 3e's migrations as one batch over a database committed at 3c's last), runs at the three batch ends: Tasks 1, 3 and 7. A task that adds a migration and is no batch end (Tasks 5 and 6) also runs its own integration files on an upgraded database, so a fault that shows only in the one-batch run does not wait for Task 7.

### 12.1 Each invariant and the test that proves it

| # | Invariant | Proved by | Task |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The migrations run in one transaction on a database committed at 3c's last migration and on a fresh one (M 3, the enum-literal rule) | the suite after `--upgrade-from` and after `--fresh`; a source test (`packages/db/test/enum-literals.test.ts`) that refuses an enum literal of a new kind outside a PL/pgSQL body in 3e's five migrations, with a deliberate violation it catches | 1, then 5, 6, 7 |
| 2 | Only `standing_apply` writes `profiles.suspended_from`, `suspended_until`, `reports_restricted_until` | the function pin of D 6.5 grown; `korbat_api`'s UPDATE of each refused (`42501`); an INSERT's values forced null | 1 |
| 3 | Only `moderation_apply` writes `posts.hidden_at`, `restored_at`, `explore_from` | the same three checks for `posts` | 6 |
| 4 | A decision holds a strike exactly when the database says one is due | `strike_due` over its table of cases (a post case and an account case; each ground; R2; a suspect item; a post already struck; a post made before and after `informed_at`; every other measure `no_effect`); `strike_mismatch` for a strike that is not due and for a missing one, at database level and through the decide route; `decideForTest` and the console's composer sign the strike the answer's `strikeDue` names | 1 |
| 5 | A strike falls only with its whole finding, and stops counting in that transaction | `overrule` refused in a `partly_reversed` review and in an interim or final decision; after a `reversed` one, `active_strikes` lacks it before the commit returns; a full reversal that lacks the `overrule` is refused | 1 |
| 6 | The ladder is the database's | `suspend` refused with fewer than three active strikes, with no strike newer than the last ladder measure, with days above the rung, with another rule than the newest strike's, and unannounced on basis `strikes`; `rests_on` forced whatever was supplied; three strikes of which the newest is under R5 and a week old: an announced `suspend` gets the basis `strikes`, and more than the rung is refused (the grounds at once never fit an announced measure); a ladder `suspend` or `terminate`, a protective `suspend`, a warning and a restriction of reporting refused (`invalid_decision`, detail `open_reports`) while their case holds an unanswered item of a `user` intake | 1 |
| 7 | A suspension ends by derivation when its basis breaks | overruling one of three strikes clears the columns in the overruling transaction and `standing_live` is empty; the same for a reversed R5 decision under a `threat` suspension and for a lifted seal under a protective one; `endedStanding` in that decision's statement with the state the period had then (not started, running, ended), each of the three rendered; an unanswered reply to the measure reads answered by that decision, and a `kept` correction of it is then refused | 1, 4 |
| 8 | One standing measure at a time | a second `suspend` or `terminate` refused (`standing_conflict`) while one stands and has not ended, one with no start yet included; accepted after a lift; a lift signed `replaced` accepted only for an announced measure that has not started, and refused for one that runs | 1, 4 |
| 9 | Taken at once only on the closed list, only by an admin | each basis accepted on its facts and refused without them: `threat` and `terrorist` (no such decision, one older than `basis_age_days`, one reversed); `flood` (19 posts in the hour); `protective` (no seal for good; only a seal taken while deciding; a seal for good older than `basis_age_days`; one that a protective suspension, lifted or not, already rested on; a `suspend` in the decision that itself holds `seal_media`), accepted once for a seal and again only for a newer one. `threat_repeat`: refused when the earlier threat suspension was lifted or its R5 decision reversed, and when the second decision's post was created before the person was told of that suspension (a second R5 decision on day 2 of a threat suspension, about a post from before it: refused); accepted for a post made after that suspension ended, with no `standing_conflict`, and ended by derivation when either R5 decision is reversed. `admin_required` for a moderator; `ground_not_established` for `terminate` under R2 | 1, 3 (`terminate` through the route: 4) |
| 10 | No write of a suspended account commits inside its suspension | the route walk (12.2) and labs B and C on the real code (12.3): the walk's pass for an account suspended at once and labs B and B6 in Task 3; the passes for a terminated account and for an announced suspension before its start, and lab C, in Task 4 | 3, 4 |
| 11 | An announced suspension never starts before `starts_at`, on any path, and the API role cannot move it | no start while `author_mail` is `pending`; `sent` gives the relay's acceptance plus the window; `failed` and `no_address` give decision plus `undelivered_hours`, and a mail given up later than that gives the moment it was given up, never a time already past; an on-time reply answered `kept` before `starts_at` starts at `starts_at`, and one answered after it starts at the answer; a second change of `author_mail` or `author_mail_at` refused by `decisions_mail_forward` | 1, 4 |
| 12 | A reply before the start holds it; one after changes nothing by itself | lab D on the real code; `on_time` equals `filed_at < start`; a `kept` answer starts it at the later of that decision's time and `starts_at`, under `FOR UPDATE`; a `partly_reversed` review that leaves the measure in force answers the reply as a `kept` one does; after a `kept` answer to an on-time reply the appeal is open for six months from the answer (`appeals_check`, `decision_final_at` and every `appealUntil` read the later `informed_at`); a second reply and a reply to an ended measure refused (`reply_not_open`); a correction drafted before the reply refused (`case_changed`) | 4 |
| 13 | No standing measure reaches a staff account, and no suspended account becomes staff | `staff_target` on each of the six measures; `grant-role` refused for an account with a standing measure (`changeRole` answers `"standing"`), with the decision and the grant racing in both orders | 1, 3 |
| 14 | A termination purges only once it is final, and its step never holds an ordinary deletion | `terminations_due()` empty during the window, during a pending appeal, during an unanswered reply, on time or late, and after a lift; a termination at once, final, with a late reply unanswered is not due, and is due after the `kept` answer; after a `kept` answer to an on-time reply it is due six months after that answer, not after the decision; it holds the account after `decision_final_at` with the times moved (`ageStandingForTest`). The step holds the profile and then the case of the terminating decision `FOR UPDATE` before it reads again: an appeal filed at the deadline commits first and keeps the account, or waits and is refused by its own deadline. The step then runs 1b's take-down, keeps frozen images (D 7.10) and sends `termination_final` after the take-down committed, once: an account lifted between the two reads gets no mail, and a take-down that fails mails nothing. A take-down made to throw is logged and counted, and an ordinary deletion of the same round is still purged; so is one when `terminations_due()` itself throws | 4 |
| 15 | A retired handle is nobody's | lab E on the real code; `handle_retired` from `changeHandle` and onboarding for the former holder and for a stranger; `handle_reserved` for the prefix, also by a direct write as `korbat_api`; `retire_handle` refused when it is not its decision's first row; the reversal's two branches | 2 |
| 16 | A mark is a person's, signed, and only on a signed-in rule report | `unfounded_marks_check`'s refusals (another transaction's decision, an outcome other than `no_violation`, an item it did not answer, an illegal-ground notice, a signed-out or CSAM item); a signed `unfounded` list that differs from the rows refused at commit, also when the extra mark is inserted after `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE` has run the check early (`unfounded_marks_recheck`); a mark no longer counted after a re-examination restricts, and after a `kept` correction of the decision that set it withdrew that decision's marks, all of them together (a decision with three marks: all three stop counting; a `kept` correction of a `no_violation` decision without a mark left is refused) | 5 |
| 17 | The reporting counts count reports, and the restriction refuses rule reports only | one report naming ten posts, all ten marked, counts as one marked report of one sent: no warning, and no report reaches a threshold alone; `unfounded` is never more than `reports`; a report sent before the frame and marked inside it counts on neither side; a warning refused below either threshold, a restriction without a standing warning, with only two marked reports sent since it, or with three that were sent before it and marked after; a warning lifted because its marks were withdrawn cannot be given again on them; lifting the warning that a restriction rests on clears `reports_restricted_until` in that transaction, with the restriction in that decision's `endedStanding`. A restricted account's rule report answers `reporting_restricted`; its notice of illegality, its CSAM report and its re-examination request are accepted | 5 |
| 18 | Explore's age and window resume | `explore_from - created_at` equals the ledger's hidden spans over two hide and restore rounds across two cases; a move between `withheld` and `removed` changes nothing | 6 |
| 19 | A suspended account is neither candidate nor approver | a round with a suspended author and a suspended engager, judged at the round's `at`; `explorePage` and the chip at read time; the skip rule ranking at once after the version change; any formula version but 2 throws; status `suspended` | 6 |
| 20 | A notifier never learns of a strike, and a reporter never follows an account's standing | an item's released line and status page are equal with and without a strike (T43); a reporter's line for an item in an account case is the same before and after a ladder suspension of that account is announced, held, started, lifted and ended, and before and after a warning or restriction of its reporting; an item that a decision with an account measure answers reads 3c's restricted outcome with `ACCOUNT_MEASURE_SCOPE` (section 10) and no sentence about access, duration or suspension | 1 (the strike), 3 and 4 (the measures) |
| 21 | The lock order holds | the decide route asserts `FOR UPDATE` as its first lock exactly for the transactions section 5 lists; lab B with 3c's purge order deadlocks, and with section 5's order a decision, a third account and a follower's purge do not; with the purged accounts themselves followed, unfollowed, replied to and quoted by lower and higher ids (the two deadlocks the review measured, X1 and X1b) a deadlock is still detected, and no request fails on it: `follow`, `unfollow` and `createPost` try once more on `40P01` (a test makes each meet one and asserts the second try's ordinary answer); the take-down of a terminated account takes the profile before the case | 3, 4 |
| 22 | What is kept is what the terms say | `erase_reply_statements` blanks a reply only once it is answered and its decision is final: an unanswered reply filed after finality, and one left unanswered past it, keep their text; `forget_report_counts` deletes marks and tallies past the frame; both go with the account; each retention step's audit row | 7 |
| 23 | No write is added without the predicate | the source pins of 12.2, each with a deliberate violation it catches | 3 |
| 24 | The R4 redaction never meets a decision whose only measures are 3e's | the retention job's candidates and `erasureOverdue` name `remove`, `remove_media`, `reset_bio` and `reset_display_name`: a ladder suspension and a retirement that cite R4 are final for months and are never a candidate, never raise and never count as overdue | 1 (read in 3c's built text), 2 (the retirement) |

### 12.2 By kind

- **Unit.** `STANDING_RULES` in the contracts equals `standing_rules()` in SQL (a database test reads the function). `MEASURES` has its 22 kinds with the labels of W.1. `statementParagraphs` for every new measure, reversal and answer of W.2, each with the six Art. 17(3) elements. The paragraph of the rule or the term (W.2), by basis: no statement of a `protective` decision contains "It breaks", and it holds `basis.protective`, the terms' section as its ground and no other sentence about the images; a statement on the basis `strikes` or `ladder_end` names the terms' section as its ground and the cited rule only as the rule of the newest strike; every other basis reads "Your account's conduct breaks". Each of the three states of a lifted or ended period (`not_started`, `running`, `ended`) renders its own sentence, and a lift signed `replaced` renders `withdrawn` and neither `replyReversed` nor `liftedBefore`. Rendering under redress version 1 and rulebook version 2 is unchanged. `mailTemplateOf` over the table of 7.15, row by row in its order, with W.6's `why` and `period` for each `suspension_lifted`. The step-up params with `days`, `announced`, `unfounded` and `replaced` (strict, no transform, C7's walk). `exploreRules` and `exploreStatusText` under `EXPLORE_V2`.
- **The tables (P9).** Every number in `REDRESS_TEXTS[2]`, in the four terms sections and in `STANDING_WORDS` and `STANDING_TEXTS` is held to `STANDING_RULES`, and the terms' five days and 24 hours to `RETENTION.mailGiveUpDays` and `REPLY_AIM_HOURS` (10.1): no sentence of sections 8 and 9 types a number of days, of strikes or of rungs. The app's and the console's message tables have an entry for each new code that a route of that front end can answer (the console: the five of 8.9). `STANDING_DRAFTS` holds its ten texts and the composer is held to them: the test compares a filled draft with the case answer it was filled from, not with the decision, since a draft says "as this page read it". D 12's test that the terms name no measure the constants lack no longer lists a strike or a retired handle as lacking.
- **The source pins** (`apps/api/test/architecture.test.ts`, in the form 3c's pins have; Task 3). (a) `liveNotSuspended` is named in exactly a pinned list of files, each a pinned number of times (as `REQUEST_DELETION` is), so whoever adds a write on behalf of an account must add its site to the list. (b) `suspended_from`, `suspended_until` and `reports_restricted_until` are named in `apps/api/src` only in `lib/standing.ts`, in `social/repo.ts`'s `lockAccounts` and in the moderation module's `standing-repo.ts`. (c) A deliberate violation of each, which the test catches. This is the control section 11 names against a write added without the predicate; the one writer of the three columns is pinned by the function test of row 2, not here.
- **The route walk** (`apps/api/test/integration/standing-route-walk.test.ts`). For an account suspended at once (Task 3), and again for one terminated (Task 4, `terminateForTest`), every route of the app's route table is called once and answers as section 7's table says: 403 `account_suspended` or its ordinary answer. The test is driven from the route table and fails on a route that the table of section 7 does not list. A third pass (Task 4), for an account whose announced suspension has not started, expects every ordinary answer.
- **Flows.** One test a flow of section 7, end to end through the routes, with the mails read from the memory mailer and the times moved by `ageStandingForTest`: among them the start of an announced suspension with no write at that instant, a deletion started during the reply window going its 1b way, an announced suspension withdrawn for a suspension at once (the person gets `suspension_lifted` with the withdrawal's lead and never `reply_answered`), and a suspension whose strike is overruled after it ended.
- **HTTP.** `POST /api/moderation/decisions/:id/reply` (its session, its owner only, 404 for a decision with `author_mail` `none`); `GET /api/staff/standing` behind the staff gate, with `PERSONAL`'s check over the answer, and its row in the staff route-table test (`apps/api/test/staff/route-table.test.ts`); `/me` carrying the standing fresh after a refusal, and `standing: null` from an app built without the moderation module (the thirteen HTTP test files that build one) and for an account without a profile.
- **Mail.** The six templates of `mailAccount`'s union and the auth module's flat `termination_final` pinned: no link in any; `userMail`'s closing sentence in each; a termination's wording in the first two; no text says "handed to your mail provider" or "delivered"; `termination_final` sent in place of `account_deleted`, after the take-down and never before it; `suspension_lifted`'s leads for a lift, a withdrawal and each state of a broken basis; `moderation_waiting`'s reply line; "sub-project 3f" in the two staff mails.
- **Console.** Server-rendered checks of 8.2 to 8.8: each control open and greyed with its sentence, for a moderator and an admin; the mark offered only on a markable item of a "No breach" decision; the browser check of 8.10.
- **App.** Server-rendered checks of every row of 9.1 and 9.2 and of each sentence of 9.3 to 9.7, with the roles of 9.8.
- **Smoke.** `/me` answers a `standing` with no strike, no measure and no restriction for the smoke account, which is onboarded (`scripts/smoke-auth-stack.mjs:353-356`); `GET /api/staff/standing` answers 401 without a staff session. A suspension needs a staff key, so it is the dry run's browser walk-through.

### 12.3 The labs, repeated on the real code

`apps/api/test/lab/standing.lab.ts`, run by hand as `contention.lab.ts` is, on a quiet machine (M 9, O6), with its numbers recorded in the task's report. Task 2 creates the file with lab E; each later task adds the labs of its own code:

| Lab (M 7) | On the real code | Passes when | Task |
|---|---|---|---|
| B1, B5 | the repository's own functions as the writers; suspensions at once through the decide route; a follower's purge at B5's share | 0 writes of the account inside a suspension; 0 deadlocks; the decision's p95 recorded | 3 |
| B2, B4 | the same with the API's lock left at `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, and with 3c's purge order | deadlocks appear: the two reorders are what removes them | 3 |
| B7 (new; the review's X1 and X1b) | the purged accounts are themselves followed and unfollowed by accounts with lower and with higher ids, and their posts replied to and quoted by accounts that follow them, while the purge runs | no request answers an error and no purge is lost: section 5's order does not remove this one cycle, so PostgreSQL still detects a deadlock here, and `follow`, `unfollow` and `createPost` try once more on `40P01` while the purge is tried again by its job's next round. The number of deadlocks detected and of second tries is recorded. LAB B5 could not meet this, since its purged accounts were never a party of its follow and quote loops | 3 |
| B6 | the account's own deletion against its suspension | either order, no error; `no_effect` `deleted` when the deletion won | 3 |
| C1, C2 | announced suspensions whose start `ageStandingForTest` brings near; then the predicate reading `now()` | 0 writes admitted at or after a start; with `now()` some are, so the clock rule is what holds it | 4 |
| D | replies fired around the start | `on_time` equals `filed_at < start` in every round; no write refused before a start, none admitted after | 4 |
| E | the retiring decision against `changeHandle` | 0 profiles left holding a retired handle | 2 |
| F | the round's statement under version 1 and 2 on generated data | the same order of cost | 6 |

## 13. Tasks

Seven tasks in M 8's order, with M 8's first two as one: the migration that makes a strike compulsory and the code that signs one cannot be two commits, since between them every rule removal would be refused. Each task is one commit, written tests first by one implementer, and each leaves the stack working and the whole suite able to pass. The gates are light for every task: install gates when the lockfile changed, the type check, the unit suites, `generate` printing "No schema changes", the task's migrations applied, the console's build and browser check or the web export when that side changed, the integration files the task names (its own new files and the existing files whose code it changes), the bundles. The whole suite of 12, on a fresh and on an upgraded database, runs at the three batch ends: after Task 1, which changes what every decision signs; after Task 3, which changes the locking of every write; and after Task 7. What a later task's tests need is built by the task that first needs it (the fixtures of section 12), and no task is given a test of code a later task builds.

**Before Task 1** (O3): 3c is complete, all twelve of its tasks. 3e changes the author's reads and the app's cases screens (3c's Task 7), the appeals (8), retention (9), the export, the terms and the smoke test (10) and the README's runbook (12). 3e's five migrations are numbered from the first number free after 3c, as section 6 says in "The numbers", the one place that writes them. The tasks below therefore name them and give no number: the schema migration (generated by drizzle), `standing_ledger`, `reporting_counts`, `explore_resume` and `standing_retention`; `--upgrade-from` names 3c's last migration. The implementer reads the built text of 0017, 0018 and any later migration of 3c first, and writes the replacements of `decisions_record`, `decisions_apply`, `review_needs_admin` and `forget_reporter` from that text, not from D 6.3 and 6.4. A difference from this design's section 6 is recorded as an amendment. In the same reading the implementer checks that 3c's R4 redaction, as built (the retention job's candidate rule and `erasureOverdue`), names the four kinds `remove`, `remove_media`, `reset_bio` and `reset_display_name` and does not read "a measure in force" from the view that 3e widens; where it reads the view, naming the four kinds is part of Task 1 (12.1 row 24).

### Task 1. The standing ledger and strikes (batch end)

- **Builds, the ledger.** The schema migration (generated by drizzle, then the grants and forced columns by hand) and `standing_ledger` (by hand): the ten kinds, the seven ledger columns, the profile, handle-history and post columns, `suspension_replies`, `unfounded_marks`, `report_tallies`, the views, `standing_rules()`, `standing_apply`, `strike_due`, the replaced functions and the new triggers of section 6. The contracts' `ACTION_KINDS` (22), `MEASURES` with W.1's labels, `STANDING_RULES`, `STANDING_DRAFTS` (W.5), and the step-up's params of 10.4 whole, for every new kind: both shapes of an action entry, `unfounded`, `replaced`, the outcome `kept` and the refinements. `insertAction` passing `days`, `announced` and `replaced`, and the inserts of the marks (7.1). `@korbat/rules`: `RULEBOOKS[3]` (W.4) and `REDRESS_TEXTS[2]` (W.3), with both version constants raised; `statementParagraphs` renders version 2's three old texts as before.
- **Builds, the routes' list.** The decide and review routes hold one list of the kinds they accept (7.1) and refuse any other with `invalid_decision` before the transaction, as they refuse a non-empty `unfounded` and a `replaced` until the task that opens each. After this task the list holds 3c's twelve kinds, `strike` and `overrule`. Each later task adds its kinds: Task 2 `retire_handle` and `unretire_handle`; Task 3 `lift_suspension` and `suspend` with `announced: false` only (a second check); Task 4 `terminate`, `suspend` with `announced: true`, the outcome `kept` and `replaced`; Task 5 the three reporting kinds and `unfounded` (the `kept` correction that withdraws a decision's marks needs nothing opened: no mark exists before that task). `decideWith` and the fixtures of section 12 pass the flag that lifts the list, so this task's database-level tests can store every kind.
- **Builds, strikes.** `strike` and `overrule` through the decide and review routes. The case answer: `CaseView.strikeDue` and `authorStanding` on a post case; on an account case `standing` with every member of section 10 but `reporting` (`activeStrikes`, `strikes`, `ladderRule`, `suspensionsInYear`, `lastSuspendedOn`, `maxDays`, `mayTerminate`, `live`, `openReports`, `atOnce`, `flood`, `seal`), and `CaseDecisionAction`'s `days`, `announced`, `basis`, `restsOn`, `earliestStart`, `countsUntil` and `period`. The console signs a strike when one is due and the `overrule` with a full reversal (`compose.ts`), with the composer's strike line and bands (8.2), the review's [review.strike] and [review.rests], and the words for `strike_mismatch` (8.9). W.2's `strike`, `strikeAnyway`, `overruled` and the strike's automation sentence; `PostView.moderation.strikeUntil` and the chip; the Cases block and the strike's line on its decision's screen (9.2, 9.4); the export's `strikes`.

Every hand-built params object in the contracts', the console's and the API's tests gains the members 10.4 makes always present (`unfounded: []` among them), which is why they land here, at a batch end. `strikeForTest` and `ageStandingForTest`. `removeDecided`, `removeFixtures` and `scripts/clean-dev-fixtures.mjs` delete the rows of the three new tables first.

- **The runbook's new bullets.** (a) The lists to watch each working day: Replies to answer first, then Accounts at three strikes, Notices not sent, Reporting records, Terminations waiting. (b) Answering a reply: read it on its case, answer within 24 hours, write what was weighed; an on-time reply holds its suspension until answered, a kept one never starts before its announced time, and a terminated account is not deleted while any reply to its termination is unanswered. (c) A protective suspension: only after an admin sealed images of the account for good, in the case the report is in; then on the account's case, in a decision of its own, only by an admin, once for that seal, for at most 14 days, never worded as a finding and with no promise of a later look. Its appeal is reviewed at once, by an admin who took no part, and does not wait for the authorities; only the seal's own appeal waits. Whoever has the server still runs `staff.js account-facts` before the admin answers the report, since korbat never holds an account against its deletion.
- **The runbook's new bullets, continued.** (d) A handle whose breach lies in the handle is retired, no longer left open. (e) A person who writes to the contact address about a notice they did not get by mail is pointed to the app, where the notice is always shown and the reply is open; korbat cannot see whether a mail it sent arrived. (f) To take a measure at once on an account whose announced suspension has not started: "Withdraw it for another measure" on the announcing decision, then the measure at once; never "Reverse", which would tell the person that their reply succeeded. (g) A mark "manifestly unfounded" that was wrong is withdrawn with "Correct this decision" on the decision that set it, which withdraws all of that decision's marks together; lifting a warning withdraws no mark, so whoever lifts one because its marks were wrong corrects those decisions as well. (h) Before a ladder suspension or termination, a protective suspension, a warning or a restriction of reporting, the reports in the account's open case are decided first: none of these measures ever answers a report.

## 14. Success criteria

1. A final decision that finds a breach of a korbat rule in a post gives exactly the strike the database computes, and no other decision gives one. The person sees each strike, until when it counts and how many they have, and can contest it with its decision for six months, also after it stopped counting.
2. No account is suspended or terminated except by a measure of a decision that a staff key confirmed and the database checked against the ladder or the closed list: announced with at least 168 hours and a reply that holds the start, or at once by an admin on a ground whose condition is a fact of the database. No announced suspension starts before the time its person was given, on any path: a kept answer to a reply never starts it earlier, so nobody is worse off for having replied.
3. While a suspension runs, no write of the account commits (none in the route walk, none in the labs), and nothing else changes: redress, the export, deletion and notices of illegal content stay open, and the account's posts and profile carry no mark.
4. Every such decision is shown to its person in the app at once and sent to them by mail, with a statement that holds every Art. 17(3) element, names no reporter, names the ground it truly rests on (korbat's terms for a step of the ladder and for a protective suspension, never "It breaks" for a protective one), says no more of a suspicion, of a mail or of a past period than korbat knows, and reads the same whenever it is rendered; the screen beside it gives the exact start and end. No text says that a mail was delivered: korbat knows only that it sent one.
5. Standing is derived, never set: the three profile columns and the three post columns have one writer each, an auditor recomputes them from the ledger, and what rested on a decision ends with it in the same transaction: a ladder suspension when an overruled strike leaves fewer than three of its strikes standing, a suspension at once when its decision is reversed or its seal lifted, a restriction of reporting when its warning is lifted.
6. A termination deletes nothing before its decision is final for the person, and nothing while an appeal or a reply, on time or late, is unanswered. korbat never refuses or delays a person's own deletion, also when the termination step fails, and keeps nothing to recognise a new account of a person who is gone (images that korbat sealed stay for the authorities, D 7.10).
7. A retired handle is used by nobody, and its reversal returns it to its former holder alone.
8. The restriction of reporting rests on marks that persons set and signed and that a correction can withdraw. Its counts count reports, never items, so no single report reaches a threshold; it never reaches a notice of illegal content; it ends with the warning that was its notice; and its counts are kept 90 days and read by nothing else.
9. Explore's published rules, version 2, are what the round computes: no suspended author, no suspended approver, and a restored post's time given back.
10. The console runs under 3a's policy with no violation in the browser check over every new screen.
11. Nobody who reported an account can follow its standing: a ladder measure, a protective suspension and a reporting measure never answer a report, and a report that an account measure does answer reads 3c's restricted outcome and no word about access, duration or suspension, once.
12. A protective suspension rests on a seal for good that the ledger holds, once for that seal and for at most 14 days; it is reviewed on appeal at once; and nothing a person reads about it says or implies a finding, or promises a look that nobody takes.

## 15. Changes to the main design and to the 1b, 2a, 2c and 3c designs

Task 7 writes each as a dated amendment: in the main design's section 9, and as a section "Changed by 3e" in each other design.

**The main design** (`2026-09-03-korbat-design.md`).

- **Line 172** (R2: "account suspended"): a report of child sexual abuse material alone supports no measure on the account. An admin's seal for good of images of the account (3c) supports at most one protective suspension, which an admin takes for up to 14 days in a decision of its own, which is no finding, and which is appealed and reviewed at once. No suspension is automatic: 3d's hash match may ask for one, and a person confirms it. Reason: korbat's staff do not open images reported this way, so korbat has assessed nothing (LAW E2).
- **Line 180** ("May not ... suspend an account (except R2)"): the exception is dropped. Reason: GDPR Art. 22 and the line above.
- **Line 185** (the action `strike`; "suspension requires 3 active strikes or an R2/R5 case"): a strike is not a choice of the moderator but the database's consequence of a final rule decision on a post, for 90 days. Three active strikes let a moderator announce a suspension of at most 3, 7 and 30 days, with 168 hours to reply; after a suspension one further strike opens the next step while three still count, and after three such suspensions decided in 365 days, whatever their length, an admin may announce a termination. A suspension at once is an admin's, on the closed list of section 2; no announced suspension takes one of its grounds. Reason: equal treatment that an auditor can recompute, and recital 64's prior warning.
- **Line 186** ("Upheld appeals reverse the action and remove the strike"): holds, and goes further: a suspension that rested on the strike loses its basis in the same transaction when fewer than three of its strikes still stand. One that has not started does not start, one that runs ends, and one that had already ended no longer counts toward a later suspension.
- **Line 192** ("Suspension shows the case list and a link to appeal each one"): the banner on every screen, and the suspension's own decision screen with the strikes it rests on, each linking to its decision and that decision's appeal (9.1, 9.2).
- **Section 2, line 109** (`strikes`): a ledger measure, not a table.
- **Section 6, row 3:** the order of sub-project 3 is 3c, 3e, 3f, 3d. korbat is not opened to the public before 3f's order path and 3d's handling of sealed images.

**The 1b design** (account lifecycle).

- 7.1, 7.2, 7.4: profile edits, the handle change and the badge's start and verify refuse a suspended account (`account_suspended`). The refusal of a profile edit is whole: a suspended account must not rename itself, so it also cannot blank its own bio, and its ways to erase are deleting posts and deleting the account (section 11 names this limit, flagged for the owner). The handle change refuses a retired handle to everyone (`handle_retired`) and the prefix `retired_` (`handle_reserved`); a retirement clears the 30-day wait; "handles you used before stay yours" holds except for a retired one.
- 7.5: the export's `moderation` member gains W.8's members, and `handleHistory` gains `retiredAt`.
- 7.6: deletion is never refused or delayed by a suspension, a termination or a seal (section 2, the row on sealed images; flagged). A termination runs 1b's take-down once it is final and no appeal and no reply is unanswered: the purge round first calls the moderation module's `takeDownDue`, which holds the account's profile and the terminating decision's case while the auth module's `takeDownTerminated` takes the account down and then mails `termination_final`, where 1b mails `account_deleted`. That step has a try of its own, and a failure in it never stops the round's ordinary deletions; `PurgeReport` gains `terminated`. `purgeProfile` locks the profiles whose counters it writes before any post (section 5).
- `grant-role` refuses an account that holds a standing measure: `changeRole` answers `"standing"`.

**The 2a design** (posting and feed).

- Sections 5 and 7: every write of the acting account obeys the one predicate "live and not suspended" and answers 403 `account_suspended` otherwise; the table of section 7 lists each route. Withdrawing (unlike, unrepost, unbookmark, unfollow), blocks, mutes and deletion stay open.
- Section 5.3: `follow` and `unfollow` lock both profile rows in id order, the one order whose lack D 3 records as a deadlock between them; `createPost` locks its author's row. Each of the three tries once more when PostgreSQL reports a deadlock (`40P01`): against the purge of the other account the order of section 5 leaves one cycle open (the review's X1 and X1b).

**The 2c design** (Explore).

- Section 7: formula version 2. Candidates and engagement add the predicate on the author and on the engager at the round's time; the window and the score's age read `coalesce(explore_from, created_at)`; step 2's skip rule counts only rankings of the current version.
- Section 8: the status gains `suspended`, and the reads add the predicate on the author. Section 10: `EXPLORE_V2` replaces `EXPLORE_V1`. Section 11: the texts of 9.7 and the change log's second entry; among them a stale sentence is corrected: "What else takes a post out" no longer names "the automatic R1 and R2 checks", which rulebook version 2 removed.

**The 3c design.**

- **Section 16** is superseded by this design for strikes, retirement, suspensions, termination, the restriction of reporting and Explore, and by 3f (section 16 below) for the rest.
