Where it differs: a suspension lives in the ledger (no `suspensions` or `suspension_lifts` table); a strike's column is `ends_at`; the reply window is 168 hours, not 72 (flagged for the owner); a reply holds the start and is answered by a correction, it does not cancel and return the case; the bridge is `auth.account_standing`; no step-up action is added; no account is held against its deletion (flagged; 3f may revisit).
- **Section 2**, row Lock order: the subject's profile is taken `FOR UPDATE` in the transactions section 5 lists. The purge's residue is closed for a decision and a third account, and not for the purged account's own counterparts: there a deadlock is still detected, and `follow`, `unfollow` and `createPost` try once more (section 5). Row Queue order and weight: per-account counts of reports now exist for 90 days, as the input of one measure with a statement, never as a key of the queue. Row What a report or notice holds: "A notice or report naming an account is answered for its display name and bio" holds unless its answering decision holds an account measure (a retirement, a suspension at once on a threat, on terrorist content or on a flood, a termination at once); the notifier then reads `ACCOUNT_MEASURE_SCOPE` in place of `ACCOUNT_ITEM_SCOPE`. A ladder measure, a protective suspension and a reporting measure never answer a report at all (section 6).
- **6.3, 6.4, 6.5:** a correction may have the outcome `kept`, to answer a reply or to withdraw all marks of a `no_violation` decision together (the one correction of a decision that restricted nothing: the built text accepts a correction of a restricting decision only, `0016_decisions_and_ledger.sql:265`); `review_needs_admin` is also true for a termination and for a measure taken at once; `moderation_apply`'s pin gains three columns. `appeals_check` and `decision_final_at` read the later of two `informed_at` when a kept answer to an on-time reply started the measure, so the six months count from that answer. For the R4 redaction an R4 measure stays one of `remove`, `remove_media`, `reset_bio` and `reset_display_name`: the retention job's candidate rule and `erasureOverdue` name those four kinds and never read the new kinds of `measures_in_force`.
- **7.3, 7.7:** a decision that holds a protective `suspend` answers the account's R2 finding and holds no seal, but it is no "answer without a seal": what the seal's case froze stays frozen, `suspicion.earlier` does not count it, and nobody may open the images because of it. Its appeal is reviewed at once; the wait for the authorities stays that of the seal's own decision.
- **7.12, 7.14, 7.15 (stale words, now naming 3f):** "before 3e" reads "before 3f" in both titles; the last sentence of the two staff mails `moderation_urgent` and `moderation_waiting`, "Until the phone alerts of sub-project 3e, this email is the only alert" (and "... the only reminder"; built as `apps/api/src/mail/templates.ts:243` and `:266`), names sub-project 3f; so does section 2's sentence that the urgent mail "is the only alert until 3e's phone alert". The queue-age alert gains replies older than 24 hours.
- **Section 9:** the terms' sentence on forgetting who sent a report gains the exception of W.7; the profile sentence on handles changes (9.5); the list "Your reports" shows a mark with its outcome (9.4).
- **Section 10:** redress version 2 holds the reply's texts; an order's redress is 3f's, in a later version. `ACCOUNT_MEASURE_SCOPE` joins `ACCOUNT_ITEM_SCOPE` (W.1). A `ReportRow`'s items gain `unfounded` (10.2; D 7.6). The statement's paragraph of the rule, "It breaks korbat rule ...", stays for every decision that holds no standing measure; a decision that holds one gets the paragraph of W.2 by its basis.
- **Section 12:** the table test no longer treats a strike or a retired handle as a measure the constants lack.
- **Section 13**, Task 12's runbook: a breach that lies only in a handle is decided with "Retire the handle" and no longer waits. The hard gate (the README's first line under Before going public, and where D states it) reads "until sub-project 3f's order path and sub-project 3d's handling of sealed images have both shipped": it named 3e, and the order path moved to 3f.
- **Section 17**, Which sub-project owns what: the record of reports of sealed images, the hand-over, the off-server copy, the phone alert and any hold on deletion are 3f's, not 3e's.
- **Not changed by 3e, noted for 3c's builder:** a seal's own statement renders "It breaks korbat rule R2" whenever its decision cites R2 (`packages/rules/src/statement.ts:154-166`, pinned by `packages/rules/test/statement.test.ts:217`), beside drafts that say korbat has not assessed the images. 3e's protective statement does not repeat that sentence (W.2); whether a seal's statement should keep it is 3c's question, with the same counsel flag.

## 16. Sub-project 3f: authorities' orders, alerts, records and recovery (scope only)

A design of its own, after 3e and before 3d. Its scope is D 16's fourth and fifth bullets, unchanged in aim, with what the research of 2026-10-05 found. That research was stopped before it was finished, and each file says where.

- **Orders** (`gap-E-orders-law.md`). A TCO removal order arrives at the published contact point in a form and by a channel that only the home authority can state, possibly unsigned, so every message there is treated as a possible order until verified against the register (E9). The safe clock for the hour runs from the earliest record of arrival. Five instruments differ in channel, deadline, what is owed back and what the person is told: a TCO removal order, a DSA Art. 9 order, a DSA Art. 10 order, and the e-Evidence production and preservation orders, which reach korbat only through a designated establishment (E15). Preservation under TCO Art. 6 is six months from the disabling, of the post as disabled, opened only for review or an authority (E20). The TCO Art. 7(2) report is owed in a year with an order; whether a removal on a notice alone triggers it is open (E21). What the author of disabled content is told (E12 (b) to (e)) is not written.
- **The measure and its records** (D 16): `legal_orders` and their events with the clock; `disable_by_order` and its reversal, added to `action_kind` under section 6's enum rule; `decision_source` `order`, an order's redress text in a new redress version, its outcome phrase, lane 0; the Annex II and III records; the step-up actions, for which 3e left `append_confirmed_audit`'s list untouched.
- **The alert channel** (`gap-E-alerts-supply.md`). A self-hosted ntfy, pinned (v2.28.0, one maintainer, not AGPL), can be private and can wake an Android phone without Google; iOS cannot carry the hour today; whether an alert wakes a person depends on phone settings no server sees (E13, with checks for the owner on real phones). The IMAP watcher and the mail host are half read (E14); the infrastructure reading (E26) is not started. Until 3f the lane-0 and queue-age alerts stay mails.
- **Records to authorities and the hand-over** (`gap-E-authorities.md`). In Germany the addressee of an Art. 18 notification, of a TCO Art. 14(5) notification and of a report of suspected child sexual abuse material is the BKA's portal, after a registration; Europol's channel was not found (E17). A hand-over of sealed images follows a demand under criminal procedure, not DSA Art. 10, goes to law enforcement alone, and ends nothing on korbat's side (E16). 3f builds the record of every such report and the hand-over's command and record.
- **Recovery** (D 16): the off-server daily copy of decisions and the restore step that applies them again; `reverse-window`, whose premise inputs P3-19 found false in 3c's ledger.
- **What 3f decides that 3e left** (section 2, flagged): whether an account whose images are sealed may be held against deletion until korbat has reported (E22, not started; 3f has the record of reports such a hold would read).
- **What 3e leaves ready:** Explore's resume rule keyed on a post's level, so `disable_by_order` needs no change to it; the profile predicate as one exported fragment; the enum rule and `--upgrade-from`; `append_confirmed_audit`'s action list; the protective suspension, which rests on a seal for good, once for that seal and for at most 14 days. A longer or a repeated measure, and the check "that somebody carries out" which LAW E2 answer 4 asks for, wait for a fact 3e does not have: 3f's record of the authority's answer, or 3d's confirmed hash match. 3e's statement therefore promises no later look by staff.

## 17. Sub-project 3d (scope only)

D 17 stands as written, with 3f in place of 3e in its paragraph "Which sub-project owns what". 3e adds three things for it. A hash match may ask for a protective suspension, but only as a request that a person confirms, and under 3e's rule only a seal for good can carry one: so a match leads an admin to seal first, or 3d adds the confirmed match as a second fact the basis may rest on, in a design of its own. 3e builds the measure and no automatic writer, and an automated request needs a statement that says so (Art. 17(3)(c); GDPR Art. 22). The Transparency Database export gains the account measures' statements: a suspension and a termination are restrictions of the account under Art. 17(1)(d), a restriction of reporting is counsel's question (LAW E6 (d)), and a strike alone is none (LAW E1 (b)). The public rules page prints rulebook version 3 and the numbers of `STANDING_RULES`, from the constants the terms already use.

## 18. The words (what sections 7 to 10 refer to by key)

Where each block belongs in the joined design: W.1 to W.5 in section 10 (they are constants of `@korbat/contracts` and `@korbat/rules`); W.6 in section 7's subsection on mails (7.15); W.7 in section 9 (the terms); W.8 with section 7's reads (the export); W.9 with section 7's `grant-role`.

Four rules hold for every text below.

1. **No reporter, no count of reports about the person, no staff handle** appears in anything a person receives (D 7.15), and nothing a reporter receives lets them follow the standing of the account they reported (W.1, the last paragraph).
2. **A statement reads as it was sent** (D 10, P1). Its sentences print the decision's own row (`days`, `starts_at`, `ends_at`, the decisions in `rests_on`) and facts the ledger fixes for good, among them where a lifted or ended period stood at this decision's time. A number the row lacks stands as a literal in the redress texts of the decision's version (W.3), never read from `STANDING_RULES` at render time. What changes later (the exact start, a reply, a lift) is shown beside the statement by the screen (9.2), never inside it.
3. **Every time** in a statement or mail prints as `utcMinute` does ("2026-10-16 14:05 UTC", `packages/rules/src/statement.ts:20`).
4. **No text says more than korbat knows or has checked.**
   - Of a mail: korbat records that its own relay accepted it. Every text says "sent", never "handed to your mail provider" or "delivered", and says that the app always shows the notice.
   - Of a suspicion: a protective suspension is never worded as a finding, and its statement never holds "It breaks korbat rule R2". No text says "nobody at korbat has seen the images", which korbat cannot know (an image may have been opened in an earlier case, or seen in the app); each says, as 3c's `SEAL_DRAFTS` do, that korbat's staff do not open images reported this way and that korbat has not assessed them. No text promises a later look by staff: the design holds none.
   - Of a ground: a decision that finds no new breach (a step of the ladder, a protective suspension) names the term it rests on, not a rule it "breaks".
   - Of a period: a sentence about a lift, a broken basis or a kept reply has a form for a suspension that had not started, one for a running one and one for one that had already ended, and says in the last case what does change: it no longer counts toward a later suspension.
   - Of a count in a signed text: it is worded by the moment the page read it.

### W.1 The measures' labels (`MEASURES[kind].label`)

| Kind | Label | Kind | Label |
|---|---|---|---|
| `strike` | "Strike" | `overrule` | "Overrule the strike" |
| `suspend` | "Suspend the account" | `lift_suspension` | "Lift the suspension" |
| `terminate` | "Terminate the account" | `unretire_handle` | "Give the handle back" |
| `retire_handle` | "Retire the handle" | `lift_reporting` | "Lift the reporting measure" |
| `warn_reporting` | "Warn about reporting" | | |
| `restrict_reporting` | "Restrict reporting" | | |

Where the lifted row is known, the console and the statement name it: "termination" for a `terminate`, "warning" or "restriction" for the two reporting measures.

**What a reporter reads** (`ACCOUNT_MEASURE_SCOPE`, `packages/contracts/src/moderation.ts`, beside D 10's `ACCOUNT_ITEM_SCOPE`). A reporter is told the outcome of their own report in 3c's words, once. When the decision that answers an item about an account holds an account measure, the line is 3c's restricted outcome, and in place of `ACCOUNT_ITEM_SCOPE` (which speaks of a display name and bio that this decision did not touch) it carries:

- `ACCOUNT_MEASURE_SCOPE.retired`, whenever that decision holds a `retire_handle`, which everybody can see on the profile: "korbat retired the handle."
- `ACCOUNT_MEASURE_SCOPE.measure`, otherwise: "korbat took a measure on the account. It does not report here which, or for how long."

No line says that access is restricted, for how long, or that the account is suspended, and none follows the measure afterwards: a later lift, a broken basis and the answer to the account's reply add no line beyond what D says of a decision changed on review or by a correction. Only a retirement, a suspension at once on a threat, on terrorist content or on a flood, and a termination at once can answer a report this way; a ladder suspension or termination, a protective suspension, a warning and a restriction of reporting answer findings only, since the database refuses them while their case holds an unanswered report (8.3 [reports.first]). A mark on a report is shown to the person who sent it, on their own list (9.4), and to nobody else.

### W.2 The statement's new sentences (`STATEMENT_WORDS`, `packages/rules/src/statement.ts`)

The order of D 10 stays: what was done; where and how long; the source; automation; the rule or the law, which for a decision that holds a standing measure is the paragraph "The rule or the term" below; the facts; the reason; redress. `{list}` is one entry a decision in `rests_on`, joined by "; ": "the strike of {decidedAt} under rule {ruleId} (your post of {postCreatedAt})". `{window}` is the hours between the row's `created_at` and `starts_at`. `{endsAt}` is the row's `ends_at` for a strike and a warning; for a measure taken at once, the row's `created_at` plus its `days`; for a kept answer to an on-time reply, `{startsAt}` plus the `days`, where `{startsAt}` is the later of that answer's time and the measure's `starts_at` (the earliest start it was announced with); for a kept answer to a late reply, the end the period already has. `{startedAt}` and `{endedAt}` are the start and the end of a period as they stood at this decision's time (`periodThen`). `{basisDecidedAt}` is the `decidedAt` of the decision in `rests_on`; on the basis `threat_repeat`, which rests on two R5 decisions, `{earlierDecidedAt}` is the older one's and `{basisDecidedAt}` the newer one's; on the basis `protective`, `{sealedAt}` is the `decidedAt` of the seal's decision, the one entry of `rests_on`.

**What was done: measures.**

| Key | For | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| `strike` | `strike` | "This decision gives your account a strike. It counts until {endsAt}. With it your account had {activeThen} active strikes when this was decided. korbat's rules fix when a decision gives a strike; the moderator does not choose it." Then `strikeNext` (W.3). |
| `strikeAnyway` | a `strike` whose other measures all have `noEffect` | "The strike counts although the post was already not shown or deleted: what counts is the breach." |
| `suspendAnnounced` | `suspend`, announced | "korbat will suspend your account for {days} days. Nothing is restricted yet." |
| `restsOn` | basis `strikes` | "It rests on the active strikes of your account: {list}." |
| `start` | announced | "It starts {window} hours after korbat sent the email with this decision, and not before {earliestStart}. If korbat's mail service cannot send it to your address, it starts later, and korbat's app alone tells you. korbat cannot see whether an email it sent reached you: this decision is always shown in the app. The exact start and end are on this decision's screen under Settings, Cases, as soon as they are fixed." |
| `suspendAtOnce` | `suspend`, at once | "korbat suspended your account for {days} days, from this decision until {endsAt}, without notice beforehand." |
| `basis.threat` | basis `threat` | "It was taken at once because korbat's decision of {basisDecidedAt} found a direct threat of violence in a post of yours (rule R5). It ends at once if that decision is reversed." |
| `basis.terrorist` | basis `terrorist` | "It was taken at once because korbat's decision of {basisDecidedAt} found terrorist content in a post of yours. It ends at once if that decision is reversed." |
| `basis.flood` | basis `flood` | "It was taken at once because an admin found that your account was flooding korbat with repetitive, unsolicited posts (rule R6). The facts below say how many posts and what they were." |
| `basis.protective` | basis `protective` | "It was taken at once because korbat's decision of {sealedAt} keeps images of your account sealed, after they were reported as possible child sexual abuse material. korbat's staff do not open images reported this way, so korbat has not assessed them. This is not a finding that you broke the law or a korbat rule: it is a protective measure, it gives no strike, and it ends on its date by itself, or earlier if that seal is lifted." |
| `terminateAnnounced` | `terminate`, announced | "korbat will terminate your account. Nothing is restricted yet. It follows the earlier suspensions of your account and rests on the active strikes of your account: {list}." Then `start`, reading "the termination" for "it". |
| `terminateAtOnce` | `terminate`, at once | "korbat terminated your account with this decision, without notice beforehand, because its decision of {basisDecidedAt} found a direct threat of violence (rule R5) in a post you made after korbat had suspended your account for an earlier threat, which its decision of {earlierDecidedAt} found. It ends at once if either of those two decisions is reversed." |
| `terminateWait` | every `terminate` | "From its start your account is suspended with no end date. Your profile and posts stay visible to others until the account is deleted. korbat deletes the account, with its posts and profile and every image it has not sealed, once this decision is final: when the six months for your appeal have passed unused, on {deadline}, or six months after korbat's answer if you reply before the start and korbat keeps the decision, or when your appeal is answered and the decision kept. Nothing is deleted while your appeal or your reply is unanswered." An announced termination prints the whole sentence; one taken at once leaves out the clause about a reply before the start. |
| `terminateLeave` | every `terminate` | "Until then you can export your data in Settings, Account. You can also delete the account yourself sooner; that ends your appeal with it, since nothing is left to restore. After the deletion korbat keeps nothing to recognise a new account of yours by. Images that korbat sealed, if it sealed any of yours, stay with it for the authorities alone, as the decision that sealed them said. A new account that carries on what this one was terminated for breaks korbat's terms." |
| `stops` | `suspend`, `terminate` | "While it runs you cannot post, reply, quote, like, repost, bookmark, follow, edit your profile, change your handle, upload images, ask for the verified badge, or report under korbat's rules." |
| `stays` | `suspend`, `terminate` | "You can still sign in and read; see your cases and reply to korbat; appeal; ask for a re-examination of a report you sent; export your data; delete your posts or your account; undo likes, reposts, bookmarks and follows; block and mute; and report illegal content. Your profile and posts stay visible to others, without any mark, and others can still follow you and like, repost, reply to and quote your posts." |
| `explore` | `suspend`, `terminate` | "While it runs your posts do not appear in Explore, and your likes and reposts of other people's posts do not count there. Both count again from the first refresh after it ends; a post whose time in Explore has passed by then does not return." |
| `retire` | `retire_handle` | "korbat retired the handle @{handle}: nobody can use it again, you included. If your profile still held it, it now shows @{placeholder} instead, and you can choose a new handle in Settings, Profile, without the usual wait: at once, or, if this decision also suspends your account at once, when that suspension ends." The last clause is printed only when the decision itself holds a `suspend` at once, and for a decision that also holds a `terminate` at once the sentence ends after "instead."; both are read from the decision's own measures, so it reads as sent. |
| `retireAlready` | `noEffect` `already` | "That handle was already retired, so this changes nothing now." |
| `warn` | `warn_reporting` | "korbat warns you about your reports. Nothing is restricted. The facts below say how many of your reports under korbat's rules a moderator marked as manifestly unfounded, and which. This warning counts until {endsAt}." Then `warnNext` (W.3). |
| `restrict` | `restrict_reporting` | "korbat restricted your reports under its rules for {days} days, until {endsAt}: until then it takes no report from your account that says something breaks a korbat rule. It follows korbat's warning of {warnedAt}, and ends at once if that warning is lifted; the facts below name the reports you sent after it that were marked." |
| `reportingOpen` | both reporting measures | "Notices of illegal content, reports of child sexual abuse material and requests for re-examination are never counted and never restricted." |

A measure whose `noEffect` is `deleted` adds 3c's `deletedAccount`. A termination's `stops` and `explore` begin "From its start" in place of "While it runs", and its `explore` ends after its first sentence. "1 day" and "1 active strike" print in the singular.

**What was done: reversals and answers.**

| Key | For | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| `overruled` | `overrule` | "korbat overruled the strike that decision gave. It no longer counts." |
| `basisEnded` | each entry of `endedStanding` that is a `suspend` or `terminate` | "The suspension of your account that korbat decided on {decidedAt} rested on that decision." (or "The termination ..."; when the reversal lifts a seal: "... rested on that seal.") Then one sentence by the state of its period at this decision's time: `not_started` "It will not start."; `running` "It ended with this one."; `ended` "It had run from {startedAt} to {endedAt}; it no longer counts toward a later suspension." |
| `basisEndedReply` | after `basisEnded`, when that measure had a reply nobody had answered | "Your reply of {filedAt} to it needs no answer now." |
| `basisEndedReporting` | an entry of `endedStanding` that is a `restrict_reporting` | "The restriction of your reports that korbat decided on {decidedAt} rested on that warning. It ended with this decision: you can report under korbat's rules again." (when its days had already run out: "It had run from {startedAt} to {endedAt}.") |
| `liftedBefore` | `lift_suspension`, period `not_started` | "korbat lifted the suspension it announced on {decidedAt}: it never starts." |
| `liftedRunning` | `lift_suspension`, period `running` | "korbat lifted the suspension of your account: it ended with this decision." |
| `liftedEnded` | `lift_suspension`, period `ended` | "korbat lifted the suspension of your account that ran from {startedAt} to {endedAt}: it no longer counts toward a later suspension." |
| `withdrawn` | `lift_suspension` signed `replaced`, in place of `replyReversed`, `liftedBefore` and D 10's lead for a reversal | "korbat withdrew the suspension it announced on {decidedAt}, because it is taking another measure on your account. This is not a decision on your reply." (the last sentence only when a reply was filed; "the termination" for a `terminate`) |
| `unretired` | `unretire_handle` | "korbat lifted the retirement of the handle @{handle}. If your profile still held the placeholder, it has the handle again. If you chose another handle since, you can change back to @{handle} at once in Settings, Profile. Nobody else could take it meanwhile." |
| `reportingLifted` | `lift_reporting` | "korbat lifted its warning about your reports: it no longer counts." or "korbat lifted the restriction of your reports: you can report under korbat's rules again." (for a restriction whose days had run out: "korbat lifted the restriction of your reports that ran until {endedAt}.") |
| `replyKeptOnTime` | a decision that answers an on-time reply and leaves the measure in force | "korbat read your reply to its decision of {decided} and keeps that decision. The suspension of {days} days starts at {startsAt} and ends at {endsAt}: with this answer, and never before the time you were first given." The appeal's new date is in the redress paragraph (`replyAnswered`, W.3). |
| `replyKeptLate` | a decision that answers a late reply and leaves the measure in force | "korbat read your reply to its decision of {decided} and keeps that decision." Then, by the period's state: `running` "The suspension goes on and ends at {endsAt}, as before."; `ended` "The suspension ended at {endedAt}, as decided." |
| `replyReversed` | a correction `reversed` that answers a reply and is not signed `replaced` | "korbat read your reply to its decision of {decided} and reversed that decision." (or "reversed part of that decision") Then `liftedBefore`, `liftedRunning` or `liftedEnded`. |
| `replyAlso` | a review that also answers a reply | after D 10's `reviewed` lead: "This also answers your reply of {filedAt}." When the review leaves the measure in force (its outcome is `kept`, or `partly_reversed` without `lift_suspension`), the second sentence of `replyKeptOnTime` or of `replyKeptLate` follows; its redress is the review's, as D 10 has it. |

For a termination the kept answers read "The termination starts at {startsAt}: with this answer, and never before the time you were first given." and "The termination stays."; `liftedEnded` has no form for one, since a termination has no end.

**Where and how long** (in place of 3c's `scope.final`, which says "has no end date"):

- `scope.suspend`: "This applies everywhere korbat is available, for {days} days from its start."
- `scope.terminate`: "This applies everywhere korbat is available and has no end date."
- `scope.reporting`: "This applies everywhere korbat is available, until {endsAt}."
- `scope.warn`: "This restricts nothing."
- A decision that holds a strike or a retirement keeps `scope.final`.

**Automation.** 3c's sentence ("No automated means were used to find this or to decide it.") stands for a decision that retires a handle and for one on the basis `threat`, `terrorist`, `threat_repeat` or `protective`. The others say what the software did and no more:

- `automationStrike`, a decision that gives a strike: "No automated means were used to find this or to decide that it breaks the rule. That the decision gives a strike follows from korbat's published rule, which its software applies."
- `automationCounted`, basis `strikes`, `ladder_end` or `reports`: "korbat's software counted what this rests on and listed your account for a moderator. A moderator read it and decided; no automated means decided it."
- `automationFlood`, basis `flood`, for which no list exists: "korbat's software counted the posts this rests on. An admin read them and decided; no automated means decided it."

**The rule or the term** (in place of D 10's one sentence, "It breaks korbat rule {id}, "{title}": {text}", which stays for every decision that holds no standing measure). A decision that holds a standing measure names the ground it truly rests on, chosen by the measure's `basis`. The numbers in these sentences are literals of the redress version, held to `STANDING_RULES` by the table test, as W.3's are.

| Key | Basis | Paragraph |
|---|---|---|
| `ground.ladder` | `strikes` | "This rests on korbat's terms, section Suspension and termination: an account with 3 active strikes may be suspended for a limited time. This decision finds no new breach. The newest of the strikes it rests on was given under korbat rule {id}, "{title}"." |
| `ground.ladderEnd` | `ladder_end` | "This rests on korbat's terms, section Suspension and termination: after three suspensions in 365 days an account with 3 active strikes may be terminated. This decision finds no new breach. The newest of the strikes it rests on was given under korbat rule {id}, "{title}"." |
| `ground.protective` | `protective` | "This rests on korbat's terms, section Suspension and termination: when korbat keeps images of an account sealed after a report of child sexual abuse material, an admin may suspend the account once, for up to 14 days. The rule such a report is made under is korbat rule R2, "{title}". korbat has not found that your account broke it." |
| `ground.conduct` | `threat`, `terrorist`, `threat_repeat`, `flood`, `reports` | D 10's sentence with "Your account's conduct breaks" in place of "It breaks" (for `terrorist`, D 10's sentence for the law, unchanged). For `threat`, `terrorist` and `threat_repeat` the `basis.*` or `terminateAtOnce` sentence above names the decision that found it. |

So no statement of a protective suspension holds "It breaks", and a step of the ladder is never worded as a finding of spam, or of whatever rule its newest strike happened to cite.

**What section 10's `DecisionStatement` must carry for these sentences**, all read from the ledger and none from a later event: on a measure `days`, `announced`, `basis`, `earliestStart`, `endsAt`, `restsOn` (each with `decidedAt`, `ruleId`, `postCreatedAt`; two entries on `threat_repeat`, the older first; on `protective` the seal's decision, with its `decidedAt` and with `ruleId` and `postCreatedAt` null), `activeThen`, `handle`, `placeholder`, `warnedAt`; on a reversal the lifted measure's kind, `decidedAt`, `handle`, `replaced` and, in place of the boolean `started`, `periodThen`: the state of the measure's period at this decision's time, `not_started`, `running` (with `startedAt`) or `ended` (with `startedAt` and `endedAt`); `endedStanding` (the measures whose basis a reversal of this decision broke, a `restrict_reporting` among them, each with its kind, `decidedAt`, `periodThen`, `seal`, which says whether the broken basis was a seal, and `replyFiledAt` when its reply was unanswered); `repliedTo` (`filedAt`, `onTime`, the measure's kind and `days`, the `startsAt` and `endsAt` this answer fixes, `periodThen`, and `appealUntil`) on a decision that answers a reply.

### W.3 Redress version 2 (`REDRESS_TEXTS[2]`; `REDRESS_VERSION` becomes 2)

`appeal`, `interim` and `notAppealable` are version 1's, word for word. Six texts join; the numbers in them are literals of this version, and the table test holds each to `STANDING_RULES` (a changed number is a new version, so old statements keep theirs).

- `strikeNext`: "A strike restricts nothing by itself. When an account has 3 active strikes, a moderator may decide to suspend it for a limited time or, after three suspensions in 365 days, an admin may decide to terminate it. You would be told first and could reply before anything is restricted."
- `reply`: "Before it starts you can reply: choose "Reply" on this decision's screen under Settings, Cases, and say anything you want the moderator to weigh. A reply sent before the start holds the start until a moderator has read it and answered you; if the decision is kept, it then starts with the answer, and never before the time you were given. A reply sent later is read and answered too, and can end this measure, but does not pause it. You can reply once. Replying does not use up your appeal."
- `replyAtOnce`: "You can reply at any time while it runs: choose "Reply" on this decision's screen under Settings, Cases. A moderator reads your reply and answers you, and can end this measure at once; the reply does not pause it. You can reply once. Replying does not use up your appeal." ("This measure" serves a suspension and a termination alike, so the text has one form.)
- `replyAnswered`: "This answer is not appealed by itself. The appeal is against the decision it keeps: once, free of charge, until {deadline}, with "Appeal" on that decision's screen under Settings, Cases. The six months for it count from this answer when you replied before the start, and otherwise from the day you were told of that decision. You can also go to court, or complain to the Digital Services Coordinator of the EU country where you live (Article 53 of the Digital Services Act)." `{deadline}` is `repliedTo.appealUntil`, read from the ledger at this answer's time; when it has already passed (a late reply answered after the six months) the paragraph reads `notAppealable` in its place.
- `warnNext`: "If 3 of the reports you send after this warning are marked that way while it counts, a moderator may restrict your reports under korbat's rules for up to 30 days."
- `appealNow`: "You can appeal from now; you do not have to wait for the start. An appeal does not hold the start: only a reply sent before the start does."

Which text a decision gets, from its own kind, outcome and measures:

| Decision | Redress paragraphs, in order |
|---|---|
| final `restricted` with an announced `suspend` or `terminate` | `reply`; `appeal`; `appealNow` |
| final `restricted` with a `suspend` or `terminate` at once, a protective one among them | `replyAtOnce`; `appeal`. A protective suspension's decision holds no seal, so D 10's sentence that the appeal of a seal waits for the authorities is not added: its appeal is reviewed at once |
| any other final `restricted` (a strike, a retirement, a reporting measure among them) | `appeal` |
| correction `kept` that answers a reply | `replyAnswered`, its `{deadline}` being `repliedTo.appealUntil`; `notAppealable` when that date has passed |
| correction `kept` that only withdraws marks | none of its own: it restricts nothing and changes no outcome, and no author receives a statement for it |
| a correction that withdraws an announced measure (`replaced`) | as D 10 has a correction that lifts a measure |
| every other decision | as D 10 |

### W.4 Rulebook version 3 (`RULEBOOKS[3]`; `RULES_VERSION` becomes 3)

Version 2 with one change, to R6 (M 2 row 20):

- text, added at the end: "Sending report after report that is plainly unfounded is not allowed either: after a warning, korbat may stop taking an account's reports under these rules for a limited time. A notice of illegal content is never restricted."
- `notAllowed` gains "Reporting the same account again and again for posts that plainly break no rule"; `allowed` gains "Reporting in good faith something that turns out to break no rule".

### W.5 The composer's drafts (`STANDING_DRAFTS`, `packages/contracts/src/moderation.ts`)

The console fills the facts from the case answer, as it fills `SEAL_DRAFTS`; the moderator may edit them and writes the reason, except where a reason is given here. Their numbers are those of the moment the console read the case, and each draft that holds a count says so ("when this was written", "up to {readDay}", an interval with both ends): the key signs the text, the database checks its own numbers at the commit, and a strike that expires or a report that arrives in between must not leave a signed sentence false beside the statement's own list. So they belong in a signed text, not in a sentence of W.2. `{readDay}` is the UTC day of the case answer.

| Key | Draft |
|---|---|
| `facts.strikes` | "When this was written the account had {activeStrikes} active strikes. Each was given by a decision that found a breach of a korbat rule in a post, and each of those decisions was sent to the account with its reasons." |
| `facts.ladder_end` | "When this was written the account had been suspended {n} times in the last {ladderMemoryDays} days, the last time by korbat's decision of {day}, and had {activeStrikes} active strikes again, one of them given since." |
| `facts.threat` | "A final decision of korbat found a direct threat of violence in a post of this account." |
| `facts.terrorist` | "A final decision of korbat found terrorist content in a post of this account." |
| `facts.threat_repeat` | "A final decision of korbat found a direct threat of violence in a post that this account made after korbat had suspended it for an earlier threat." |
| `facts.flood` | "The account made {posts} posts between {from} and {to}." The admin adds what made them repetitive and unsolicited: the count alone is no flood. |
| `facts.protective` | "On {sealedOn} an admin of korbat decided to keep images of this account sealed, after they were reported as possible child sexual abuse material. korbat's staff do not open images reported this way, so korbat has not assessed them." |
| `reason.protective` | "An admin found the report credible enough to keep the images sealed and to report them to the police, who can assess them. korbat makes no finding that this account broke the law or a korbat rule. It stops the account from writing for a short, fixed time." |
| `facts.warn` | "In the {frameDays} days up to {readDay} the account sent {reports} reports under korbat's rules. A moderator marked {unfounded} of them as manifestly unfounded: {marked}." |
| `facts.restrict` | "Of the reports the account sent between korbat's warning of {warnedOn} and {readDay}, a moderator marked {marksSince} as manifestly unfounded: {marked}." |

`{posts}`, `{from}` and `{to}` are `standing.flood`, `{sealedOn}` is `standing.seal.decidedOn` (8.1), and `{n}` and `{day}` in `facts.ladder_end` are `standing.suspensionsInYear` and `standing.lastSuspendedOn` (10.3). Every count in the two reporting drafts is a count of reports, a report once however many of its items were marked, and by the day the report was sent. `{marked}` lists those reports by what korbat generated: "report {ref} of {day} (item {n})", with the day the report was sent (`sentOn`, 10.3) and each marked item's number. `reason.protective` is 3c's `SEAL_DRAFTS.reason.final` in its first sentence, which is true of the seal for good the suspension rests on; it holds no "until an admin has looked into the report", since nobody looks into anything in those days. Under a protective draft the composer shows D 8's hint in the words of rule 4 above, "Never write that the images are child sexual abuse material: korbat has not assessed them." (D 8's own sentence ends "nobody at korbat has seen them", which is more than korbat knows), and adds "Do not write that the account posted such material, and promise no later look by staff."

### W.6 Mails

Seven templates join the mailer's closed union (`apps/api/src/mail/mailer.ts`), each with a `case` in `renderTemplate` and an entry in the templates test. Six are a decision's statement mail under a subject and a lead of their own, and join `AuthForModeration.mailAccount`'s union; the seventh, `termination_final`, is a flat template of the auth module and no member of that union (below). `sendStatement` (`apps/api/src/moderation/statement.ts:109`) picks the template with `mailTemplateOf(statement)` in the order 7.15 states, which the table below follows (`suspension_lifted`, 7.15's fourth row, has a row here for each `why`), and `moderation_decision` otherwise, so a strike, a retirement, a lifted reporting measure and their reversals ride on 3c's mail and subjects. Each of the six is built as `moderation_decision` is: the lead, the statement's paragraphs, "Read it in korbat under Settings, Cases. korbat's emails never contain a link to act on a decision.", and `userMail`'s closing sentence. One decision still gets one mail, recorded in `author_mail`.

| Template {fields} | Sent for | Subject | Lead |
|---|---|---|---|
| `suspension_announced` {measure, days, window, earliestStart, statement} | a final decision with an announced `suspend` or `terminate` | "korbat has decided to suspend your account: you can reply first" (or "... to terminate your account: ...") | "korbat has decided to suspend your korbat account for {days} days. Nothing is restricted yet. The suspension starts {window} hours after korbat sent this email, and not before {earliestStart}." "You can reply before it starts: sign in to korbat, where this decision is always shown and a banner on every screen gives the exact time and a Reply button. A reply sent before {earliestStart} is certainly in time, and holds the start until a moderator has answered you." "The decision and its reasons follow." |
| `suspension_started_at_once` {measure, days, endsAt, statement} | a final decision with a `suspend` or `terminate` at once | "korbat suspended your account" (or "korbat terminated your account") | "korbat suspended your korbat account for {days} days, until {endsAt}. It did so at once, without notice beforehand; the reasons follow. You can reply and appeal in korbat from now: sign in and use the banner on any screen." |
| `reply_answered` {outcome, statement} | a correction or review whose statement has `repliedTo`: `outcome` is `kept` when it leaves the measure in force (a `partly_reversed` review that does so among them) and `reversed` when it lifts it | "korbat answered your reply" | `kept`: "A moderator read your reply and kept the decision. The answer and its reasons follow." `reversed`: "A moderator read your reply and reversed the decision. The answer follows." |
| `suspension_lifted` {measure, why: `replaced`, decidedAt, statement} | a correction whose `lift_suspension` is signed `replaced`; its statement has no `repliedTo` (10.2), so a withdrawal is never mailed as an answer to the reply it closes | "korbat withdrew the suspension it announced" (or "the termination") | "korbat withdrew the suspension of your korbat account that it announced on {decidedAt}, because it is taking another measure on your account. This is not a decision on your reply." (the last sentence only when a reply was filed) "The decision about the other measure reaches you separately." |
| `suspension_lifted` {measure, why: `lifted`, period, statement} | a review or correction that holds `lift_suspension` and answers no reply | "korbat lifted the suspension of your account" (or "the termination") | by `period` (the state at that decision's time): `not_started` "korbat lifted the suspension of your korbat account that it had announced: it never starts."; `running` "korbat lifted the suspension of your korbat account. This suspension no longer restricts your account."; `ended` "korbat lifted a past suspension of your korbat account: it no longer counts toward a later suspension." |
| `suspension_lifted` {measure, why: `basis`, period, statement} | a decision whose statement has a `suspend` or `terminate` in `endedStanding` (a restriction of reporting that ended with its warning rides on `moderation_decision`) | by `period`: `not_started` "A suspension of your account will not start"; `running` "A suspension of your account has ended"; `ended` "A past suspension of your account no longer counts" | `not_started` "korbat reversed a decision that the suspension it announced for your korbat account rested on. The suspension will not start."; `running` "The suspension of your korbat account has ended, because a decision it rested on was reversed. This suspension no longer restricts your account."; `ended` "korbat reversed a decision that a past suspension of your account rested on; that suspension no longer counts." |
| `reporting_warned` {statement} | a final decision with `warn_reporting` | "korbat warns you about your reports" | "korbat has looked at the reports your account sent under its rules and warns you about them. Nothing is restricted. The reasons, the reports concerned and what may follow are below." |
| `reporting_restricted` {days, endsAt, statement} | a final decision with `restrict_reporting` | "korbat restricted your reports under its rules" | "korbat takes no reports under its rules from your account for {days} days, until {endsAt}. You can still report illegal content. The reasons follow." |

For a termination the leads of the first two read "to terminate your korbat account" and "korbat terminated your korbat account", without days or an end.

**`termination_final`** {decided, imagesSealed} is no decision's mail and no member of `mailAccount`'s union: the moderation module never sends it. It is a flat template of the auth module. `takeDownTerminated(account, facts)` reads the account's address, takes the account down, and only after that commit sends it to the address it read with `notifyAccount(deps, email, "termination_final", { decided, imagesSealed })` (from `facts.decidedAt` and `facts.imagesSealed`, 7.8 step 3), best effort, exactly where 1b's `confirmDeletion` sends `account_deleted` (`apps/api/src/auth/flows/deletion.ts:79-83`) and in its place. So it goes once, and only for an account that was taken down: an account whose termination was lifted between the round's two reads gets none, and a take-down that fails mails nothing and is tried again. The take-down does not replace the address (`finishPurge` does, later: `apps/api/src/auth/repo.ts:511-527`), so no earlier moment is needed. A failed send holds nothing.

- Subject: "korbat has closed your terminated account".
- "korbat's decision of {decided} to terminate your korbat account is final: the time to appeal has passed, or your appeal was answered and the decision kept, and no reply of yours is unanswered."
- "korbat has closed the account and is now deleting its posts and profile and every image it has not sealed, as that decision said. This cannot be undone. The handle stays reserved, so nobody else can take it."
- "korbat keeps nothing to recognise a new account of yours by. A new account that carries on what this one was terminated for breaks korbat's terms." When `imagesSealed` is true, between the two sentences: "Images of yours that korbat sealed stay with it for the authorities alone, as the decision that sealed them said."
- `userMail`'s closing sentence.

**Changed staff mails.**

- `moderation_waiting` gains the optional pair `replyCount`, `replyOldest` and the line "{count} replies to a suspension or termination have waited more than 24 hours, the oldest since {oldest}. A reply sent before the start holds its suspension until someone answers."
- `moderation_urgent` and `moderation_waiting` end "Until the phone alerts of sub-project 3f, this email is the only alert." (and "... the only reminder."): the phone alerts moved to 3f with the orders.

**What O4 found in the code.** A decision's `author_mail` is never `folded`: the 24-hour fold is a receipt's state only, and `sendStatement` records `sent`, `failed` or `no_address`, never `suppressed` (`statement.ts:115-118`). `author_mail_at` is written by `recordAuthorMail` (`repo.ts:1457`) as the database's `now()` once the relay accepted the mail, only for `sent`. A mail still `pending` after five days is recorded `failed` (`repo.ts:1605`), which is before the earliest start. So the start is the relay's acceptance plus the window when the mail was sent, and decision plus `undelivered_hours` when the relay refused the address or the mail was given up; `folded` and `suppressed` in M 3.2's view are branches no decision reaches. `sendStatement` is called by the decision route after its commit and by the dispatch job, so M 3.6 item 4's lock belongs in `recordAuthorMail`, and in the give-up statement, which also writes `author_mail`. 3e changes one thing here: for a decision that holds an announced measure the time is stamped on every move out of `pending`, not only for `sent`, so that a mail given up after the dispatch job stood still for days starts the suspension at the give-up and never at a time already past (section 6).

**What "sent" means.** "Sent" is korbat's own relay taking the mail (`apps/api/src/mail/smtp.ts:55-65`), not the person's mail provider: korbat reads no bounce, so a notice that bounces later still starts after the window (flagged `(counsel)` in section 2). That is why no text here says "handed to your mail provider" or "delivered": each says "sent", and says that the app always shows the notice. `no_address` is what `mailAccount` answers for an account that is deleted or was never there (`apps/api/src/auth/flows/notifiers.ts:201-207`), so no notice of a living account is recorded so.

### W.7 The terms (`apps/app/src/lib/terms.ts`)

Draft, without a new `TERMS_VERSION`, as D 9's sections; counsel reviews them in sub-project 4. Each `{name}` is printed from `STANDING_RULES` ({d1}, {d2}, {d3} are `ladder_days`), and the table test holds each section to it. Two numbers in these sections are not members of `STANDING_RULES`, and the test holds each to the constant of the code that acts on it: the five days after which a mail that cannot be sent is given up (the dispatch job's, `repo.ts:1605`; `RETENTION.mailGiveUpDays`, 10.1), and the 24 hours korbat aims to answer a reply to a suspension taken at once in (the queue-age alert's, section 7; `REPLY_AIM_HOURS`, 10.1). The second is an aim that korbat publishes and not a promise it can keep with one or two people: flagged `(owner)`. That a start counts from korbat's sending, and that korbat reads no bounce, is flagged `(counsel)` (section 2).

**"Strikes"**

"When a moderator's final decision finds that a post of yours breaks a korbat rule, the decision also gives your account a strike. A strike restricts nothing by itself and is used for one thing: counting toward a suspension. It counts for {strike_days} days from its decision. A decision gives no strike when it rests on the law alone, when it concerns child sexual abuse material (rule R2), when it is about your profile and not a post, or while korbat is still deciding. A post gets at most one strike, and a post you made before you were told of your newest strike gets none, so several posts decided in one sitting count as one warning. A strike also counts when the post was already removed or deleted. The decision's statement tells you of the strike, until when it counts and how many you have; your strikes are listed under Settings, Cases. A strike falls only with its decision: appeal that decision, and if it is reversed the strike stops counting at once, and a suspension that rested on it ends at once if fewer than {ladder_strikes} of its strikes still stand. If that suspension was already over, it no longer counts toward a later one."

**"Suspension and termination"**

"When your account has {ladder_strikes} active strikes at once, a moderator may decide to suspend it: for at most {d1} days the first time, {d2} the second and {d3} the third, counting the suspensions decided in the last {ladder_memory_days} days. A moderator may choose fewer days, never more, and a moderator who chose fewer days still used that step. After a suspension, one further strike is enough for the next step while at least two earlier strikes still count. After three such suspensions in that time, whatever their length, the next step may be the termination of the account, which only an admin decides. Nothing happens automatically: a moderator reads the strikes and decides. Such a decision finds no new breach: its ground is this section, and its statement says so and lists the strikes it rests on.

korbat tells you first. The decision is always shown in the app with its reasons, and korbat also sends it to you by email. The suspension starts {reply_window_hours} hours after korbat sent that email. If korbat's mail service refuses your address or cannot send the email for five days, only the app can tell you, and the suspension starts {undelivered_hours} hours after the decision. korbat cannot see whether an email it sent reached you, so keep the address of your account current. The app shows the start as a date and time. If you reply before the start, it does not start until a moderator has read your reply and answered you; the answer reverses the decision, or keeps it, and then the suspension starts with the answer and never before the time you were first given. A reply sent later is read and answered too, and can end the suspension, but does not pause it. You can reply once, and replying does not use up your appeal: when korbat keeps a decision after a reply you sent before the start, your six months to appeal count again from that answer.

korbat suspends an account at once, before any reply, only in four cases, each decided by an admin: after a final decision that a post of the account is a direct threat of violence (rule R5), for up to {threat_days} days; after a final decision that a post of the account is terrorist content, for up to {terrorist_days} days; when an admin finds that the account is flooding korbat with repetitive, unsolicited posts (rule R6) and it made {flood_posts} or more posts within {flood_minutes} minutes, for up to {flood_days} days; and, as a protective suspension, when korbat keeps images of the account sealed after a report of child sexual abuse material, by a decision at most {basis_age_days} days old, once for each such decision and for up to {protective_days} days. In the last case korbat's staff do not open the images, so korbat has not assessed them: the suspension is a protective measure and not a finding that you broke the law or a rule, it gives no strike, counts toward no later step, and ends on its date, or earlier if the seal is lifted. Its appeal is reviewed at once and does not wait for the authorities. In the first two cases the suspension ends at once if the decision it rests on is reversed. An announced suspension never rests on one of these four cases. You get the statement at once and can reply and appeal from the first minute; korbat aims to answer a reply to a suspension taken at once within 24 hours.

While suspended you cannot post, reply, quote, like, repost, bookmark, follow, edit your profile, change your handle, upload images, ask for the verified badge, or report under korbat's rules. You can still sign in and read, see your cases, reply to korbat, appeal, ask for a re-examination, export your data, delete your posts or your account, undo likes, reposts, bookmarks and follows, block and mute, and report illegal content. Your profile and posts stay visible, without any mark, and others can still follow you and react to your posts. Your posts do not appear in Explore while you are suspended, and your likes and reposts do not count there.

A termination is decided after three suspensions as described above, with the same time to reply, or at once after a direct threat of violence (rule R5) that you post within {ladder_memory_days} days after korbat suspended your account for an earlier one, while that suspension and the decision it rests on still stand. A second post from before that suspension is no ground for it. Until the decision is final the account is suspended and nothing more. It is final when the six months for your appeal have passed unused, or when your appeal is answered and the decision kept; nothing is deleted while your appeal or a reply of yours is unanswered. korbat then deletes the account with its posts and profile and every image it has not sealed. Until then you can export your data and delete the account yourself; deleting it ends your appeal, since nothing is left to restore. After the deletion korbat keeps nothing to recognise a new account of yours by; images that korbat sealed stay with it for the authorities alone, as the decision that sealed them said.

Each of these decisions can be appealed from the moment you are told, as Appeals says; an appeal does not hold a start, only a reply does. One person may hold more than one account, but not to carry on with another account what one was suspended or terminated for: korbat treats that as the same conduct and decides about it like any other breach, on what the account does."

**"Reporting in bad faith"**

"Report what you believe breaks a rule; a report that turns out wrong costs you nothing. korbat acts only on reports that are manifestly unfounded: reports that anyone could see name nothing a rule could cover, without weighing them. Examples are many reports of the same account for posts that plainly break no rule, the same text sent against different posts, and a report sent again after it was answered. A moderator may mark such a report when deciding it. You see the mark on your list of reports together with the report's outcome, and you can ask for a re-examination. The mark stops counting if a re-examination finds a breach, and if korbat withdraws it because the report was arguable after all. korbat counts reports, not the posts or accounts a report names: a report counts once, by the day you sent it, however many of its items were marked, so no single report can lead to a warning.

If at least {report_warn_marks} of the reports you sent under the rules within {report_frame_days} days are marked, and they are at least half of the reports you sent under the rules in that time, a moderator may warn you: the warning lists the reports and restricts nothing. If {report_restrict_marks} of the reports you send after that warning are marked while it counts, which is {report_frame_days} days, a moderator may stop taking your reports under the rules for up to {report_restrict_days} days; the restriction ends at once if the warning is lifted. A person decides each step, looking at how many reports you sent, how many were marked and what they said; the count alone decides nothing. Notices of illegal content, reports of child sexual abuse material and requests for re-examination are never counted and never restricted. You can appeal the warning and the restriction."

**"Handles"**

"When the breach lies in a handle itself (impersonation, someone's private information, a threat), a moderator may retire it. A retired handle can be used by nobody again, you included. Your profile then shows a placeholder that begins with retired_, and you can choose a new handle without the usual wait: at once, or, while your account is suspended, when the suspension ends. If the decision is reversed, the retirement is lifted: your profile gets the handle back if it still shows the placeholder, and otherwise you can change back to it at once; nobody else could take it meanwhile. Handles that begin with retired_ cannot be chosen. korbat keeps a retired handle, as a handle and nothing else, for as long as it refuses it to everyone, also when the breach was someone's private information (rule R4): refusing it needs it."

**Changed sentences.**

- "Who can use korbat" (`terms.ts:15`): "... but not to evade a suspension." becomes "... but not to carry on what another of their accounts was suspended or terminated for (Suspension and termination)."
- "Your data" gains: "korbat keeps the record of every strike, suspension, termination, warning and retired handle with its decision, as it keeps every decision. What ends is its use: a strike counts for {strike_days} days, a suspension counts toward the next one for {ladder_memory_days} days, a warning about reporting for {report_frame_days} days. The text of a reply to a suspension is erased once korbat has answered it and its decision can no longer be appealed; a reply that is not answered yet is never erased. To decide about reporting in bad faith korbat counts, for each account and day, how many reports under its rules the account sent, and keeps which of them a moderator marked manifestly unfounded; both are deleted after {report_frame_days} days and with the account, so for that long a marked report stays linked to the account that sent it. A retired handle is kept as a handle (Handles). Your export holds all of this."
- D 9's sentence that korbat forgets which account sent a report 30 days after its last outcome gains ", except through the mark of a report found manifestly unfounded (Reporting in bad faith)".
- "How moderators decide" lists the measures from `MEASURES`, so the six new labels join it; "Appeals" gains "An appeal never holds the start of a suspension; a reply does. When korbat keeps a decision in answer to a reply you sent before its start, the six months to appeal it count from that answer. The appeal of a protective suspension is reviewed at once; only the appeal of a decision that seals images waits for the authorities."
- The section of D 9 that explains each outcome phrase to a person who reported (D 12's table test holds the terms to every phrase) gains the meaning of `ACCOUNT_MEASURE_SCOPE`: "When korbat's answer to a report about an account is a measure on that account, korbat tells you that it took a measure, and not which or for how long. korbat does not tell you what happens to that account afterwards."

`RETENTION` gains `replyText` ("until it is answered and its decision is final for its author"), `reportCountDays` ({report_frame_days}) and `standingRecord` ("kept; counted for {strike_days}, {ladder_memory_days} and {report_frame_days} days"), which the retention list prints (M 3.5).

### W.8 The export's members

`ExportFormat.moderation` (D 7.11) gains, for the account's own data only:

| Member | Holds |
|---|---|
| `strikes` | one entry a strike: `decisionId`, `postId`, `ruleId`, `givenAt`, `countsUntil`, `overruledAt` (null while it stands) |
| `standing` | one entry a suspension, termination, warning or reporting restriction: `decisionId`, `kind`, `basis`, `days`, `announced`, `decidedAt`, `earliestStart`, `startsAt`, `endsAt`, `liftedAt`, `liftedBy` (a decision id), `replaced` (the lift was a withdrawal for another measure), and `basisEndedAt`, `basisEndedBy` when a reversal broke what it rested on |
| `replies` | one entry a reply: `decisionId`, `filedAt`, `onTime`, `text` (null once erased, with `erasedAt`; never erased before it is answered), `answeredAt`, `answeredBy` |
| `reporting` | `frameDays`; `days` (each `day` with its `reports`, the number of rule reports sent that day); `marks` (each `ref`, `position`, `sentOn`, `markedAt`, `withdrawnAt`, `counts`); `markedReports` (the number of reports in the frame that hold a mark that counts, which is what a threshold reads) |

The export's `handleHistory` entries gain `retiredAt`. Nothing here names another person: a mark is listed by the reference and position of the person's own report.

### W.9 The command line

`grantRole.standing`, which `grant-role` prints when it refuses a promotion (section 7): "This account holds a suspension, a termination or a restriction of reporting that stands and has not ended, so it cannot be made staff. Nothing was changed. If the measure was wrong, an admin lifts it with a correction first."

## 19. Amendments from the dry run (2026-10-12)

The dry run built this design task by task in a scratch clone (2026-10-09 to 2026-10-12). Where the code had to differ from the text above, or the text left something open that the code settled, the rule the code follows is recorded here, in the order of the tasks that settled it (Tasks 1 to 7); where a bullet and an earlier section disagree, the bullet is the later and verified word. Migration numbers: 3c's review added `0019_redaction_scope.sql`, so each of this design's five migrations is one higher than sections 6 and 13 write: the schema migration is `0020_fearless_absorbing_man.sql`, then `0021_standing_ledger.sql`, `0022_reporting_counts.sql`, `0023_explore_resume.sql` and `0024_standing_retention.sql`, and `--upgrade-from` names 19. The review after the last task adds its own amendments below.

- **Migration numbers (6, The numbers).** The schema migration is 0020_fearless_absorbing_man.sql (drizzle's own name) and standing_ledger is 0021_standing_ledger.sql, one higher than the design's 0019 and 0020, because 3c's last task added 0019_redaction_scope.sql; --upgrade-from names 19.
- **A strike's row names the measures that rest on it (10.3, 8.7).** CaseDecisionAction gains restedOnBy: on a strike, the kinds (suspend, terminate) of the account's standing measures that rest on the strike's decision and still stand (no reversal lifted them, standing_basis_broken_at is null); null on every other kind. A post case's answer otherwise carries nothing from which the console could tell that [review.rests] applies, since the suspension is a measure of the account's case.
- **The author's list carries the active strikes (9.4, 10.3).** AuthorCasesPage gains activeStrikes, the count of active_strikes for the account. Task 1 builds the Cases block 'Your standing', while /me's standing arrives with Task 3; the block reads the page the screen already loads.
- **The flag that lifts the routes' list (7.1, 13 Task 1).** It is DecideOptions.allKinds, an optional last argument of the moderation module's decide and review, never of the routes; decideWith, reviewWith and the deciding world's decideAs pass it. Without it the list ROUTE_KINDS (3c's twelve kinds, strike and overrule) is checked before the transaction, and a kept correction, a non-empty unfounded and replaced true are refused invalid_decision.
- **A refusal raised at insert keeps its detail (7.1).** When the database refuses a decision while its rows are inserted, the ModerationError carries the exception's DETAIL as detail and the refusal is logged at info, so that tests and the operator can tell which check refused; the answer to the client is still the code alone.
- **The reversal in part that leaves only the strike (6.3, 7.11).** decision_holds refuses it with the detail 'partly_reversed: no measure of the reviewed decision but its strike is left in force.', so that the refusal says why lifting every other measure is no reversal in part: the strike falls only with the whole finding, and then the outcome is reversed and the overruling is signed.
- **forget_reporter and moderation_apply stay as 3c built them (13, Before Task 1).** Task 1 replaces decisions_record, decisions_apply, decision_actions_check, decision_actions_apply, decision_holds, review_needs_admin, appeals_check, decision_final_at, item_current_outcome and measures_in_force, and reads but does not replace forget_reporter (the W.7 exception for marked reports lands with the marks' counting in Task 5) or moderation_apply (the post columns hidden_at, restored_at and explore_from get their writer with Explore's resume in Task 6).
- **The reporting branches count marks inline until reporting_counts (6.3, 7.5).** decision_actions_check counts a report as marked when it holds a mark that no correction withdrew and that was sent within report_frame_days by UTC day ((reported_at at time zone UTC)::date > today minus report_frame_days); Task 5's reporting_record must count the same way or replace these branches.
- **Statement kinds not yet worded throw (W.2).** measureParagraph throws for suspend, terminate, retire_handle, warn_reporting, restrict_reporting, lift_suspension, unretire_handle and lift_reporting, whose words come with later tasks, so that no statement can silently drop a measure; strike and overrule are worded.
- **StatementMeasure's endsAt and activeThen are optional (10.3, W.2).** Both are optional members, so that statements of redress version 1 and 3c's data parse unchanged; the strike's paragraph throws when either is missing.
- **The console's preview of a strike (8.2, W.2).** previewOf adds the strike the params will carry, with endsAt now plus strikeDays and activeThen from the author's band plus one (0, 1, 2 and 3 or more give 1, 2, 3 and 4). The page reads the standing in bands (D 8), so for 3 or more the preview names a lower bound; the statement the author receives holds the database's count.
- **The strike line's place (8.2).** The line stands inside the 'When' fieldset under Restrict, for both kinds, so that it follows the kind, the ground and the rule the moderator picks; on an account case and for an outcome without a measure there is none.
- **review_needs_admin's measure at once (6.3).** The clause reads a.announced IS FALSE, so that a row of 3c's kinds, whose announced is null, never makes a review an admin's through it.
- **The terms keep 3c's measures until Task 7 (W.7, 13 Task 7).** terms.ts lists only the measures of a post, its images and a profile's fields (and their reversals); the six standing measures and their reversals join the measures' sentences with Task 7's sections, since 3c's table test (no strike and no suspension in the terms) holds until the terms explain them. The table tests filter the contracts' kinds the same way.
- **Fixtures that move the clock (12).** ageStandingForTest(migrateDb, accountId, interval) moves the account's decisions, ledger rows, appeals, replies and marks back, and also its posts' created_at and deleted_at, so that strike_due's 'posted after the person was told' reads as it would have; strikeForTest(apiDb, accountId, n, rule = R1) commits n real removals one after the other through the decide route's own code, each of a post made after the previous removal committed, because decisions in one transaction share now() and would tie.
- **The profile's column grants and 3c's tests (6.1).** With UPDATE granted on eleven columns only, 3c's account-deletion test ages profiles.created_at as the migrator, not as the API role.
- **measures_in_force compares kinds as text (6, the enum-literal rule).** The replaced view compares kind::text with its literals, so 3c's schema test pins the kinds with either cast.
- **text-verify reads 3e's params (10.4; 3c's 7.13).** staff.js text-verify compares each decision's audit row with its ledger rows by kind, image, days and whether the measure was announced, its marks with the params' unfounded and a withdrawal with replaced; a row signed before 3e, which carries neither member, reads as no marks and false. Without this every decision of 3e would be reported as differing from its audit row.
- **keys and the profile's column grants (6.1).** With UPDATE granted on eleven columns of profiles and no table-wide right, the API role can no longer take ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on profiles (42501), so it cannot stall keys' read of the handles at all; 3c's keys test now asserts that refusal and holds the lock as the migrator to show that keys still gives up after 10 seconds on the API role's read.
- **The export's retired handles (W.8).** The design says that the export's handleHistory entries gain retiredAt. handleHistory is a list of plain strings in korbat-export-1, whose members are only ever added, and four readers parse it as such. So the entries stay strings and the profile gains one member after showInExplore, last in the profile: retiredHandles, one entry { handle, retiredAt } for each handle of the account's history that is retired now (empty when none is). A retired handle still appears in handleHistory, and a reversal takes it out of retiredHandles.
- **The three ends of the retirement sentence (W.2, 7.10 step 3).** W.2 prints the clause 'at once, or, if this decision also suspends your account at once, when that suspension ends' whenever the decision holds a suspension taken at once. The code follows 7.10 step 3 instead and prints one end, read from the decision's own measures (retireEnd in @korbat/rules): 'without the usual wait: at once.' for most decisions, an announced suspension or termination included, since neither has started when the statement is sent; 'without the usual wait: when that suspension ends.' when the decision suspends at once; and nothing after 'instead.' when it terminates at once. A sentence that says 'if this decision also suspends your account' when the decision is known to do so would not read as sent.
- **A retirement that changed nothing (W.2, retireAlready).** For a retirement whose noEffect is already or deleted, the statement prints the first sentence of retire ('korbat retired the handle @{handle}: nobody can use it again, you included.', STATEMENT_WORDS.retireLead) and then retireAlready or 3c's deletedAccount. It leaves out the clause about the placeholder and the new handle, since such a measure wrote no placeholder; StatementMeasure.placeholder is null for it.
- **The reporter's line after a retirement (7.11 item 7, W.1, 10.1).** NoticeItemStatus.scope and the input of outcomeWords gain the value account_retired. intake.ts (itemStatus) and dispatch.ts (updateLine) give it to an item about an account once its outcome is released, when the answering decision holds a retire_handle (the item rows gain retired, read like sealed); a suspect item keeps account_suspect, which speaks of the images. outcomeWords prints ACCOUNT_MEASURE_SCOPE.retired after a restricting outcome only: after partly_reversed and reversed it prints no scope sentence, since 'korbat retired the handle.' after 'korbat reversed its decision to restrict it.' would contradict the reversal. The phrase measure is built and held by its table test, and no scope reads it until Task 3 opens the suspensions taken at once.
- **What the console reads for the retirement (10.3).** CaseStanding gains snapshotHandleRetired: the handle of the case's newest snapshot is retired already, so a retirement now would have noEffect already; the composer keeps the control open and prints D 8's no-effect sentence, as 8.3 asks. CaseDecisionAction gains handle, present only on a retire_handle row: the handle it retired, from its decision's snapshot in lower case. The case page names it beside the row, and the preview of a correction or review that gives it back prints it, also once the decision's snapshot is erased and the page no longer shows it.
- **The preview's placeholder (8.3).** The database makes the placeholder from the measure's id, which does not exist before the decision is recorded. The preview prints retired_ and twelve x (PREVIEW_PLACEHOLDER, 20 characters): the placeholder's form and length, and plainly not one the author could meet. The statement the author receives prints the real one.
- **The statement's data for a retirement (10.2).** StatementMeasure gains handle and placeholder on a retire_handle, and announced on a suspend or terminate (Task 2 reads it for the sentence's end; the design lists it among the measure's members for Task 3); StatementReversal gains handle on an unretire_handle. All are optional, so redress 1 and 3c's data parse unchanged. The API reads the handle from the decision's snapshot (for a reversal, the lifted decision's), in lower case, and the placeholder with the database's own expression over the measure's id.
- **The refusals' shapes (10.5, 7.10 item 4).** ProfileStore.create answers reason handle_retired from its own check and from the guard, and handle_reserved when the guard refuses a placeholder's form that a caller let through; onboarding answers them 409 and 400. The social repository's HandleChangeResult gains the same two reasons, and the handle change answers them 409 and 400. The guard's code is read by handleRefusal, exported by the auth module's index beside isUniqueViolation. RETIRED_HANDLE_PREFIX and isRetiredPlaceholder live in packages/contracts/src/auth.ts, beside the handle rules, and checkHandle refuses every handle that begins with the prefix.
- **A placeholder left behind (7.10 step 5).** The handle change reserves in handle_history every handle it leaves, so a holder who moves on from the placeholder leaves it reserved for the account, and the export's handleHistory names it. Nobody else could take it anyway (the prefix is reserved), and the reversal still finds the profile by the placeholder it holds, so the code keeps the change as it was.
- **Lab E on the real code (12.3).** The retirements go through the decide route's code with a real confirmation each, made ready before the races, and the change back to the judged handle is the social repository's changeHandle, not the flow, whose 30-day wait would refuse it first. With 60 accounts, 30 of them racing: 60 retirements committed, 21 changes refused handle_retired, 9 races won by the change and then given the placeholder, 0 profiles left holding a retired handle; decisions p50 77 ms and p95 303 ms; busyPercent 3.
- **The lifted measure's members of a statement (10.2, W.2).** The design says that a reversal of the statement gains the lifted measure's kind and its decidedAt. StatementReversal already has a member kind, the reversal's own (lift_suspension), so the lifted measure's are liftedKind (suspend or terminate) and liftedDecidedAt; periodThen and replaced keep the design's names. All four are optional and null on every other reversal, so a statement stored before 3e still parses.
- **When the basis was decided, for the console's preview (10.3, CaseStanding).** The account case's standing names atOnce and seal but not when the decision a threat or a terrorist suspension would rest on was made, and W.2's basis.threat and basis.terrorist name that decision by its time. CaseStanding gains basisDecidedAt { threat, terrorist, protective }, each the time of the newest decision that fits decision_actions_check's row (a final R5 restriction, a final decision on the law marked terrorist content, a seal for good), read by standingFacts beside atOnce, so that the preview prints the sentence the statement will hold.
- **What rests on a decision, for a review or a correction (8.3 [review.basis] and [review.protective], 10.3).** CaseDecisionAction's restsOn points from a measure to the decisions it rests on; a review or a correction of one of those decisions needs the other direction before the key is asked. CaseDecision gains standingOn, absent when empty: [{ kind, basis }] for each suspension or termination that stands, whose basis holds and that names this decision in rests_on (standingOnDecisions in standing-repo.ts). The composer's correction and review views print [review.basis] or, for a protective one, [review.protective] from it; the notes sit in Composer.tsx, where the reversed decision is chosen, and Case.tsx does not change.
- **mailTemplateOf answers the whole mail (7.15, 7.12 step 1).** The design says sendStatement picks the template with mailTemplateOf(statement). The code's mailTemplateOf(statement, paragraphs) answers the whole DecisionMessage, a union of moderation_decision, suspension_started_at_once (measure, days, endsAt, paragraphs) and suspension_lifted (measure, why lifted or basis, the period's state, paragraphs), since each template's fields are read from the same statement that picks it; mailSubjectOf keeps answering the subject of moderation_decision alone. The rows for suspension_announced, reply_answered and the reporting templates come with Tasks 4 and 5.
- **/api/me's composition (5, 7.12).** As the design says, createApp composes /api/me from Auth.meFor(session) and the moderation module's standingFor(userId).
