The auth module's own handler stays, answering standing: null, so that an app built without the moderation module still answers the full MeResponse; meFor answers the response without standing (the type MeWithoutStanding).
- **Which reports a suspended reporter may not send (7.3, 7.9 step 7).** recordIn's first step refuses with account_suspended exactly a signed-in report under a korbat rule (ground kind user, no allegation of illegality, not the path for child sexual abuse material) whose reporter's row, locked by lockAccounts, is suspended; a notice of illegality, the path for child sexual abuse material and every signed-out report are unchanged. lockAccounts also answers reportsRestricted, which nothing reads until Task 5 adds reporting_restricted.
- **Lab B2 on the real code (5, the lock order, item 1; 12.3).** The design names followers' counter updates as the partner of the upgrade. With Task 3's follow and unfollow taking both rows FOR NO KEY UPDATE first, a follow never holds the subject's row FOR KEY SHARE, so the partner the lab found is a block of the subject by an account that follows it: its lockLive takes the row FOR KEY SHARE and its removal of the follow then updates follower_count. With the first lock left at FOR NO KEY UPDATE (the old lock, copied into the lab): 7 deadlocks in 36 suspensions, all of that pair, 4 of which reached the block's request (a block has no second try) and 3 the decision's own one retry, and a decision p95 of 2203 ms; with FOR UPDATE first (B1 and B5), 0 deadlocks and a p95 of 307 ms.
- **Lab B4 on the real code (5, the lock order, item 3; 12.3).** The design reports 30 deadlocks in 45 s with 3c's purge order (pgbench, the purge's share raised eight times). The lab on the real code, with 3c's purge copied into it, two of each subject's three followers purged while third accounts reply to, quote and like the subject's post and the subject is suspended at once FOR UPDATE, found 0 deadlocks in 36 suspensions and 72 purges (the deadlocks of its earlier runs were the lab's own, below). The new order stays as section 5 gives it: it costs one locking statement, and B5 with it found 0 deadlocks too.
- **A block against an unfollow (5, the lock order).** Section 5 reorders follow, unfollow, createPost and the purge but not block, which still takes both rows FOR KEY SHARE (lockLive) and then writes the follower_count of the account it blocks before the following_count of its own. The lab's third and fourth runs found that a block of an account by B, while that account unfollows B (lockPair, id order), deadlocks when B has the lower id; the unfollow's flow tries once more, the block's does not. The code leaves the block as 3c built it, since the design does not reorder it; the lab keeps the subject's follow target apart from the accounts that block it. A later task may give block lockPair's order or the one retry.
- **The labs' writers and the purge job's rounds (12.3).** The labs run the repository's own functions, with the flows' arguments: follows with the pair's guard, as graph.ts calls them, and follow, unfollow and createPost through onceMoreOnDeadlock, the flows' own function, each second try counted. A purged account writes nothing during its own purge (the take-down comes first, so no request of its own meets it; an early run that let the purged followers unlike found the unlike against the purge's removal of likes, a pair production cannot form). The purge job's next round is modelled as an immediate retry, at most five rounds (PURGE_ROUNDS) before a purge counts as lost; in production the job retries every round. Deadlocks are the database's own count over each run (pg_stat_database, read after the server processes have reported). B1 to B5 use 36 subjects (LAB_B_SUBJECTS), B6 and B7 30 accounts each.
- **The take-down holds the terminating decision's row, not its case (5, the lock order, item 5 step 2; 7.8 step 3; 13 Task 4 item 2).** The design says that takeDownDue takes 'the case of the terminate's decision FOR UPDATE, profile before case as in every decision'. korbat_api has no UPDATE privilege on cases, so a row lock on a case is refused (permission denied for table cases), and granting one, or a definer function that locks the case, would be migration 0022, which this task may not add. takeDownDue therefore takes, after the profile, the terminating decision's own row FOR UPDATE (lockTerminatingDecision in standing-repo.ts): an appeal being filed inserts a row whose foreign key takes that decision row FOR KEY SHARE, so it either commits first and is read by the second terminations_due(), or waits until the take-down has committed. Because appeals_check reads the deadline before that foreign key lock, an appeal whose deadline check passed an instant before the deadline could still be in flight; the take-down therefore also requires that the decision became final at least TAKE_DOWN_SETTLE_MS (60 seconds, finalSettled) before it acts, longer than any bounded lock wait of an appeal. A termination is thus taken down in the first purge round at least a minute after it became final. If the case lock is wanted, 0022 can grant it and the decision row lock can stay as a second guard.
- **takeDown answers whether it took the account down (5, ModerationForAccount.takeDownDue).** The design types the callback as (account, facts) => Promise<void>. The code's TakeDown answers Promise<boolean>: AuthForAccount.takeDownTerminated answers true only when it marked the account deleted (the auth repository's new takeDownTerminated marks a row not yet marked, writes the account_deleted event with metadata { terminated: true } and the staff side's audit rows with the server as actor, and answers null for a row already marked), so terminated counts the take-downs made, and a round that meets an account already taken down (its purge failed after the take-down) counts nothing and sends no second termination_final.
- **A failed take-down is logged with its case, not the account (5, the lock order, item 5).** The design says such a failure 'is caught, logged with the account and counted in failed'. No log line may carry an account id, so the log line names the terminating decision's case (caseId), from which an operator finds the account.
- **The withdrawal's statement knows whether a reply was closed (10.2 StatementReversal; W.2 withdrawn).** W.2's withdrawn sentence says that the withdrawal is no decision on the person's reply only when they replied, and 10.2 gives the lift's reversal liftedKind, liftedDecidedAt, periodThen and replaced but not the reply. StatementReversal gains replyFiledAt (optional, null when no reply was filed), read in the correction's transaction, so the statement reads the same whenever it is rendered.
- **DecisionView.standing carries the period and the reply, not the measure's kind and days (9.2, 10.2).** The design's DecisionView.standing is read by the app for the block 'Where it stands now'. The code's DecisionStanding holds state, earliestStart, startsAt, endsAt, endedAt, endedBy, replaced, restsOn (each strike with its rule, decidedAt, countsUntil, state and overruledAt; a decision a measure at once rests on with state null), reply (filedAt, onTime, text, erasedAt, answeredAt, answeredBy) and replyOpen; the kind, the days and the basis are read from the statement's measures (standingMeasureOf in apps/app/src/lib/standing.ts), which never change, so they are not sent twice.
- **The Standing screen's rows carry their case (8.6, 10.3 StandingLists).** 10.3 lists caseId for noticesUndelivered and terminationsWaiting only, while 8.6 says each row ends in a link to its case where one exists. atThreeStrikes and reportingRecords rows also carry caseId, the account's open account case or null, so the console links it beside 'Open a case on this account'. reportingRecords is empty until Task 5 builds the view reporting_record.
- **The basis of a termination at once, for the preview (10.3 CaseStanding.basisDecidedAt).** Task 3 gave basisDecidedAt threat, terrorist and protective. A termination at once rests on two R5 decisions, and W.2's terminateAtOnce names both by their times, so basisDecidedAt gains threatRepeat { earlier, newer } (optional; null when the ground is not met), read by standingFacts beside atOnce, so the console's preview prints the sentence the statement will hold.
- **Appeals of a standing measure lead the first page (8.5, 10.3 AppealsPage).** The design says /appeals lists first the appeals of a decision that holds a running suspension or a termination, then the rest as D 8 orders them. pendingAppeals is paged by a cursor over D 8's order, so the first group is read in full on the first page (at most a page of them, rows marked standingFirst) and excluded from the cursor's pages; a later page carries the rest in D 8's order. More than a page of such appeals at once would leave the excess in D 8's place.
- **A strike's row in the console's preview may have no post time (W.2 restsOnEntry).** The design's restsOnEntry names each strike with the post it was given for. The console builds its preview from the case answer, whose strikes carry no post's creation time, so restsOnEntry takes postCreatedAt null and then leaves the bracket out; the statement the database stores always has it.
- **The app's times: a day for a name ending in On, a minute for one ending in At (9, 9.1 to 9.3).** The design says times are printed by standingTime. The code prints {earliestStart}, {startsAt}, {endsAt} and {appealUntil} with standingTime (the device's minute and the UTC minute) and the names that end in On ({repliedOn}, {liftedOn}, {endedOn}, {answeredOn}, {erasedOn}) with standingDay, as the strike's [strike.overruled] already does; [reply.sent.onTime] and [reply.sent.late] print the minute the reply was received.
- **Two sentences of 'Where it stands now' the table leaves open (9.2).** For an announced termination [now.announced] says only 'Starts on {startsAt}.' since a termination has no end; for a protective suspension whose basis broke while it ran, [now.basis.running] reads 'Ended on {endedOn}, because korbat lifted the seal it rested on.' (the design's replacement phrase does not occur in that sentence). The link to the decision that ended a measure is worded 'Read the decision that did this', and the reply's link to its answer 'Read the answer'.
- **The reply screen's sent notice takes the focus as a focusable alert (9.3, 9.8).** The design says the sentences after Send reply are a Notice that receives focus. ui.tsx's Notice takes no ref, so the reply screen draws the same box as a focusable View with role alert and moves the focus to it once the answer arrives.
- **A locking statement is judged before it waits behind a holder that changes nothing (7.2 rule 1, 11 'A write is judged when it gets the row', 12.3 LAB C1).** The design says that a transaction that began before a start and waited for a lock is judged when it gets the row. Lab C1 on the real code confirms this behind a holder that changes the profile row (PostgreSQL re-reads the row and computes the predicate again: 150 wave writes, 0 admitted), but behind a holder that only locks the row FOR UPDATE and changes nothing (a decision about the account whose measure leaves the profile's columns as they are, a late reply, the take-down), a SELECT ... FOR KEY SHARE computes its target list, and an UPDATE its WHERE, before it waits: 22 of 72 such writes, judged before the start, committed after it, the latest 339 ms after, bounded by the holder's time. No write was judged at or after a start, so invariant 10 as 11 words it holds; the sentence 'judged when it gets the row' holds only behind a changing holder. Closing the gap would mean judging after the lock in a statement of its own (Task 3's write paths) and is left to the design's owner.
- **Labs C and D run in a block of their own with the notices world (12.3).** Labs C1, C2 and D are added to apps/api/test/lab/standing.lab.ts in a describe of their own, which builds the notices world so that each announced suspension's notice is mailed and its start fixed, then brings the start 1.5 s ahead with ageStandingForTest. C2's predicate reading now() is copied into the lab as nowGatedWrite. They run alone with -t 'labs? [CD]'; LAB_C_SUBJECTS (25) and LAB_D_SUBJECTS (60) size them as the design's labs. The run of this task: C1 25 starts, 3,245 free writes and 150 wave writes, 0 admitted at or after a start, 0 refused before one; C2 102 of 150 wave writes admitted after the start, the latest 311 ms after it; D 60 replies from 275 ms before to 307 ms after the start (29 on time, 31 late), on_time equal to filed_at < start in all 60, 10,285 writes, none refused before a start or after an on-time reply, none admitted after the start of a late one.
- **The counts' migration number (6, The numbers; 6.3).** The migration the design numbers 0021 `reporting_counts` is `0022_reporting_counts.sql`: 3c's last task added 0019, so every migration of 3e is one number higher.
- **The rows of reporting_record (6.3).** The view lists an account that holds a tally or a mark that counts within the frame; an account with neither has no row, and every reader takes it as 0 reports and 0 marked reports. Its frame is the one decision_actions_check's restriction counts by: a UTC day later than today, by `now()`, less `report_frame_days`. It is written as one row set of account ids with a correlated count on each side, so a read for one account uses each table's index.
- **The warning's time in the case answer (8.1, 10.3).** `CaseStanding.reporting` also carries `warnedAt`, the warning decision's instant, beside the day `warnedOn`: the console's preview of a restriction prints the warning it follows to the minute ({warnedAt}, W.2), and a day cannot.
- **The marks the drafts list (10.3, W.5).** `CaseStanding.reporting.marked` holds one row a mark that counts (`ref`, `position`, `sentOn`, `markedOn`) and also `sinceWarning`, true when its report was sent after korbat told the account of its warning, so the restriction's draft lists only those; the list holds at most 100 rows. {marked} names each report once, with all its marked items: "report N-... of 2026-09-03 (items 1 and 3)".
- **What markable means (10.3).** `CaseItem.markable` is the item's own fact, whether or not it is answered: it is no re-examination and came from a `user` intake with a sender still known that alleges no illegality and is not on the path for child sexual abuse material. The console offers the tick box only beside the items a final "No breach" decision made now would answer; the database's check refuses every other mark.
- **The rows of the person's list (9.4, 10.2).** `AuthorCaseRow` gains two optional members: `reportingDays`, the days of the decision's restriction of reporting, and `endedStanding`, the kinds whose basis a reversal of this decision broke (a restriction lifted by its own reversal before is left out). The app words the restriction's row, "your reports under the rules restricted for {days} days", and "a restriction of your reports that rested on it ended"; the suspension's "a suspension that rested on it ended" stays for the task that words Task 4's rows.
- **What a lift of a reporting measure names (10.2, W.2).** `StatementReversal.liftedKind` takes `warn_reporting` and `restrict_reporting` too, and on a lifted restriction `periodThen` says whether its days still ran (`running`, from its decision) or had run out (`ended`, with its decision's time plus its days as its end), which `reportingLifted` prints. A restriction in `endedStanding` carries the same two states.
- **DecisionView.reportingStanding is optional (10.2).** The member is nullable and optional, so a view built before Task 5 still parses; the API always sends it, null for a decision without a restriction. For a restriction that ran its days the state is `ended` and the sentence prints its end as decided (`until`).
- **The order of the reporting records (8.6).** "Each newest first" reads, for the reporting records, as the account with the newest mark that counts first.
- **The routes' list after Task 5 (7.1 item 2; 13).** `ROUTE_KINDS` holds all 22 kinds and `refuseUnopened` no longer reads the marks: the database's check of every mark and decision_holds at commit decide whether a mark can stand. `DecideOptions.allKinds` now changes nothing; the fixtures still pass it.
- **The words the design leaves out (8.4, 8.7).** The composer's mark fieldset is "Reports this decision answers" with a tick box "Manifestly unfounded: item {n}" for each item; the case page says "Marked manifestly unfounded" beside an item whose mark stands and "Mark withdrawn" beside one a correction withdrew; "Withdraw this decision's marks" shows, in place of a preview, "No statement is sent: withdrawing marks changes nothing for the author of what was reported, and the people who reported see it on their own lists." The reversals read "Lift the warning about reporting" and "Lift the restriction of reporting". A restriction's days default to `report_restrict_days` when it is chosen.
- **The preview of a lift of a warning (8.3, 8.7).** The console's preview of a review or a correction that lifts a warning does not print basisEndedReporting for the restriction that rests on it: that restriction's decision lies in another case, which the case answer does not carry. [review.warning] says beside the reversal that the restriction ends when the key confirms, and the statement the person receives carries the paragraph, read from the ledger.
- **How the tests meet the frame and clean the tallies (12).** `ageStandingForTest` also moves the reports the account sent (`intakes.received_at`) and its tallies, by the interval's whole days, so a report and its mark leave the frame together. Since every signed-in rule report leaves a tally, the cleanup lowers the tallies of the intakes it deletes (`untally`, in removeCasesIn and in the notices harness) and `removeFixtures` deletes the tallies of the profiles it removes; `scripts/clean-dev-fixtures.mjs` deletes the doomed accounts' tallies only.
- **The job's timer reads EXPLORE_V2 (section 5).** Section 5 says that `social/explore.ts` holds only the job's timer and does not change. It changes by one name: its timer reads `refreshMinutes` from `EXPLORE_V1`, which 10.1 stops exporting, so it reads `EXPLORE_V2.refreshMinutes`, the same 5 minutes. Nothing else of the job changes.
- **A removal that ends its own case's withhold passes through visible (6.4; invariant 18).** 6.4 says that a move between `withheld` and `removed` writes none of the three columns. That holds for a move made by one call of `moderation_apply`, such as a removal reversed while another case's withhold stands, which the tests prove with committed decisions. The commonest path is not such a move: a final removal decided in the case whose interim withhold it ends runs `decisions_apply` first, which closes the case and derives the level (the withhold has ended, so the post is visible for that instant), and then `decision_actions_apply`, which derives it again with the removal. So `moderation_apply` writes `restored_at` and `explore_from` for the end of the withhold and then `hidden_at` anew, all at that transaction's `now()`. No other transaction sees the visible level, the span that ended and the one that starts meet at that instant, so `explore_from` stays `created_at` plus the ledger's hidden spans to the microsecond, and the next real restore overwrites `restored_at`. No reader reads the three columns of a post that is not visible. The rule is kept as 6.4 states it: telling such a pass from a real restore would need the values it overwrote.
- **The status judges a suspension at the database's clock (7.13 item 7).** The status answers `suspended` when the author's suspension runs at `clock_timestamp()`, read in the statement of `exploreFacts`, as `explorePage` and `listedInExplore` judge it, and not at the flow's request time `now`, so the status says why the page and the chip leave the post out. Its other rules still judge at `now` and at the ranking's time, and a round judges at its own `at`.
- **A post can come back before the next round (7.13 item 6).** 7.13 says that when a suspension ends the author's posts return with the next round, if still in their window. The page and the chip judge the author at each read against the newest ranking, so a post that ranking holds comes back at the first read after the suspension ends. In practice that ranking was computed during the suspension and holds none of the author's posts, so they return with the next round; only a suspension that ends within the same refresh interval as the ranking that listed the post (one lifted at once, say) shows the post again at the next read. How Explore works and the status line promise no earlier than the next refresh, which stays true.
- **The retention migration's number (6, The numbers; 6.5).** The migration the design numbers 0023 `standing_retention` is `0024_standing_retention.sql`: 3c's review added `0019_redaction_scope.sql`, so each of 3e's five migrations is one higher (`0020_fearless_absorbing_man.sql`, `0021_standing_ledger.sql`, `0022_reporting_counts.sql`, `0023_explore_resume.sql`, `0024_standing_retention.sql`), and `--upgrade-from` names 19.
- **The frame's boundary (6.5, 7.14).** `forget_report_counts()` deletes exactly the complement of `reporting_record`'s frame: every mark, withdrawn ones included, whose `(reported_at AT TIME ZONE 'UTC')::date` is at or before today's UTC day minus `report_frame_days`, and every tally of such a day. A report sent on the last day outside the frame goes with its tally; one sent the day after stays on both sides, so what the warning and the restriction read is the same before and after the step.
- **The two steps' place and the round's report (7.14).** The steps run after `statements` and before `access_log`, each one call through `inStep` with an id-less audit row (`step`, null ids, `count`) when it changed something. `RetentionReport` gains `replies` (the replies whose text was erased) and `reportCounts` (the marks and tallies deleted), and the round's log line carries both.
- **RETENTION's three members (10.1, W.7).** `replyText` is the phrase the terms print, 'until korbat has answered it and its decision can no longer be appealed'; `reportCountDays` is `STANDING_RULES.reportFrameDays`; `standingRecord` is `{ strikeDays, ladderMemoryDays, warningDays }`, each read from `STANDING_RULES`, in place of W.7's string 'kept; counted for {strike_days}, {ladder_memory_days} and {report_frame_days} days'. `RETENTION` held numbers only, and the terms' table test names every number of it by its path: so the record's three periods are numbers the table holds to their sentences in 'Your data', and the reply's period is a phrase the test holds to its sentence and changes in the test with other constants, as it changes a rule's text. `STANDING_RULES` is declared before `RETENTION` in `moderation.ts`, which reads it.
- **The terms' sentence on a reply's text (W.7, 'Your data').** It reads 'The text of a reply to a suspension is kept {RETENTION.replyText}; a reply that is not answered yet is never erased.' in place of '... is erased once korbat has answered it and its decision can no longer be appealed; ...': the same rule, printed from the constant.
- **Numbers the terms work out from a constant (W.7, 12.2).** `reportWarnShare` is printed 'half' for 0.5 and as a percentage otherwise; 'at least two earlier strikes' prints `ladderStrikes` minus one; 'three such suspensions' and 'after three suspensions' print the number of the ladder's rungs (`ladderDays.length`); 'six months' prints `APPEAL_MONTHS`. The table test holds each, and its run with other constants keeps arrays as arrays and counts those worked-out values among the constants' digits.
- **What each new measure does, in How moderators decide (W.7).** W.7 has no words for them, so `MEASURE_MEANINGS` gives: strike 'a mark on the account that restricts nothing by itself and counts toward a suspension, as Strikes says'; suspend 'for a number of days the account can no longer post, engage, follow, edit its profile or report under the rules, as Suspension and termination says'; terminate 'the account is suspended until the decision is final, and then deleted, as Suspension and termination says'; retire_handle 'the handle can be used by nobody again, and the profile shows a placeholder in its place, as Handles says'; warn_reporting 'a warning about reports that are manifestly unfounded, which restricts nothing, as Reporting in bad faith says'; restrict_reporting 'for a number of days korbat takes no report under its rules from the account, as Reporting in bad faith says'. Every kind of `MEASURES` is listed by the ledger's two marks, each meaning pointing to its section in words rather than in brackets, since the sentence already sets the meaning in brackets; `LISTED_TARGETS` and the test's `listedIn` are gone.
- **Where the four sections stand, and two sentences W.7 leaves as they were (W.7).** Strikes, Suspension and termination, Reporting in bad faith and Handles follow Appeals and come before Terrorist content. Appeals' first sentence reads 'If a final decision restricts your content or your account', since a standing measure restricts an account and can be appealed; W.7's paragraph on a reply and the six months joins Appeals before its last paragraph. The reporter-forgetting sentence gains its clause at its end: '..., while it can still be re-examined, except through the mark of a report found manifestly unfounded (Reporting in bad faith).' The Handles section names rule R4 as 'rule R4', without the rule's title.
- **ACCOUNT_MEASURE_SCOPE in the terms (W.7, 3c's section 12).** The paragraph after the outcome phrases says W.7's two sentences and quotes both phrases from the constant between them: when korbat's answer to a report about an account is a measure on that account, it tells the reporter that it took a measure, and not which or for how long, the answer adding `ACCOUNT_MEASURE_SCOPE.measure`, or `ACCOUNT_MEASURE_SCOPE.retired` when it retired the handle. The paragraph begins with no quotation mark, so it is no answer paragraph, and the table test holds it as it holds every outcome phrase. The placeholder's prefix in Handles is `RETIRED_HANDLE_PREFIX`.
- **The export's three members as built (W.8).** `standing` lists the account's `suspend`, `terminate`, `warn_reporting` and `restrict_reporting` rows, newest first: for a suspension or a termination `earliestStart` is the row's `starts_at` and `startsAt` and `endsAt` are its `suspension_periods` row (null with no start, `endsAt` null for a termination); for a warning `startsAt` is its decision and `endsAt` its end of counting cut to a lift; for a restriction `startsAt` is its start and `endsAt` its days' end cut to a lift and to a broken basis; `earliestStart` is null for the two reporting measures, `replaced` false unless a withdrawal signed it. `replies` are newest first. `reporting.days` lists every tally korbat holds for the account, newest day first, also one the retention job has not yet deleted; `marks` lists every mark, withdrawn ones included, newest report first, with `counts` as `markCounts` reads it; `markedReports` is `reporting_record`'s `unfounded`. An account module built without the moderation module writes empty lists and `frameDays` from `STANDING_RULES`.
- **text-verify and the marks korbat deletes (6.5, 3c's 7.13).** `forget_report_counts` and `forget_reporter` delete marks that a decision's params signed, so `staff.js text-verify` takes a signed mark with no row for no difference when korbat's own rules may have deleted it: its report was sent on a UTC day at or before the frame's boundary, or its intake names no sender any more. An intake whose record `erase_intake` erased also names no sender, so a mark deleted by hand from such a report is not told apart; a mark deleted while its report is in the frame and its sender known is still reported.
- **The smoke test's two steps (12.2, Smoke).** `/api/me` for the onboarded smoke account must answer `standing` deeply equal to `{ activeStrikes: 0, measure: null, reporting: null, handleRetired: false }` (compared with `isDeepStrictEqual`), and `/api/staff/standing` joins `caseRoutes`: 404 from Caddy on the app's site, 401 `staff_session_required` on the console's.
- **The README's 3e text and its docs test (13, Task 7).** The README describes 3e under Moderation in a paragraph that opens 'Account sanctions (sub-project 3e;' and ten bullets, before the runbook, and the runbook's bullets (a) to (h) stand together where 3c's handle bullet stood, which becomes (d). The Standing screen holds four lists; 'Replies to answer' stands on the queue screen (8.5), so bullet (a) reads the replies there first. `apps/admin/test/readme.test.ts` lists the bullets' titles, holds every number of that text to `STANDING_RULES` and `REPLY_AIM_HOURS` in its sentence, refuses any other run of digits, and checks that the lines 3e made false are gone. The hard gate already read 3f and 3d and is unchanged.
- **How the retention of 3e is tested (12).** `standing-retention.test.ts` runs as the migrator inside transactions that are rolled back, deciding through the module's own decide and moving the clock with `ageStandingForTest` on the transaction (its own transaction becomes a savepoint); the round's two steps run through `retentionRound` over that transaction with a media interface that drops and requests nothing, after a first round that clears whatever another suite left due. A reply filed after its decision became final is a reply to a termination at once, the one measure that has not ended six months on.
- **The review's migration (6, The numbers).** What the review of the built code upheld in the database is one hand-written migration the design does not name, `0025_standing_review.sql`: it replaces the view `reporting_record` (0022) and the functions `decision_actions_check`, `decision_holds`, `suspension_replies_check` (0021) and `erase_reply_statements` (0024), each stated whole from its newest text, and back-fills `report_tallies`.
- **The closing judgment (7.2 rule 2, 11.2 'A write is judged when it gets the row'; section 19's Task 4 bullet on lab C1).** 7.2 rule 2 says a write reads only columns of a profile row that its own statement locks or updates, never a second statement's. Every write of 7.3 that a suspension refuses now ends its transaction with one more read of the acting account's row by the clock, after every lock, insert and counter update: `closingJudgment(tx, account)` in `lib/standing.ts` (deleted, suspended, reports restricted), through `judgeActing` in the social repository (createPost after `within`, like, repost and bookmark after their counters, follow after its counters, changeHandle last), in `insertProcessing` after its insert, and in the intake of a signed-in report under a rule as its step 6 (account_suspended, then reporting_restricted). It rolls the write back. A locking statement computes its judgment before it waits, so behind a holder that only locks the row (a decision about the account), behind one that changes the other party's row (lockPair and lockLive project every row before locking), and behind a decision that holds the post FOR UPDATE (a foreign key or a counter), a write judged before a start committed after it; with the closing judgment none does. The read waits for nothing: the write holds the acting row at least FOR KEY SHARE until its commit, and every writer of a start takes that row FOR UPDATE first. 11.2's bullet is replaced: a write is judged when it gets its rows and again by its last statement, so none commits after a start that came before its end. Task 4's bullet that left the gap to the owner is closed.
- **The profile's edit, the badge and the upload (7.2's table).** `updateProfile` and `ProfileStore.setVerified(id, at)` are each a transaction: the row FOR NO KEY UPDATE in a statement of its own, then the UPDATE with the fragment in its WHERE, which, coming after the only wait, is the write's closing judgment. `insertProcessing` is a transaction of its one statement and the closing judgment. A deleted or suspended owner answers account_suspended, as the one statement did.
- **The bound of a starved lock (5, the lock order; 7.6 step 9; 7.8 step 3; 3c 7.5).** Wherever lock_timeout is the bound, `boundLockWaits` (a confirmed decision, a reveal, a staff session's revocation) and `boundWaits` (the reply, the take-down), the same length is set as the transaction's `statement_timeout`, in one `set_config` statement: lock_timeout is armed again at each wait of one statement, so a FOR UPDATE on a profile that others' likes keep out of reach with FOR KEY SHARE (each new one joins the holders without queueing behind it) waited as long as the likes kept coming (16 likers: 10.6 s; 24: past 120 s). `isBoundReached(err)` (55P03 or 57014) is retried, answered `late` or `service_unavailable` as a lock timeout was; the take-down's round counts it in `failed`. The starvation itself (liveness) is recorded for the owner, not removed.
- **No profile is held across a mail (5, the lock order, item 4; 7.8 step 3; 7.14 item 3).** `sendStatement` takes the decision's row (FOR NO KEY UPDATE, while its mail is owed), sends, and only then the subject's profile FOR NO KEY UPDATE, right before it records the outcome; `giveUpAnnounced` takes the decision's row, then the profile, then records. A deciding transaction takes the decisions it answers FOR KEY SHARE only, through its foreign key, so the new order meets no decision in the opposite order; the take-down, the only transaction that takes a profile and then a decision's row FOR UPDATE, only takes a termination whose period started, which an owed notice never gives. The auth module's `takeDownTerminated` starts `termination_final` without awaiting it (notifyAccount logs a failure itself), so the take-down's transaction, which holds the profile FOR UPDATE, never waits on the relay; the mail still goes only after the auth module's take-down committed. One cost of the order: when the profile stays held past the mail's lock wait after the send, the record is rolled back and the mail stays pending to be sent again, as after a crash between the relay and the record (the later start favours the person).
- **A notice whose measure was withdrawn is never sent (7.6, 7.14, 7.15).** `sendStatement`, after the decision's row is held, reads in a statement of its own whether a later decision lifted the statement's suspension or termination or a reversal broke its basis (`standingWithdrawn`); it then records the mail `failed` without sending it (the measure, lifted, fixes no start). Before, the dispatch's retry sent `suspension_announced` with an earliest start and an invitation to reply after the person had read `suspension_lifted`.
- **A mark counts only once its item's outcome is released (6.3, 7.9 step 3, 7.12, 8.4, W.8).** Before the daily release the person can neither read a mark nor ask for a re-examination of it. `reporting_record`, the restriction's inline count in `decision_actions_check` and the API's `markCounts` (so `reportingFacts`' counts and marked list, `reportingRecords`, the marked reports since a warning) count and list a mark only when `intake_items.outcome_sent_at` is set; the export (`exportedReporting`) lists only such marks, and `markedReports` follows the view. W.8's `markedAt` is kept to the second (the review's suggestion of a day was considered: a released mark is the person's own released outcome).
- **The tallies' back-fill (6.3).** 0022's trigger counts only reports received after it, so 0025 sets each account's tally of each UTC day within `report_frame_days` to at least the number of its signed-in rule reports of that day (`greatest` of the tally and the count, where the review's finder added the two), so a database that ran 0022 before this migration counts none twice and a second run changes nothing. Its test runs the migration's own statement over reports whose tallies were removed, as an upgrade meets them.
- **A withdrawal is an admin's, in the database too (7.6 step 12, 8.7).** `decision_holds` accepts a stored `replaced = true` only when the deciding account's auth role is admin, and raises `admin_required` otherwise; only the console enforced it before.
- **A reply after the take-down (6.2 suspension_replies_check step 4; 7.6 step 9).** Step 4 also refuses `reply_not_open` when the account's `auth.users.deleted_at` is set: the take-down marks it while it holds the profile FOR UPDATE, and the step runs after the profile's lock, so a reply that waited behind the take-down is refused; the profile is marked deleted only by the purge after it.
- **A reply's text when its decision holds nothing in force (6.5, 6.7, 7.14).** `erase_reply_statements` also erases an answered reply's text once the measure's decision holds nothing in force (`measures_in_force`), the moment an appeal of it is no longer open (`appeals_check`), not six months after the person was told.
- **The badge's undo (7.2's table, the row of setVerified).** The table says verifyBadge clears the phone hash it wrote when setVerified answers false. It writes back the hash it read before (`user.phoneHash`): an account that already had a badge keeps the number behind it, and that number is not freed for another account's badge.
- **'Nothing changed now' (3c's outcome line; 7.12).** The expression the status page and the outcome mail read (`answerChangedNothing`) also requires that no `retire_handle`, `suspend` or `terminate` of the answering decision changed something (`no_effect` null), so a reporter is never told that nothing changed beside 'korbat took a measure on the account'.
- **A decision about a measure on the terms names the terms (W.2, 10.6).** `DecisionStatement.reviewed` gains `basis` (the reviewed decision's suspension or termination's basis, null for none, absent before 3e), carried by the API's statement and the console's preview. A review, a correction, a lift or an answer to a reply whose reviewed measure rests on `strikes`, `ladder_end` or `protective` prints `STATEMENT_WORDS.ground.restedOnTerms` ('That decision rested on korbat's terms, section Suspension and termination.'), never 'That decision rested on korbat rule ...'.
- **The banner after an answered reply (9.1).** [banner.announced]'s last sentence, 'If you reply before it starts, it waits for korbat's answer.', is printed only while /me's `replyOpen` is true, for a suspension and a termination alike: after korbat answered the reply a second one is refused.
- **Cases (9.4).** `AuthorCaseRow` gains `standingMeasure` ({kind, days, announced}) and `liftedStanding` ({kind, replaced}). The rows read 'a suspension of {days} days, announced' or 'at once', 'a termination', 'suspension lifted', 'suspension withdrawn for another measure', 'termination lifted' (a word the design does not give: the lift of a termination read 'suspension lifted'), and on a reversal 'a suspension that rested on it ended' or 'a termination that rested on it ended' (the design names the first only). A kept correction, korbat's answer to a reply, reads 'korbat kept its decision' as a kept review does, never 'No action'. 'Your standing' prints the banner's sentences for a measure not fixed, announced, held or running, a termination included. A like, repost or bookmark on a card and a follow refused account_suspended show `RefusedNotice` (refusalSentence from /me with 'Read the decision'); `reporting_restricted` and `reply_not_open` are worded by the app's own sentences, never the server's.
- **Where it stands now (9.2).** The API sends a period's end cut to a lift or to the break of its basis, so `nowSentence` reads an end at that very moment as 'it was running then' ([now.lifted], [now.basis.running]) and only an earlier end as 'it had run out'.
- **[reports.marked] (9.4).** Its second sentence, 'You can ask for a re-examination.', is printed only while a re-examination of the item is open; otherwise the line is its first sentence alone (`STANDING_TEXTS.reports.markedClosed`).
- **The console's reply row of a termination (8.5).** A late reply to a termination reads 'after the start: the termination runs' and names no end.
- **Lab C (12.3).** C1's wave behind a holder that only locks the row is asserted (no write commits at or after the start), and two further waves run on accounts of their own, behind a holder that changes the other party's rows and behind a decision that holds the post FOR UPDATE; each of the three waves sends every route of 7.3 that a suspension refuses (post, reply, quote, like, repost, bookmark, follow, the profile's edit, the handle, an upload, the badge, a report under a rule). The integration file `standing-closing.test.ts` holds the same in 24 scenes, and the bound in a test of sixteen likers.

## 20. Changed by 3f (2026-10-17)

`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-09-authorities-orders-3f-design.md` changes the deletion's hold, the functions it replaces, the runbook and two words of 3e's.

- **Section 2, row 27** ("no hold") and **section 15's line on 1b 7.6**: a deletion is still never refused or delayed; the address alone is held, for at most seven days, while a report is owed (3f's section 2, row 35; flagged for the owner and counsel, with "no hold" as the stated alternative and one setting, `API_REPORT_HOLD`).
- **Section 6:** the functions 3f replaces are written from the text "as 3e leaves it"; `measures_in_force`'s list of kinds and `append_confirmed_audit`'s list of actions grow; the enum rule binds 3f's six migrations (0026 to 0031), and `enum-literals.test.ts` covers them.
- **Section 13**, Task 7's runbook bullet (c): "whoever has the server still runs `staff.js account-facts` before the admin answers the report, since korbat never holds an account against its deletion" is replaced by the README's: `notification-facts` after the seal for good, with the address held meanwhile. Task 4's tests of `PurgeReport.terminated` gain the member `held` (3f's Task 5).
- **Section 16** is superseded by 3f's design. **W.3:** an order's redress is version 3's `order` texts. **W.6:** the two staff mails end as 3f's W.4 says.

## 21. Changed by 3d (2026-10-19)

`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-09-transparency-automation-3d-design.md` changes nothing of the sanctions and adds the two objection routes to what a suspension leaves.

- **Section 17** (a match may ask for a protective suspension): not built. The protective suspension rests on an admin's seal for good, as 3e built it; 3d adds no second basis (section 2, row 29).
- **Section 17** (the Transparency Database export gains the account measures): no export exists; the edition counts the account measures by kind.
- **W.4:** rulebook version 4 follows version 3; R6 is unchanged.
- **Section 6:** `strike_due` answers false for a decision without a decider (a test of Task 6 holds it).
- **7.3, the table of what a suspension leaves, and section 2, row 9:** the two objection routes of 3d (`POST /api/moderation/decisions/:id/objection`, `POST /api/moderation/uploads/:refusal/objection`) join the writes a suspension leaves, as the reply and an appeal are left, for a suspended account and for a terminated account whose termination is not yet final. Each gets its row in 3e's route table, so 3e's route walk passes, and Tasks 6 and 7 each send their objection from a suspended account. Without the rows the way to a person that a statement promises would be closed for the accounts whose case is gravest.
